# Framework master: vision, composition rules and proof chains

This is the working specification for an LLM with no conversation history. It contains the vision, 38 product families, the construction grammar, requirements, all 349 supplied proof definitions and their function chains, and the order for deriving a deliverable from them. Read the operating instructions first, then the complete records for every family, proof and dependency used in the task.

“Proof” is the source corpus's name for a record. These records include function-chain specifications, hardening requirements and conditional arguments. A record's existence supplies no automatic execution or coverage pass. Keep its assumptions, blocked steps and evidence limits when using it. This edition removes archive packaging, conversation transcripts and historical run dumps.

## 1. Full vision

Build a reusable factory that turns formal product-family definitions into configurators, then turns accepted organizational operating models into configured executable systems. The target includes discovering useful dependency-based work across industries, placing each use case within a family or composition, and closing the precise gaps that each new case exposes.

The commercial premise is that established organizations already hold substantial parts of their specification in procedures, policies, SharePoint collections, forms, responsibility matrices, approval rules, technical documents and system records. Connecting that material with current identities, authority, interfaces and observations can recover an operating model. Its completeness, accuracy and governing authority must be established for the actual organization. Existing procedures supply a baseline; new implementations add explicit changes, constraints and system bindings over that baseline.

Upstream LLM work explores the product space, reads sources, identifies concepts and dependencies, proposes formal interpretations, researches public library functions, and designs reusable compositions. Accepted interpretations become versioned models and derivation assets. Runtime handoffs, decisions, transformations and presentation use defined functions and data with zero runtime LLM reasoning. Attributable human answers and approvals can enter through typed, authorized boundaries and resume a defined workflow.

The reusable asset is the derivation: which inputs a family requires, which variations it allows, which components it assembles, which systems it binds, what it emits, and what establishes acceptance. Industries and roles select profiles. A policy engine can serve many industries. Admission can cover suppliers, employees, customers or equipment when the selected profile preserves the actual requirements. Physical commissioning, for example, can compose admission with measurement and control families.

Established software capabilities extend the destinations of existing chains. Typed data, decision, authorization and workflow models can feed API, storage, messaging, interface and deployment generators. The result can be a connected application. Every newly qualified operation or handoff creates additional possible compositions with existing outputs. Every connection still needs compatible meaning, identity, time, state, authority, effects and acceptance evidence.

| Construction level | Inputs | Output | Required correspondence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Family generator G | Family specification F, capabilities, variation model, derivation assets and target profile | Family-specific configurator C_F | Its questions, accepted choices and derivation rules implement F |
| Configurator C_F | Accepted operating model M, feature choices V, data and system bindings B | Configured executable system A and its acceptance obligations | A preserves the accepted model and selected features |
| Executable system A | Requests, events, observations, recorded human inputs and current authority | Decisions, artifacts, state transitions and reconciled effects | Actual behavior satisfies the admitted contracts in the selected environment |

The notation is G(F) = C_F and C_F(M, V, B) = A. F37 covers configurator generation. F38 assembles configured families into larger systems. The configurator knows what questions to ask, which combinations are valid, which components to assemble and which checks the product must pass.

The output space includes rule and decision services; identity and knowledge systems; workflows, transactions and operating applications; software, APIs and infrastructure; planning and allocation; quantitative and analytical products; experiments and diagnosis; learning and qualification; engineering and manufacturing definitions; spatial and streaming systems; bounded physical control; documents, media and interfaces. The family catalogue below defines the distinct contracts and their shared foundations.

Maintenance belongs in the original derivation. Stable identities connect source requirements to models, components, outputs and checks. A changed rule, source, component, authority, interface or observation identifies which outputs lose support, which alternatives remain admissible, and what must be regenerated, migrated, reviewed and retested. Historical versions remain available under their original scope. Reuse is allowed only while its dependencies and authority remain valid.

The coverage method explores operations, composition forms and the dimensions that alter semantics. Repeated patterns belong in parameterized families. A materially different output type, state behavior, construction procedure or acceptance argument can require a new family or rule. A finite grammar can express unboundedly many finite compositions. Demonstrating that a particular grammar covers every possible real-world requirement needs a separate completeness argument; the 38-family partition remains a working classification.

The practical generator path uses qualified templates, declarative models and existing generators. Literal third-Futamura-projection correspondence is the optional P349 target: it additionally requires a specified evaluator, a compatible self-applicable partial evaluator, genuine residual artifacts and behavior-preservation evidence. P305 supplies the practical configurator path without requiring that optional claim.

## 2. Order of operations for the LLM

1. Define the deliverable from the request and actual source requirements. Give each requirement a stable ID. Record outputs, accepted meanings, inputs, authority, state, time, quantities, coverage, external effects, failure outcomes and acceptance conditions. Preserve unresolved inputs.
2. Work backwards from every required output. Select the smallest matching family profile. Resolve its required inputs, then recursively select predecessor families or accepted external inputs. Continue until every branch ends at an actual source, a typed human input, an environment binding or a named primitive.
3. Read the full selected family cards, their specific R requirements, universal requirements R02/R03/R29/R30/R32, and relevant proof records. Existing anchors are reuse candidates. Additional proof mappings identify obligations to examine. A mapping alone supplies no implementation.
4. Resolve each selected proof's prerequisites before using its conclusion. Follow every arrow inside its chain in order. A line naming multiple inputs is a join that needs every named input. P numbers identify records; numerical P order is never a universal execution schedule.
5. Build the actual construction graph using O01–O18 and C01–C14. Identify every function by public callable and selected version. Bind exact input/output schemas and meanings. Use qualified existing adapters at incompatible boundaries. A purpose label or unavailable local helper remains a missing implementation.
6. Check every edge against the fourteen-field boundary contract and HG00–HG07. State what the producer guarantees, what the consumer assumes, what the adapter preserves and how errors propagate. No silent conversions of evidence basis, identity, units, authority or completion state are allowed.
7. Resolve the FamilySpec and feature constraints. Generate typed questions for missing choices. Classify configurations as valid, invalid, unresolved or unsupported. Family design, organization configuration, deployment and runtime have distinct binding stages.
8. Derive every applicable leaf, edge, feature, composition, environment and acceptance obligation. Include global properties such as non-overbooking, information-flow limits, conservation, effect uniqueness, deadlock avoidance and progress. A component pass does not discharge these global obligations.
9. Present the complete forward chain from accepted inputs to deliverables. Generate artifacts through the chosen qualified derivation assets. For a factory claim, produce the actual configurator and the actual configured product as separate outputs.
10. Execute the required generated guards and independent checks. HG04 requires actual producer → serialization → reload → adapter → actual consumer. Preserve unknown, timeout, rejection, unsupported, failed and not-run outcomes. Validate actual target effects through their receipt and reconciliation contracts.
11. Evaluate each original requirement against the resulting artifact and evidence. Report structural representation, primitive binding, execution and acceptance separately. A structural fit can remain blocked at implementation or acceptance.
12. Record exact dependency links and the regeneration, invalidation, migration and requalification order after change. Any upstream change that removes support invalidates the affected downstream claims.

For each task return: the required deliverables; selected family profiles; backward dependency derivation; forward function chains with proof/stage IDs; edge contracts and adapters; global obligations and checks; and the unresolved requirements with their exact blocking step. Supply actual outputs when execution is requested and available. Do not attach the research archive to an ordinary deliverable.

During a coverage test, freeze the catalogue. An added family, new semantic rule or newly authored adapter is an extension proposal and cannot be used to score the original catalogue as covering that case. Keep unsupported requirements intact. Failure to find a construction within a search budget means no witness was found within that budget.

## 3. Execution contract

Runtime uses defined functions and data, with zero runtime LLM reasoning. Every meaningful transformation, comparison, resolution, validation, decision and handoff uses a suitable existing public library primitive. Missing primitives or qualified adapters block the affected path. Qualified backend-generated code must remain within that backend's supported derivation contract.

Research the selected public callable in its own project's source or documentation at its pinned version before admitting it. An import succeeding proves installation only. When an independent implementation is needed, search across available ecosystems and examine shared dependencies. A wrapper around the implementation under test cannot be counted as a second engine.

The inherited assurance contract requires generated inputs, independent library expectations or justified invariants, published assertion callables and the standard pytest runner. Hand-authored case functions, expected fixtures, custom comparison helpers and bespoke runners do not qualify under that contract. Historical authored examples below describe intended properties and counterexamples; use generated guards to qualify a current implementation.

Deterministic execution still needs an explicit profile for clocks, ordering, random streams, numerical tolerance and external observations. Statistical, learned and simulated outputs retain their empirical or probabilistic meaning. Defined numerical inference can be a selected capability; runtime language-model reasoning is excluded.

The original P1–P190 chains sometimes name project-local helpers. Their names record historical construction intent and do not establish available public primitives. Apply the attached hardening requirements and research an admissible public replacement where needed. P191–P300 remain theoretical full-chain requirements. P301–P349 retain their specified profiles and open integration gates. No historical run is promoted by this edit.

The later bounded continuation demonstrated a stateless integer SCENARIO factory and native generated-input assertion bindings. Those bounded results do not qualify every family or complete the original whole-chain obligations. The original Ghostwriter-specific P344.S2 remains distinct from the proposed native-assertion alternative.

### HG00–HG07: every function boundary

HG00: Every producer emits a typed envelope with schema name/version, producer and environment versions, payload hash, stable source/record identities, scope, evidence basis, completeness, clock/cutoff contract and validation state. Every consumer validates the exact envelope and payload before work. Changes to schema, identity, units, clock or evidence need a named adapter with a tested contract. A missing adapter blocks the handoff.

HG01: Preserve SOURCE, MENTION, CANDIDATE, OBSERVATION, SCENARIO and RENDERED bases. No implicit promotion of a mention to an event, scenario to actual history, predicted effect to receipt, or discovered process to approved normative process. Validation, authorization and completion are separate gates.

HG02: Whole-input validation precedes effects. Reject unknown required fields, duplicate keys, ambiguous joins, lost rows, unhandled variants, NaN/infinity, stale versions, missing authority and missing source coverage. Retain rejected and unresolved identities. For a stream, define the complete finite event, batch, window revision or transaction being validated before its effect.

HG03: Join by source identity and explicit scope. Never silently join missing keys or merge on display labels. Reconcile inputs into accepted, replayed, rejected and unresolved outputs. Preserve sequence, multiplicity, nulls, typed zero and excluded contributors.

HG04: Execute the actual producer, serialize its output, reload it, call the actual adapter and execute the actual consumer. A substitute fixture checks a local contract only. The integration gate remains INTEGRATION_UNVERIFIED until the actual path passes at its named scope.

HG05: Propagate failed, not-run, unknown, timeout and unsupported states. Keep counterexamples, bounds, termination status and gaps. Sample agreement supports its stated sampled scope; a universal claim needs the corresponding argument.

HG06: Changed upstream meaning invalidates downstream decisions through exact dependencies and expanded-scope reevaluation. Preserve historical outputs. Qualify revised outputs with their new identities and required approvals.

HG07: Keep execution checkpoints cumulative, atomic and append-only; preserve prior failures and evidence. This compact specification contains the definitions and requirements. It does not embed the execution archive or claim that historical files are present.

### Conditional composition proof

Let K specify admitted inputs, allowed output or trace behavior, invariants and permitted failure outcomes. Write Implements(A, K, E) when artifact A in environment E satisfies K under K's assumptions.

Assume (1) each selected leaf implements its contract; (2) each selected composition rule preserves the required observable semantics; and (3) every actual binding and environment discharges that rule's side conditions. Then a finite construction tree implements its derived root contract.

Proof by structural induction: at a leaf, assumption (1) supplies the implementation relation. At an internal node, the induction hypotheses supply the child relations. Assumptions (2) and (3) establish the parent relation. Induction reaches the root.

For sequential composition with adapter T, establish a satisfiable admitted producer domain and the implication that every admitted producer output, transformed through T, satisfies the consumer precondition. T must preserve the specified meaning and other boundary fields. The consumer's contract then establishes the required downstream postcondition. Failure and external-effect behavior must also compose under their declared contracts.

Joins additionally require identity, population and multiplicity conditions. Forks require synchronization and interference conditions. Iteration requires invariants and the chosen termination or convergence argument. Streams and indefinite services require safety and progress over traces. Physical feedback requires timing and model assumptions. Finite syntax alone supplies none of these extra properties.

This proves the conditional construction rule. Applying it requires the actual leaf and rule premises. Semantic completeness of the catalogue and coverage of every domain are separate claims.

## 4. Construction grammar

The abstract grammar is:

```text
System := QualifiedLeaf(operation, parameters)
       | Seq(System, System, binding)
       | Join(System, System, key_and_scope)
       | ForkJoin(branches, barrier)
       | Choice(guards, branches, default)
       | Iterate(body, invariant, exit, budget)
       | MapReduce(partition, body, reduction)
       | Await(event, deadline, timeout_path)
       | StreamWindow(body, watermark, correction)
       | Atomic(reads, writes, isolation)
       | Saga(steps, receipts, compensations, retry_policy)
       | HumanInput(schema, authority, continuation)
       | Feedback(controller, plant, sample_clock, invariant)
       | Substitute(old, new, observation_map)
       | Revise(graph, change, checkpoint)
```

This is an abstract syntax and obligation scaffold. Parameters are typed references to accepted records. Seq requires a named port binding. Join does not infer keys from labels. A loop or feedback edge never bypasses an unresolved contract. The selected FamilySpec fixes which productions are available to its members and which backend implements each production.

The operator classes below form a compact coverage vocabulary. They are not a claim that exactly 18 mathematically irreducible operations exist. An operation class can have several qualified primitives and semantics profiles, including one for each materially different numeric, temporal or effect contract.

| ID | Operation class | Signature | Boundary obligation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| O01 | Acquire and decode | Source locator + acquisition contract -> bounded source bytes or acquisition gap | Retain source version, media type, byte completeness and acquisition state. |
| O02 | Parse and formalize | Source bytes + grammar + accepted mappings -> structured model + unresolved alternatives | Preserve scope, source spans and accepted semantics; ambiguity remains data. |
| O03 | Type and normalize | Values + schema + units + locale -> typed values or discrepancies | Conversions preserve declared quantities, meanings, identity and loss policy. |
| O04 | Resolve identities and references | Records + identity policy -> candidate or accepted links | Link evidence and adjudication are explicit; every input survives. |
| O05 | Select, join, group and aggregate | Collections + query + population contract -> derived collection or measure | Preserve keys, multiplicity, missing states, predicates and contributor lineage. |
| O06 | Traverse dependencies and relations | Typed graph + relation semantics -> closure, paths, components or gaps | Declare edge meaning, direction, cycle semantics and completeness. |
| O07 | Evaluate rules and constraints | Accepted logic + scoped facts -> decision, obligations, conflicts or unknown | Retain modality, applicability, logic, defaults and all result states. |
| O08 | Advance and monitor state | State + event + transition/clock contract -> next state + observations | Declare ordering, guards, finality, liveness and allowed effects. |
| O09 | Calculate quantities and geometry | Typed numeric/spatial operands + model -> computed values or geometric objects | Conserve exact quantities where required; declare rounding, reference systems and numeric errors. |
| O10 | Estimate, infer and fit | Data + statistical/learned model + assumptions -> estimate, model or abstention | Separate empirical support, uncertainty and applicability from deterministic execution. |
| O11 | Search, solve and optimize | Domain + constraints + optional objective -> candidate, solution, bound or unknown | Retain domain bounds, feasibility, optimality, timeout and infeasibility scope. |
| O12 | Simulate dynamics | Model + initial conditions + inputs + numerical policy -> scenario trace | Control time, random streams, discretization, coupling and empirical validity claims. |
| O13 | Select observations and experiments | Question/hypotheses + possible observations + objective -> evidence acquisition plan | Establish what can be distinguished, at which cost and under which design assumptions. |
| O14 | Construct and specialize | Accepted model + configuration + qualified derivation assets -> artifact or generator | Prove representation and behavior correspondence through construction stages. |
| O15 | Render and encode communication | Accepted content/model + presentation contract -> human-facing artifact or delivery intent | Preserve selected content and qualifiers through layout, interaction and media encoding. |
| O16 | Compare and qualify | Claim + named evidence + acceptance contract -> qualified, failed or open claim | Independent checks execute at the declared scope; no absent evidence is promoted. |
| O17 | Persist, dispatch and reconcile | Validated authorized intent + effect contract -> state change + receipt or uncertain effect | Bind intent, authority, freshness, transaction, retry and external receipt semantics. |
| O18 | Diff, invalidate, migrate and repair | Versioned objects + change + dependency graph -> affected set and supported revision | Preserve historical versions; invalidate all lost support and qualify replacements. |

The composition forms impose their own obligations:

| ID | Composition | Form | Acceptance obligation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C01 | Sequential composition | Seq(A, B) | Every connected output refines its consumer input contract; failures propagate. |
| C02 | Keyed join | Join(A, B, key, scope) | Join keys, cardinality, temporal scope and missingness are compatible; unmatched inputs reconcile. |
| C03 | Fork and synchronization | ForkJoin(branches, barrier) | Shared state, branch completion, cancellation and resource interference have explicit semantics. |
| C04 | Guarded choice | Choice(guards, branches, default) | Choice has declared overlap, priority and unknown behavior; default never supplies missing authority. |
| C05 | Iteration and fixed point | Iterate(body, invariant, exit, budget) | Bounded loops require a bound; fixed points require convergence or an explicit unresolved outcome; infinite services use a service contract. |
| C06 | Partition, map and reduce | MapReduce(partition, body, reduction) | Partition covers the declared population; recombination preserves multiplicity, identity and reduction semantics. |
| C07 | Event wait and timeout | Await(event, deadline, timeout_path) | Event identity, clock uncertainty, lateness, cancellation and resume state are explicit. |
| C08 | Streaming window and correction | StreamWindow(body, watermark, correction) | Each finite window/transaction is validated before its effects; late input and retractions revise dependent outputs. |
| C09 | Atomic transaction | Atomic(reads, writes, isolation) | Whole transaction input validates; concurrent execution meets the chosen isolation contract and retry rules. |
| C10 | Retry and compensation | Saga(steps, receipts, compensations, retry_policy) | Retries preserve intent identity; compensation follows declared dependencies and never claims impossible reversal. |
| C11 | Human evidence boundary | HumanInput(schema, authority, continuation) | A person supplies a typed, attributable input; resumption is deterministic and may reject stale or unauthorized input. |
| C12 | Feedback and hybrid coupling | Feedback(controller, plant, sample_clock, invariant) | Discrete and continuous dynamics, latency, stability/safety claims and model validity are qualified together. |
| C13 | Refinement and substitution | Substitute(old, new, observation_map) | The replacement preserves the required observable behavior and operational assumptions over a stated domain. |
| C14 | Versioned incremental rebuild and replay | Revise(graph, change, checkpoint) | Dependency invalidation, migration, replay and guarded reuse agree with the complete rebuild contract. |

### State-space dimensions

The following axes define the configuration search space. Each selected value becomes an explicit field or referenced profile. The cross-product can be very large and may include incompatible combinations. Validity constraints remove only combinations known to be invalid. Unknown or unsupported combinations remain visible with their own states.

| ID | Axis | Values |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A01 | Semantic object | Text, record, entity graph, rule, event, task, quantity, model, geometry, signal, artifact, generator |
| A02 | Transformation purpose | Formalize, decide, coordinate, quantify, construct, investigate, qualify, maintain |
| A03 | Dependency shape | Chain, tree, DAG, cyclic graph, hierarchy, hyperedge, state machine, continuous coupling |
| A04 | Execution mode | Batch, interactive, event-driven, streaming, scheduled, real-time, hybrid |
| A05 | Time | Timeless, valid time, known-at time, interval, calendar, uncertain clock, event time |
| A06 | Logic and uncertainty | Classical, four-state evidence, defaults, probability, interval uncertainty, unknown |
| A07 | State and consistency | Stateless, local transactional, distributed, replicated, offline, historical replay |
| A08 | Authority and effects | Read, propose, approve, commit, compensate, disclose, actuate |
| A09 | Population | Closed finite domain, partial collection, paginated snapshot, open stream, unknown coverage |
| A10 | Numeric and spatial semantics | Exact, floating tolerance, units, currency, uncertainty, coordinates, topology |
| A11 | Human participation | Accepted upstream interpretation, recorded runtime input, adjudication, sign-off |
| A12 | Construction target | Records, rules, workflows, office/media, UI, software, infrastructure, physical design, generator |
| A13 | Variability | Mandatory, optional, alternative, cardinality, numeric parameter, requires, excludes |
| A14 | Change | New source, correction, withdrawal, expiry, policy revision, migration, backend replacement |
| A15 | Assurance claim | Structural validity, behavioral refinement, bounded correctness, statistical performance, physical validation |
| A16 | Failure | Invalid, missing, conflicting, timeout, stale, partial effect, unavailable, out-of-envelope |
| A17 | Deployment | Local, shared, isolated tenant, federated, cloud, edge, embedded, offline |
| A18 | Scale and resources | Volume, latency, throughput, memory, concurrency, capacity, operating cost |

Three questions distinguish the product implications of a new capability: what new output contracts can it satisfy; which existing inputs can it now consume; and which new compositions become possible with existing families? This creates a systematic expansion procedure when a new library, format or target is qualified.

### Boundary and composition rules

For each connection A -> B, require compatibility across the following fields:

| Field | Required contract |
| --- | --- |
| schema | Schema URI/version, dialect, representation, cardinality and null contract |
| meaning | Concept, role, modality, qualifiers, units, allowed loss and accepted mapping |
| identity | Stable source/record IDs, join keys, tenant, object and purpose scope |
| time | Effective/known-at clocks, cutoff, order, timezone, uncertainty and freshness |
| basis | SOURCE, MENTION, CANDIDATE, OBSERVATION, SCENARIO or RENDERED |
| coverage | Complete/partial/unknown population, exclusions, missing dependencies and acquisition limits |
| authority | Principal, resource, action, purpose, delegation, applicable approval and expiry |
| state | Pre-state, expected version, permitted transition and concurrency contract |
| effects | Read/write/dispatch/actuate classification, intent identity, receipt, retry and compensation |
| numeric | Exactness, precision, tolerances, coordinate systems and uncertainty propagation |
| resources | Execution limits, deadline, memory, throughput, cancellation and fairness assumptions |
| evidence | Claim scope, environment, producer/adapter/consumer versions, hashes and validation state |
| change | Dependency IDs, invalidation triggers, supersession, replay and migration policy |
| exceptions | Rejected/unresolved IDs and distinct failed/not-run/unknown/timeout/unsupported outcomes |

The acceptance relation is stronger than matching schema names. A's guarantees, together with the explicitly accepted environment assumptions, must establish B's preconditions. If an adapter changes representation, it needs its own preservation contract and HG04 execution. If that implication cannot be established, the edge remains blocked or unresolved.

Local contracts also need a global check. Two valid reservations can overbook a shared resource. Two individually authorized components can reveal a prohibited joined view. Two reliable retries can duplicate one external intent. A composition can deadlock even when each component can terminate in isolation. R14 supplies the global obligations for shared state, authority, conservation, progress and effects.

The required evidence for a composed claim is the union of applicable leaf, edge, composition, environment and acceptance obligations. Qualification is a conjunction over those obligations, with each failure or open state preserved. It is not a numeric average of component scores. Compatibility evidence and successful local checks are separate fields.

The following variation rules are part of the scaffold:

| Rule | Selected feature | Required capabilities | Proof requirements |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| V01 | external_write = true | current authorization; freshness fence; effect identity; receipt reconciliation; explicit retry policy | R10, R28 |
| V02 | long_running = true | durable state; versioned replay; timeout and cancellation; compensation classification | R10, R12 |
| V03 | partial_population = true | coverage status; provisional results; named missing dependencies | R02, R19 |
| V04 | probabilistic_result = true | model provenance; evaluation population; uncertainty; abstention policy | R23, R31 |
| V05 | generated_software = true | accepted behavior model; architecture profile; component catalogue; behavior correspondence | R07, R08 |
| V06 | generated_interface = true | interaction model; role-filtered views; accessibility profile; service agreement | R09, R11 |
| V07 | multi_tenant = true | tenant-bound identity; isolation; release policy; cross-tenant negative cases | R14, R27 |
| V08 | offline_editing = true | local versioning; sync and conflict rules; stale-authority rejection | R12, R25 |
| V09 | physical_design = true | units; geometry; tolerances; materials; engineering validity | R15, R16, R17 |
| V10 | physical_actuation = true | plant and hardware contract; timing; interlocks; bounded operating envelope | R20, R28 |
| V11 | streaming = true | event identity; event-time clock; window finality; late correction and retraction | R19 |
| V12 | cross_family = true | bound ports; shared-state owner; global invariants; joint failure handling | R14, R32 |
| V13 | privacy_release = true | protected unit; allowed computation; contribution and release limits; audit policy | R27 |
| V14 | runtime_llm_reasoning = true | EXCLUDED_BY_EXECUTION_CONTRACT | Contract exclusion |
| V15 | required_primitive_missing = true | BLOCKED_NO_QUALIFIED_PRIMITIVE | R02 |
| V16 | review_or_approval = true | artifact and version binding; eligible reviewer population; scope; expiry | R25 |
| V17 | retention_or_deletion = true | replica inventory; holds; provenance retention contract; purge semantics | R27 |
| V18 | adaptive_experiment_or_learning = true | fixed selection procedure; recorded observations; stopping rule; selection validity | R21, R22 |

In addition, any missing approved source meaning blocks the affected executable rule; any missing provider capability blocks the affected effect; and any unresolved architecture target blocks generation for that target. Other supported configurations remain available under their own complete contracts.

### FamilySpec and configurator contract

A reusable FamilySpec must contain the following records:

| Record | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Identity and scope | Stable family ID, version, purpose, supported profiles, vocabulary and source references |
| Port declarations | Input/output semantic types, schemas, multiplicities, profile-specific requiredness and complete boundary contracts |
| Feature model | Mandatory, optional and alternative features; cardinalities; numeric domains; requires/excludes; binding stages |
| Shared semantics | Entities, rules, clocks, quantities, authority, state, effects, unknowns and exception behavior |
| Derivation assets | Qualified operation references, composition template, backend targets, transformation contracts and artifact identities |
| Question model | Missing-input partition, allowed answers, question dependencies, explanation and unresolved-answer behavior |
| Proof model | Per-feature, per-operation, per-edge and global obligations; evidence scope; independence and promotion rules |
| Operating envelope | Runtime resources, deployment, data coverage, latency, failure and provider constraints |
| Maintenance model | Dependency edges, versioning, extension points, migration, invalidation, regeneration and retirement |

The 38 family cards list input and output roles used across their profiles. A concrete FamilySpec resolves requiredness and cardinality for every selected profile before it generates a configurator. An absent mandatory value is represented as an unresolved input, with a generated question or a blocked derivation. A consumer never infers that an omitted field was optional.

The generated configurator has a defined sequence:

1) Validate the FamilySpec, capability catalogue and target profile.
2) Bind available accepted operating-model inputs with source and authority references.
3) Evaluate the partial configuration and expose required unanswered questions.
4) Classify combinations as valid, invalid, unresolved or unsupported.
5) Produce the typed construction graph with explicit adapter and system bindings.
6) Derive the complete proof-obligation manifest for the chosen graph.
7) Generate the selected artifacts through qualified derivation assets.
8) Execute the required qualification path and publish the resulting scope and gaps.
9) Record source-to-family-to-configurator-to-product dependencies for future changes.

Its outputs include a configuration manifest, question/gap ledger, construction graph, generated artifacts, source/requirement trace map, proof-obligation manifest, environment/provider bindings, acceptance result and change plan. Failed generation must still return accountable input identities and the blocked stage.

Different decisions belong at different binding stages. Family creation fixes the product contract and permitted variation. Organization configuration fixes accepted operating meaning, policies and authority models. Deployment binds systems, credentials and resource envelopes. Runtime supplies requests, observations and current authorization. A runtime request cannot silently redefine a family feature or an approved semantic mapping.

The feature model is also the question generator's basis. Questions should resolve a declared missing dimension or distinguish admissible configurations. A valid answer narrows the space and recalculates its dependencies. An unanswered question leaves it open. No free-form runtime reasoning is needed to invent either the question or its effect on configuration.

The implementation record for every selected capability must contain: project and version; exact public callable; primary-source URL and section; input/output schema; preconditions; result and error semantics; adapter obligations; independent oracle and dependency check; generated-input strategy; actual HG04 evidence; measured envelope; licensing and target compatibility; status and remaining gaps. Until populated and qualified, these fields stay explicitly unselected.

## 5. Guard generation and independent acceptance

### GP01

id: GP01
state: SPECIFIED_NOT_EXECUTED
input_generation: Hypothesis6.131.9 strategies generate admissible and adverse inputs. hypothesis-jsonschema0.23.1 is restricted to its supported Draft4/6/7 profile, with common-profile checks for downstream validators.
test_generation: Ghostwriter equivalent/roundtrip style=unittest where native call signatures and domain semantics match. Its output uses public TestCase.assertEqual. No authored case functions or fixtures are introduced.
comparison: Use published NumPy/assertion callables with independent library results or justified output invariants. A fixture literal, same-engine rerun or no-crash result is not silently treated as an independent semantic oracle.
runner: pytest8.3.5 standard runner. Report generated/collected/executed/passed/failed/skipped/error counts, seeds/settings, health-check/rejection behavior and minimized counterexamples.
nonvacuity: Reject st.nothing/TODO strategies, zero tests, all-rejected/all-skipped domains, empty targets and unsatisfiable antecedents as successful evidence.
scope: Per-proof bounded profile, explicit finite/nonfinite/null/time/status handling, exact/tolerant comparison policy and actual producer/reload/adapter/consumer coverage.
unqualified: Cross-language/stateful contract-to-test assembly and incompatible callable adapters require existing qualified generators/bindings; no handwritten test body or runner is supplied.
Important boundary: ordinary JSON Schema validation cannot detect duplicate source keys already discarded by a parser, and type validation alone cannot establish authority, coverage or meaning. The chosen raw parser/schema/model options and any additional public checker must cover those HG02 obligations before runtime admission.

### Oracle independence by computation

I01 | JSON structure | jsonschema / Ajv / Pydantic
  Distinct implementations; accepted common schema profile and matching options required.
I02 | SMT decision | Z3 / cvc5
  Distinct engines; shared semantic encoding and satisfiable antecedent remain obligations.
I03 | Graph/obligation derivation | NetworkX / clingo / SMT
  Distinct algorithms; missing accepted edges/rules remain common-mode failures.
I04 | SHACL Core | pySHACL / Jena
  Distinct Python/Java implementations for a declared Core intersection; pySHACL/RDFLib is a shared dependency pair.
I05 | Relational transforms | pandas / Polars
  Distinct implementations on aligned keys, nulls, ordering, types and cardinality.
I06 | Generated API | OpenAPI Generator / Schemathesis
  Different roles, not independent business engines; real domain operation/state is required.
I07 | Generated UI | JSON Forms / Ajv / Playwright / axe
  JSON Forms uses Ajv; browser readback and independent domain model cover different obligations; axe has incomplete/manual scope.
I08 | Durable workflow | Temporal replay / TLC
  Replay consistency plus separate accepted model; external receipts still required.
I09 | Storage migration | SQLAlchemy / Alembic / PostgreSQL
  Shared stack, not three independent oracles; actual database state and independent reconciliation are needed.
I10 | Program verification | Dafny / Z3
  Dafny uses Boogie/Z3; do not count as independent Z3 confirmation.
I11 | Solid geometry | CadQuery / FreeCAD
  Shared Open CASCADE lineage; exact analytic references support bounded shapes only.
I12 | Spatial topology/reference | Shapely / PROJ wrappers
  GEOS/PROJ wrapper pairs are not independent engines; use separately derived restricted analytic references or qualify another engine.
I13 | Physical simulation | FMPy / alternate FMU runner
  The same FMU may contain the same model/solver; independent model and empirical evidence remain necessary.
I14 | Power analysis | statsmodels / SciPy / R pwr
  SciPy is a statsmodels dependency. R pwr with pinned R stats provides a separate implementation for aligned two-sample t-test profiles.
I15 | ML conversion | scikit-learn / ONNX Runtime
  Distinct selected operator path with native/converter/provider audit; empirical generalization is a separate claim.
I16 | Media | FFmpeg / SoundFile-libsndfile
  Independent admitted PCM path. FFprobe/PyAV share FFmpeg; general compressed codecs are not qualified.
I17 | CRDT and matching | Automerge / matching internal checks
  Convergence/stability checks are invariants; business governance/blocking-pair semantics require independent accepted models.
I18 | Privacy | OpenDP relation / ledger
  Formal mechanism relation and independently reviewed adjacency/accounting; noisy sample agreement does not prove privacy.
I19 | Hybrid proof | KeYmaera X / arithmetic backend
  Record Z3/Mathematica backend; separate model/physical/timing evidence.
I20 | Provider/physical evidence | HTTPX / asyncua / receipts
  Clients are transport tools; actual authoritative state and independent physical observations establish effects.
I21 | Manufacturing interpretation | FreeCAD postprocessor / CAMotics
  CAMotics is a located independent 3-axis simulation candidate. Its automated simulation entry, dialect, discretization and FreeCAD-output binding remain unqualified.

## 6. Product families

All 38 cards are family contracts with proposed research mappings. Universal requirements R02, R03, R29, R30 and R32 apply to generated family systems alongside each card’s specific requirements. Role names are semantic contract slots; the selected FamilySpec must supply actual schemas, requiredness and cardinalities. Existing anchors identify reusable proof records. Extension mappings identify further proof obligations to select and qualify. Neither list is a runtime schedule.

### F01. Source formalization and operating-model assembly

Product contract: Compile source-linked actors, objects, modalities, conditions, exceptions, dependencies and meanings into accepted models while retaining unresolved alternatives.

Input roles: SourceBundle; SemanticAcceptance.

Output roles: OperatingModel; RuleModel; EvidencePack.

Variation: Source media; Controlled language; Ontology and aliases; Document authority and scope; Multi-view model profile.

Configurator questions: Which source version governs? Which mappings are accepted? What source coverage is required? Which unresolved distinctions block compilation?

Acceptance: Every executable statement has a stable source anchor and accepted meaning; omissions and ambiguity survive as typed gaps.

Composition: Feeds decisions, workflows, applications, training and contracts from the same accepted model. External observations enter separately from normative text.

Operation classes: O01, O02, O03, O06, O16.

Existing anchors: P1, P7, P191, P195, P196, P204, P281, P288.

Specific proof requirements: R06, R02.

Mapped extensions: P302, P303, P306, P344, P345, P346, P348.

### F02. Entity identity and relationship registries

Product contract: Maintain scoped identities, relationships, temporal membership and reversible identity decisions.

Input roles: Dataset; IdentityAdjudication.

Output roles: EntityRegistry; EvidencePack.

Variation: Exact or probabilistic candidate linkage; Relationship kind; Cycles; Temporal membership; Merge and withdrawal policy.

Configurator questions: What establishes identity? Which relationships are directed or transitive? What source population is complete? Who may adjudicate a candidate?

Acceptance: Every record remains accountable; identity candidates, confirmed links and unknown relationships stay distinct after merge, split and historical replay.

Composition: Supplies stable subjects for admission, agreements, supply chains, access and historical analysis.

Operation classes: O03, O04, O06, O17, O18.

Existing anchors: P37, P38, P201, P202, P203, P209, P251, P253, P254, P284, P291.

Specific proof requirements: R02, R12, R30.

Mapped extensions: P302, P303, P310, P316, P317, P344, P345, P346, P348.

Acceptance: Accepted, rejected, unchanged and unresolved records reconcile to the source population; required relationships and quantities survive the mapping.

Composition: Pairs with connector construction for operated integrations and with application generation for replacement systems.

Operation classes: O01, O03, O04, O05, O16, O17, O18.

Operation classes: O06, O14, O16, O17, O18.

Mapped extensions: P302, P303, P314, P318, P330, P343, P344, P345, P346, P348.

### F31. Verification, qualification and assurance

Acceptance: Every promoted claim is supported at its actual scope; evidence absence, timeout, failure and local-only checks propagate without promotion.

Composition: Attaches claim-specific evidence to families, configurations, compositions and generated systems. Formal, empirical and authorized acceptance are separate.

Operation classes: O03, O06, O11, O16.

## 7. Required claims and proof dependencies

R IDs name requirements; P IDs name proof records. The mappings below identify candidate ways to discharge each requirement. Each requirement still needs its actual admitted profile and evidence.

### R01. Family metamodel and semantic well-formedness

Required claim: A FamilySpec has typed features, valid bindings, unique identities and complete declared derivation and acceptance roles.

Required evidence: Validate actual serialized family specifications, including generated invalid and conflicting variants; compare feature domains with an independent representation.

Existing anchors: P95, P194, P260, P273.

Prerequisites: R02.

Mapped proofs: P301.

### R02. Typed ports and composition contracts

Required claim: For each bound edge, producer guarantees establish consumer assumptions across schema, semantics, identity, time, authority, evidence and effects.

Required evidence: Actual producer -> serialize -> reload -> researched adapter -> actual consumer, with generated schema-compatible semantic failures and explicit missing-adapter outcomes.

Existing anchors: P95, P194, P207, P261, P278.

Prerequisites: None beyond the inherited execution contract.

Mapped proofs: P302.

### R03. Derivation of proof obligations

Required claim: Every selected feature, operation and composition rule produces all mandatory proof obligations with stable traceability.

Required evidence: Compare emitted obligations against an independently represented obligation relation over the bounded feature grammar, including interaction-triggered obligations.

Existing anchors: P148, P189, P250.

Prerequisites: R01, R02.

Mapped proofs: P303.

### R04. Valid configurations and diagnostic questions

Required claim: Configuration classification and questions preserve the complete declared finite feature domain, its conflicts and its unresolved choices.

Required evidence: Independent valid-configuration parity; generated inconsistent, partial and boundary configurations; bounded exhaustive domains and separately measured interaction coverage.

Existing anchors: P198, P260, P271, P273, P274, P275.

Prerequisites: R01.

Mapped proofs: P304.

### R05. Generated configurator correspondence

Required claim: A generated configurator accepts exactly the declared supported configurations and emits the correct specialized derivation plan and question flow.

Required evidence: Execute the generator and generated configurator on named FamilySpec bytes; compare with an independent declarative semantics over the same bounded domain.

Existing anchors: P238, P260, P273, P275.

Prerequisites: R01, R03, R04.

Mapped proofs: P305, P349.

### R06. Source-to-operating-model correspondence

Required claim: Accepted source mappings survive into connected entity, rule, workflow, quantitative, interface and requirement views without lost modality or scope.

Required evidence: Source anchors and approved annotation evidence; generated grammar inputs; disagreement ledger and multi-view consistency checks; no independent-engine agreement substituted for semantic acceptance.

Existing anchors: P1, P191, P195, P196, P204, P281, P288.

Prerequisites: R02.

Mapped proofs: P306.

### R07. Behavior model to generated program

Required claim: Generated application observations refine the accepted behavior model, including authorization, state, time, quantities and error outcomes.

Required evidence: Execute emitted code against the source model through independent semantic oracles and generated traces; retain finite abstraction bounds and counterexamples.

Existing anchors: P148, P191, P238, P267, P286, P289.

Prerequisites: R06, R02.

Mapped proofs: P307, P308, P312.

### R08. Architecture and maintainable regeneration

Required claim: Generated modules obey declared dependency, interface and extension rules; regeneration preserves supported extensions and changes only justified behavior.

Required evidence: Compiler/static-analysis and architecture checks; generated feature deltas; rebuild comparison; extension collision and incompatible upgrade cases.

Existing anchors: P148, P212, P238, P267, P290.

Prerequisites: R07.

Mapped proofs: P309, P346.

### R09. Domain model to API, client and service

Required claim: Generated server, schema and client agree on business operations, versions, authorization and error semantics across the supported protocol profile.

Required evidence: Execute generated endpoints and clients with stateful generated sequences and independent domain-state checks, including unknown and unauthorized outcomes.

Existing anchors: P207, P261, P266.

Prerequisites: R07, R02.

Mapped proofs: P307, P311, P312.

### R10. Event and distributed effect semantics

Required claim: Connected event and action chains preserve identity, ordering, retries, receipts and compensation across crashes and provider uncertainty.

Required evidence: Generated duplicate, reordering, partition and crash schedules using qualified fault-injection and state-model primitives; reconcile actual remote receipts.

Existing anchors: P210, P241, P242, P243, P244, P266, P295.

Prerequisites: R02, R12.

Mapped proofs: P313, P314, P328.

### R11. Generated UI, accessibility and model agreement

Required claim: Visible fields, actions, interaction states and readbacks agree with the accepted domain model and accessibility profile.

Required evidence: Real browser interactions against generated artifacts and actual services; accessibility and visual checks; generated role, locale, viewport, offline and conflict variants.

Existing anchors: P65, P72, P75, P98, P102, P264.

Prerequisites: R07, R09.

Mapped proofs: P315.

### R12. Persistence, migrations and concurrency

Required claim: Generated storage and migrations preserve identity, relationships, values and selected isolation invariants under concurrency and rollback.

Required evidence: Actual database engines, serialized migration outputs, independent record reconciliation and generated competing transactions; named dialects and isolation levels.

Existing anchors: P30, P137, P245, P261, P284, P293.

Prerequisites: R02.

Mapped proofs: P310, P316, P317.

### R13. Build, deployment and operational realization

Required claim: Builds and deployments realize the selected configuration with complete declared dependency, resource, isolation and operational contracts.

Required evidence: Actual generated build and deployment artifacts; provenance; measured workloads and faults; environment and dependency hashes; rollout and recovery evidence.

Existing anchors: P249, P263, P268, P269.

### R14. Global invariants across family compositions

Existing anchors: P148, P189, P238, P250, P260, P266, P286.

Prerequisites: R05, R07, R09, R13, R14, R29, R30.

Literal projection profile: if the formal third Futamura projection is selected as a claim, R05 must additionally identify the interpreter/evaluator, partial evaluator, self-application domain and behavior-preservation relationship. Producing a useful family generator is already a concrete architectural milestone; the literal projection claim needs that extra evidence.

Mapped proofs: P348.

## 8. Composed family chains

These twelve theoretical recipes show the family bindings and their additional obligations. They supply construction patterns; current execution acceptance still follows the selected proof profiles.

### X01. SOP to a maintained equipment-booking application

Families: F01, F02, F05, F07, F11, F24, F25, F26, F30, F31, F32.

Inputs: Accepted booking, qualification and calibration procedures; equipment and staff records; calendar and system contracts.

Bindings:
1) F01 supplies source-bound eligibility and booking behavior to F05 and F24.
2) F02 supplies equipment and operator identities; F11 supplies resource and time feasibility.
3) F24 emits the application and storage contract; F25 exposes booking operations; F30 generates the matching user interface.
4) F26 binds deployment and monitoring; F31 qualifies the resulting instance; F32 maintains the dependency links.

Generated product: Booking application, generated service and UI, storage/migrations, access configuration, build/deployment plan, requirement traceability and evidence manifest.

Emergent obligations: Concurrent booking requests must preserve non-overlap across UI, API and database. Calibration and qualification must still hold at the contracted reservation/use time. A valid preview cannot commit against a changed equipment version.

Change propagation: Requiring two qualified operators changes the behavior model, reservation constraints, UI fields, service contract, database relations and corresponding proof obligations.

Proof requirements: R06, R07, R08, R09, R11, R12, R13, R14, R30, R32.

### X02. Supplier admission through procurement activation

Families: F01, F02, F05, F06, F07, F08, F09, F13, F15, F25, F31, F32.

Inputs: Supplier procedures, eligibility policy, party records, agreements, approved purchasing-system contract and current authority.

Bindings:
1) F02 resolves the supplier and its scoped relationships.
2) F05 returns eligibility and outstanding obligations; F08 adds agreement duties.
3) F09 coordinates evidence and approval work; F06 gates each action.
4) F13 obtains provider readiness receipts; F07 activates only when all required objects and controls are ready.

Generated product: Supplier admission portal, evidence queue, approval workflow, procurement connector and recurring review system.

Emergent obligations: Supplier identity, agreement party, approval subject and purchasing-system record must denote the same scoped entity. A provider-created record is one readiness fact among the required gates.

Change propagation: A receiving subsidiary changes the applicable requirements and reusable evidence without erasing the supplier’s other memberships.

Proof requirements: R02, R06, R10, R14, R25, R28, R30, R32.

### X03. Configured physical product to delivered and maintained asset

Families: F07, F11, F12, F13, F14, F15, F16, F21, F27, F28, F31, F32.

Inputs: Customer requirements, approved component catalogue, engineering models, stock, manufacturing process and installation constraints.

Bindings:
1) F27 emits one configuration’s geometry, material requirements and manufacturing definition.
2) F21 evaluates design scenarios; F15 nets materials and constructs supply work.
3) F11 and F12 allocate production and delivery capacity; F13 records actual orders and movements.
4) F07 uses inspection and commissioning evidence to activate the asset; F16 starts maintenance duties.

Generated product: Configure-to-order design, BOM and cost pack, manufacturing preparation, delivery plan, commissioning and service schedule.

Emergent obligations: Drawings, BOM revisions, toolpaths, inspection limits and delivered serial identity must agree. Resource planning and costs must use the same design version.

Change propagation: A discontinued part invalidates the affected geometry, engineering checks, stock plan, instructions and future maintenance parts list.

Proof requirements: R15, R16, R17, R18, R28, R30, R32.

### X04. System replacement with data and operating-model migration

Families: F01, F02, F03, F24, F25, F26, F31, F32, F33.

Inputs: Source behavior and data contracts, destination requirements, approved mapping, history, interface contracts and cutover constraints.

Bindings:
1) F01 supplies accepted behavior; F24 constructs the replacement system.
2) F03 migrates records with F02 identity reconciliation.
3) F25 connects remaining systems; F26 supplies rollout and version bindings.
4) F31 compares supported observable behaviors; F33 preserves replay and recovery.

Generated product: Replacement application, migration and discrepancy pack, cutover plan, compatibility evidence and rollback/recovery plan.

Emergent obligations: Migrated data must support the replacement’s behavioral invariants, including historical decisions and already-issued external effects. Old and new systems cannot both dispatch the same intent during cutover.

Change propagation: A destination schema revision changes mappings, generated storage, connectors and affected historical replay contracts.

Proof requirements: R07, R08, R09, R10, R12, R13, R30, R32.

### X05. Detection to diagnosis, repair and verified recovery

Families: F04, F06, F09, F11, F13, F16, F19, F31, F33, F34.

Inputs: Observed events, approved diagnostic hypotheses, repair catalogue, asset identity, runbook, current authority and provider contracts.

Bindings:
1) F34 produces a detection with finality and observation scope.
2) F04 supplies supporting records; F19 identifies the next discriminating test.
3) F11 schedules authorized tests and repair work; F09 coordinates dependencies.
4) F13 executes permitted actions and F31 checks recovery; F33 reconciles replayed state.

Generated product: Incident investigation and response system with a reproducible evidence and repair history.

Emergent obligations: The diagnosis must remain supported by the current evidence. Repeated alerts must not duplicate repairs. A stopped alert alone cannot establish restored service.

Change propagation: A corrected sensor event retracts the affected diagnosis, cancels stale pending work and records any already-completed effect separately.

Proof requirements: R10, R14, R19, R21, R28, R30.

### X06. Research question to laboratory campaign and report

Families: F11, F13, F17, F18, F21, F22, F25, F29, F31.

Inputs: Research question, factors and ranges, measurement model, instruments, permitted resources and declared analysis.

Bindings:
1) F22 emits a design and analysis contract; F11 assigns runs and resources.
2) F25 exposes instrument operations; F13 records actual measurements and failures.
3) F17 reconciles observations; F18 executes the declared analysis.
4) F29 publishes source-linked results and F31 qualifies the claims supported by the actual campaign.

Generated product: Experiment planner, run orchestration, instrument bindings, analysis and reproducible report.

Emergent obligations: Scheduling changes cannot silently destroy randomization or blocking. Missing runs and instrument changes must alter the supported estimands and uncertainty.

Change propagation: A failed instrument creates a revised measurement contract, affected-run ledger and re-evaluation of which comparisons remain supported.

Proof requirements: R16, R21, R23, R28, R31, R32.

### X07. Procedure revision to qualified workforce coverage

Families: F01, F02, F07, F09, F11, F23, F29, F30, F31, F32.

Inputs: Accepted procedure delta, competency mappings, learning assets, assessments, workforce and assignment records.

Bindings:
1) F32 identifies changed competencies from the F01 operating-model revision.
2) F23 creates targeted learning and assessment paths.
3) F11 schedules assessment and work coverage; F30 exposes the learner and assessor views.
4) F07 accepts new qualification evidence before enabling restricted work.

Generated product: Procedure-linked learning, assessment, reassessment and qualified staffing system.

Emergent obligations: The person, competency, procedure version and assessed evidence must agree across learning and access systems. Previous course attendance cannot silently satisfy a revised capability.

Change propagation: A new equipment procedure invalidates only the affected qualification claims and dependent assignments, using explicit mappings.

Proof requirements: R06, R11, R22, R25, R30, R32.

### X08. Agreement to usage billing, collection and entitlement

Families: F05, F06, F07, F08, F13, F14, F17, F25, F31, F34.

Inputs: Accepted agreement terms, account identity, usage events, pricing and tax inputs where applicable, payment interface and entitlement policy.

Bindings:
1) F08 emits version-bound terms and duties; F34 reconciles usage events.
2) F17 computes billable quantities and F14 generates the charge ledger.
3) F13 reconciles payment effects.
4) F05 and F07 update entitlements using the applicable grace, dispute and suspension policy.

Generated product: Metering, billing, collection, dispute and entitlement product suite.

Emergent obligations: Usage window, price version, payer and service identity must match. A payment timeout is an unknown effect, and must not cause a second charge or an unjustified suspension.

Change propagation: A backdated meter correction changes the affected ledger and adjustment duties while retaining the original invoice history.

Proof requirements: R10, R12, R14, R19, R28, R31.

### X09. Spatial observation to field response

Families: F02, F11, F12, F13, F17, F19, F28, F31, F34.

Inputs: Sensor or inspection observations, coordinates and uncertainty, geographic boundaries, resource availability and response policy.

Bindings:
1) F28 resolves spatial and temporal membership; F34 detects supported threshold events.
2) F19 requests additional observations where location or cause remains uncertain.
3) F11 and F12 allocate qualified field resources and routes.
4) F13 records field actions and F17 measures actual outcomes.

Generated product: Environmental response, utility fieldwork or geospatial service operations system.

Emergent obligations: Coordinate systems, time windows and uncertainty must agree before dispatch. A route plan cannot imply arrival or resolution.

Change propagation: A corrected boundary or coordinate reference invalidates affected detections, coverage totals and unexecuted dispatch proposals.

Proof requirements: R16, R18, R19, R28, R30.

### X10. Model-based controller construction and operation

Families: F21, F24, F25, F26, F27, F31, F34, F35.

Inputs: Accepted plant and control models, geometry, disturbances, timing requirements, sensor/actuator contract and deployment target.

Bindings:
1) F21 supplies model simulation and numerical coupling evidence.
2) F35 supplies control invariants and the operating envelope.
3) F24 and F25 generate the bounded controller implementation and hardware bindings.
4) F26 binds the runtime; F31 joins formal, implementation and hardware evidence; F34 records monitored conditions.

Generated product: A bounded control-system configuration with generated software, device bindings and explicit deployment evidence requirements.

Emergent obligations: Sensor error, execution time, communication latency and actuator saturation must fit the verified assumptions jointly. The simulation clock cannot stand in for hardware timing.

Change propagation: A changed sampling rate or actuator model requires renewed hybrid, implementation and timing qualification.

Proof requirements: R07, R13, R16, R20, R28, R32.

### X11. One accepted model to synchronized audience outputs

Families: F04, F06, F10, F17, F29, F30, F31, F32.

Inputs: Accepted content, data, source model, audience access policy, format requirements and review state.

Bindings:
1) F04 and F17 produce authorized source-linked records and measures.
2) F06 derives each audience’s allowed view.
3) F29 generates documents, slides, messages and media; F30 generates matching interactive views.
4) F10 binds review to each artifact version; F32 invalidates affected outputs on source change.

Generated product: A synchronized publishing and review system with native, printable and interactive outputs.

Emergent obligations: Audience filtering must happen before every output and derivative, including notes, attachments, hidden fields and cached views. Review must bind to the actual exported version.

Change propagation: Changing one amount or release permission identifies all affected formats, cached interfaces and prior approvals.

Proof requirements: R11, R24, R25, R27, R30.

### X12. Generator of configurable operating systems

Families: F01, F24, F25, F26, F30, F31, F32, F37, F38.

Inputs: Family specifications, feature models, qualified capabilities, operating models, target profiles, derivation assets and proof requirements.

Bindings:
1) F37 generates a configurator for each selected family definition.
2) Each configurator binds organization-specific models and target constraints.
3) F24, F25, F26 and F30 construct the selected software, service, environment and interface artifacts.
4) F38 composes the members and derives global obligations; F31 qualifies the resulting system and F32 maintains its derivation lineage.

Generated product: A reusable factory that generates family configurators and then configured executable systems with evidence and change manifests.

Emergent obligations: Correctness must survive both generation steps and cross-family assembly. Valid local feature choices may still create incompatible shared state, authority or capacity.

Change propagation: A new qualified component changes the available configuration space and downstream generator/product obligations with explicit compatibility and migration evidence.

Proof requirements: R01, R02, R03, R04, R05, R07, R08, R09, R13, R14, R29, R30, R32.

## 9. Public library functions for P301–P349

These are the recorded research pins and admitted public entries. They are not a tested universal environment lock or a claim about current releases. Select and qualify a compatible target environment before execution. The L identifiers in this section apply to P301–P349. Older proof records retain explicit function names and their separately recorded pins. Historical S/L/HD citation shorthand has been removed where its bibliography was absent; it must never be resolved against this different L namespace.

### L01 | Pydantic | 2.10.6 | pydantic

Role: Typed boundary validation and serialization
Assurance: jsonschema plus Ajv over a declared common schema subset
Boundary: Strict JSON and strict Python have different accepted representations; coercions, extra fields and non-finite values need explicit configuration.
  L01.validate: pydantic.TypeAdapter.validate_json
    Contract: An accepted type and explicit strict/extra policy; validate all records before effects.
  L01.dump: pydantic.TypeAdapter.dump_json
    Contract: Retain aliases, nulls, discriminators and envelope fields; serialize the validated model.
  L01.schema: pydantic.TypeAdapter.json_schema

Recorded project sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pydantic/pydantic/v2.10.6/pydantic/type_adapter.py

### L02 | jsonschema | 4.23.0 | jsonschema

Role: JSON Schema validation and schema checking
Assurance: Ajv 8.17.1 is a separate implementation; align dialect and format settings
Boundary: Schema validation cannot establish source semantics, key uniqueness across objects or authority.
  L02.schema: jsonschema.Draft202012Validator.check_schema
  L02.validate: jsonschema.Draft202012Validator.validate
    Contract: Pin Draft 2020-12; explicit format checking and reference registry; reject unexpected fields in the contract.
  L02.errors: jsonschema.Draft202012Validator.iter_errors

Recorded project sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python-jsonschema/jsonschema/v4.23.0/jsonschema/validators.py

### L03 | Ajv | 8.17.1 | npm:ajv

Role: Independent JSON Schema implementation
Assurance: jsonschema 4.23.0; separate JavaScript and Python validator implementations
Boundary: Use Ajv2020 for draft 2020-12; formats and extra options must match; validation alone supplies no domain meaning.
  L03.compile: Ajv2020.compile
    Contract: Import ajv/dist/2020; pin strictness, coercion disabled, defaults disabled, matching formats and reference set.
  L03.dialect: Ajv2020

Recorded project sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ajv-validator/ajv/v8.17.1/lib/core.ts; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ajv-validator/ajv/v8.17.1/lib/2020.ts

### L04 | pySHACL | 0.30.1 | pyshacl

Role: Shape validation and rule expansion
Assurance: Independent SHACL engine remains unqualified; Z3 checks selected relational invariants only
Boundary: pySHACL depends on RDFLib and OWL-RL; RDFLib is not an independent SHACL oracle. Empty target populations need explicit coverage checks.
  L04.validate: pyshacl.validate
    Contract: Declare inference, target population, shapes graph and supported SHACL Core profile.
  L04.rules: pyshacl.shacl_rules
    Contract: Rule expansion requires its own termination/coverage profile; Jena Core validation is not a rule-expansion oracle.

Recorded project sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RDFLib/pySHACL/v0.30.1/pyshacl/entrypoints.py

### L05 | RDFLib | 7.1.3 | rdflib

Role: RDF parsing, query, serialization and accepted graph projection
Assurance: NetworkX for explicit topology; independent query engine needed for unrestricted SPARQL equivalence
Boundary: Parser and query semantics do not approve ontological meaning; external retrieval and base URIs must be controlled.
  L05.parse: rdflib.Graph.parse
  L05.query: rdflib.Graph.query
    Contract: Accepted SPARQL query; explicit graph/base URI and controlled network access.

### L06 | NetworkX | 3.4.2 | networkx

### L07 | Z3 | 4.13.4.0 | z3-solver

Role: Implication, counterexamples, finite feature constraints and exact arithmetic logic
Assurance: cvc5 1.2.0; distinct SMT implementation, restricted common theories
Boundary: Unknown is unresolved. Use fresh solver instances for the reference profile; engine agreement still shares the formal encoding.
  L07.load: z3.Solver.from_string
    Contract: Fresh solver, accepted SMT-LIB over the declared common logic; bind every symbol to its contract.
  L07.check: z3.Solver.check
    Contract: Preserve sat, unsat, unknown, timeout and errors; require satisfiable antecedent before implication acceptance.
  L07.model: z3.Solver.model
  L07.core: z3.Solver.unsat_core

  L07.track: z3.Solver.assert_and_track

### L09 | clingo | 5.7.1 | clingo

Assurance: Z3/cvc5 after an explicit common semantics mapping
Boundary: ASP default negation, multiple models and grounding limits are not classical missing-value semantics; embedded Python is excluded.

  L63.info: soundfile.info

Recorded project sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bastibe/python-soundfile/0.13.1/soundfile.py

### L64 | CPython standard library | 3.12.10 | cpython

Role: Published assertions and standard JSON serialization
Assurance: Expected values supplied by independent libraries or justified invariants
Boundary: Assertions compare values; they do not justify expected values. The local editorial runtime is separately recorded.
  L64.assert: unittest.TestCase.assertEqual
    Contract: Published callable emitted by Ghostwriter unittest style; expected output comes from a library/invariant.
  L64.dump: json.dumps
    Contract: allow_nan=False; UTF-8 transport; stable identities and required fields retained.
  L64.load: json.loads
    Contract: Schema-validation follows parsing; JSON parse success is only syntax acceptance.
  L64.parse: ast.parse
    Contract: Source parse check only; no business semantics or arbitrary execution claim.

Recorded project sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.12.10/Lib/unittest/case.py; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.12.10/Lib/json/__init__.py; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.12.10/Lib/ast.py

### L65 | R pwr | 1.3-0 | R:pwr

Role: Independent two-sample t-test power and sample-size calculations
Assurance: statsmodels on matched test/effect/alternative/sample-size semantics; R stats is a separate numerical implementation path
Boundary: Pin R runtime before execution. The fixed-test profile does not implement arbitrary adaptive stopping.
  L65.power: pwr::pwr.t.test
    Contract: pwr1.3-0 with pinned R runtime; align equal sample count, standardized effect, sidedness and alpha; one unknown parameter.
  L65.unequal: pwr::pwr.t2n.test
    Contract: n1,n2>=2, matched two-sample pooled-variance effect, sidedness and alpha; R stats is a separate execution path from SciPy.

Recorded project sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cran/pwr/1.3-0/R/pwr.t.test.R; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cran/pwr/1.3-0/R/pwr.t2n.test.R

## 10. Full proof-chain catalogue

Read the complete record and its hardening requirements before using a chain. Arrow order is operational order. A later hardening requirement constrains the earlier chain. Historical file paths in a chain identify input/output roles and dependencies; those example files are not supplied by this compact specification. An unavailable helper, absent adapter or undeclared schema remains a gap. Historical demonstrations and literal test examples are never substituted for a current generated acceptance guard.

### Historical baseline pins

The earliest chains recorded the following environment. Later per-proof pins and hardening sources can differ. Resolve the selected chain’s actual lock; do not combine these versions by assumption.

```text
ufal.udpipe 1.4.0.1 ; predpatt 1.0.1 ; clingo 5.8.2 ; clorm 1.6.3 ; z3-solver 5.1.0.0 ; networkx 3.6.1 ; timexy 0.1.3 ; spacy 3.8.16 ; zen-engine 2.0.2 ; Jinja2 3.1.6 ; pm4py 2.7.23.8 ; pydantic 2.13.5 ; duckdb 1.5.5 ; markdown 3.6 ; lxml 6.1.3 ; python-pptx 1.0.2 ; python-docx 1.2.0 ; openpyxl 3.1.2 ; csv-diff 1.2 ; PyYAML 6.0.3
```

## P1  procedures -> facts

Primary family: F01.

Status: historical chain; apply the hardening requirement below before admission.

```

-> read (compiled_ai.read.read_html | compiled_ai.read.read_text; html.parser.HTMLParser)

-> facts
```
