```
-> bind derivations to source entities (rdflib.Graph.add; PROV-O predicates)
-> typed rule register + rejected or unresolved clauses
```

contract: Grammar and semantics are versioned separately. require, permit, and forbid are distinct. Boolean connectors have declared precedence. Source-candidate extraction cannot silently approve a mapping.

theoretical positive test: For the S01 four-voting-member minimum, an approved count >= 4 expression retains actor, integer threshold, and clause reference. The four-member boundary satisfies the expression; three does not.

theoretical adverse test: Treating the phrase "not merely" as an unconditional prohibition changes the meaning. The compiler must reject that unsupported construction or require an explicit approved mapping. Removing an exception or swapping actor/object also fails semantic acceptance.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. A grammar, typed AST, and source map can support deterministic compilation of a bounded controlled language. Unrestricted procedure-to-rule equivalence remains unestablished.

public-data limit: S01 tests a narrow governance domain. It supplies neither banking applicability nor a labeled corpus proving extraction accuracy.

required implementation and evidence: Implement the grammar and source-offset adapter; archive positive and counterexample mappings; run parse/print/parse, semantic mutation, and reviewer-acceptance tests. Report unsupported-clause coverage.

### HARDENING P191 -- The grammar refuses the construction the proof says it must refuse
approved clause text -> parsed controlled expression -> typed rule record
functions: lark.Lark.parse for the controlled language; pydantic.BaseModel.model_validate with strict typing and a Literal modality; z3.Solver for the boundary evaluation
sources: raw.githubusercontent.com/lark-parser/lark/1.3.1/lark/grammars/common.lark

## P192  typed request dimensions + rule scopes -> applicability evaluation -> scoped requirement candidates

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P4 policies; P6 typed records; P191 rule AST
new capability: Explicit scope algebra compiled through the new AST rather than action-lemma matching

```
P191 rule records + request-dimension schema + typed request
-> validate enumerations and absent dimensions (pydantic.TypeAdapter.validate_python)
-> expand only finite declared domains (itertools.product; explicit scope adapter)
-> derive applicability and unresolved facts (clingo.Control.add/ground/solve)
-> check candidate consistency under the same scope encoding (z3.Solver.check)
-> preserve included, excluded, and unresolved reasons (pandas.DataFrame.merge with cardinality validation)
-> requirement candidates + applicability explanation rows
```

contract: Unknown and wildcard are different. A missing region never means all regions. A request may bind a set only where the dimension contract explicitly permits sets.

theoretical positive test: A voting-member rule applies to a voting-member request; the same rule is out of scope for a fully typed regular-member request. A missing member type stays unresolved.

theoretical adverse test: A fabricated catch-all converts missing member type into permission. A rejected enum, absent booking entity, or inconsistent multi-value binding blocks a definitive requirement manifest.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Finite scope matching is expressible in existing rule and constraint engines once the cross-engine scope semantics are fixed.

public-data limit: Public control-profile selection is an analogue. Actual jurisdiction and legal-entity requirement tables are absent.

required implementation and evidence: Define scope set operations and three-way applicability; exhaustively enumerate the bounded test domain; keep grounded rule IDs for every candidate; independently evaluate a small truth table.

### HARDENING P192 -- The missing dimension stays unresolved under the engine the proof names
typed request dimensions and rule scopes -> three-way applicability -> requirement candidates
functions: clingo.Control add, ground and solve with an explicit has-dimension predicate and default negation; the answer set asserted directly rather than inferred from the absence of a diagnostic

## P193  scoped obligations and prohibitions -> satisfiable overlap search -> contradiction register

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P2 solver checks; P192 scoped requirements
new capability: Conflict witnesses tied to overlapping scope and modality

```
P192 requirements + approved precedence policy
-> group by actor, action, object, and relevant effect identity (pandas.DataFrame.groupby)
-> encode scope intersections and contrary requirements (z3.And/Not/Implies)
-> track each source-backed assertion (z3.Solver.assert_and_track)
-> solve and extract conflict witnesses or tracked cores (Solver.check; Solver.model; Solver.unsat_core)
-> link findings to rule and source nodes (networkx.MultiDiGraph.add_edge; rdflib.Graph.add)
-> contradiction register + separate ambiguity and solver-undecided rows
```

contract: Different jurisdictions alone do not conflict. A permission is not automatically a requirement; conflict semantics and precedence must be declared. An unsat core is not claimed minimal without an explicit minimization test.

theoretical positive test: Injected require(action) and forbid(action) with an identical satisfiable scope produce a witnessed contradiction. Disjoint fully known scopes produce no same-scope contradiction.

theoretical adverse test: Globally conjoining all rules erases their scopes and yields false conflicts. Solver unknown or timeout must remain undecided. A missing authority cannot be repaired by an automatic deny-overrides assumption.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. The method detects contradictions in the encoded theory. It does not choose the legally correct rule.

public-data limit: Public clauses do not supply a precedence hierarchy for banking regulations or competing policy owners.

required implementation and evidence: Implement conflict semantics; assert a known overlap and a disjoint control case; test the witness independently; preserve solver settings and source IDs. Route owner resolution as new versioned input.

### HARDENING P193 -- The core names a sufficient subset, not the conflicting set
scoped contrary requirements -> tracked assertions -> conflict witnesses and cores
functions: z3.Solver.assert_and_track for the source-backed assertions; Solver.check and Solver.unsat_core for the witness; a drop-one re-check over the core for the minimality test; a re-solve for completeness
sources: raw.githubusercontent.com/Z3Prover/z3/z3-5.1.0/src/api/python/z3/z3.py; raw.githubusercontent.com/cvc5/cvc5/cvc5-1.3.4/include/cvc5/cvc5.h

## P194  rule and control graph -> shape and completeness validation -> source-linked defect report

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P37 RDF graph; P95 schema contracts; P191 rule records
new capability: pySHACL validation over a declared complete target inventory

```
P191 rule graph + expected control inventory + SHACL shapes
-> load local RDF and preserve stable identifiers (rdflib.Graph.parse)
-> validate required links and types (pyshacl.validate)
-> inspect results and focus-node references (rdflib.Graph.query)
-> compare validated target IDs with the independent expected inventory (set difference; pandas.merge)
-> link every defect to its source and failed shape (PROV-O links; Jinja2.Environment with StrictUndefined)
-> defect register + coverage report
```

contract: SHACL validates declared shapes, not truth. Zero targeted nodes cannot count as success when controls were expected. Missing owners remain missing.

theoretical positive test: A control with one valid source, one declared authority reference, and required evidence links conforms to the test shapes. Every expected control appears in the coverage reconciliation.

theoretical adverse test: Remove its actor link, mistype an evidence date, or remove the class that selects the node. The first two yield violations; the third must be caught by the independent target-inventory check.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Shape validation plus target coverage supports graph integrity and prevents vacuous conformance.

public-data limit: OSCAL gives structural examples. It does not identify the authorized people for the target program.

required implementation and evidence: Implement shapes and explicit target inventory; run missing-property, duplicate-ID, wrong-datatype, and empty-target mutations; archive the full validation report.

### HARDENING P194 -- The report conforms because it targeted nothing

## P195  source revisions and text normalization -> stable span mapping -> semantic-change candidates

Primary family: F01.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P1 canonical text and byte checks; P120 content comparison
new capability: Lark-assisted token classification with bidirectional source-span mapping

```
original text + revised text + normalization policy
-> hash each original byte sequence before decoding (hashlib.sha256; explicit codec)
-> preserve raw and normalized offsets (unicodedata.normalize; versioned span-map adapter)
-> tokenize supported controlled expressions (lark.Lark.lex)
-> compare original clauses and AST fields separately (difflib.SequenceMatcher.get_opcodes; typed AST comparison)
-> record derivation and version relationships (rdflib.Graph.add; PROV-O revision links)
-> source-change map + semantic-review candidates
```

contract: Offsets are tagged as bytes or Unicode code points. Normalization is not assumed length preserving. A cosmetic text change and a semantic change are different findings.

theoretical positive test: A whitespace-only change preserves the typed rule while updating its source hash and span map. A threshold edit from four to five changes the numeric AST field.

theoretical adverse test: A removed negation, altered exception, or combined Unicode character must not be hidden by normalized text equality. A pointer into the wrong source version is rejected.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Exact text and AST comparison can identify changes within the supported representation. It cannot decide meaning for an unsupported paraphrase.

public-data limit: No public multilingual gold-standard policy corpus was ingested. Translation equivalence remains untested.

required implementation and evidence: Implement code-point/byte maps; require reverse span checks; exercise Unicode composition, insertion, and deletion; retain raw source versions.

### HARDENING P195 -- Normalization moves the offsets it is asked to preserve
original and revised source text -> normalized text and span map -> cosmetic and semantic change findings
functions: unicodedata.normalize with the form declared and both lengths measured; difflib.SequenceMatcher.get_opcodes for the change shape; the typed AST comparison from P191 for the semantic side
sources: raw.githubusercontent.com/numpy/numpy/v2.4.6/numpy/_core/multiarray.py:; raw.githubusercontent.com/workalendar/workalendar/17.0.0/workalendar/core.py; raw.githubusercontent.com/ruby/ruby/v3_3_6/lib/unicode_normalize/normalize.rb; raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/v3.11.15/Doc/library/difflib.rst

## P196  control imports and clause references -> dependency closure -> complete-or-unresolved rule pack

Primary family: F01.

```
-> compute dependency closure and cycles (networkx.descendants; strongly_connected_components)

-> reconcile every required reference with a resolved source version (pydantic model validation; set difference)
-> sealed rule pack or explicit unresolved-reference report
```

contract: References, precedence edges, and ownership edges have different types. A reference cycle is not automatically a workflow cycle. Wildcards use the pinned specification semantics.

theoretical positive test: Select an explicit S03 control ID and its declared parameter references; all reachable mandatory targets enter the pack with their own source versions.

theoretical adverse test: A missing imported catalog, unresolvable fragment, ambiguous version, or cycle requiring unsupported semantics blocks a completeness claim. A network fetch is forbidden during offline qualification.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Closure over a finite typed reference graph is feasible once source resolution and import semantics are implemented.

public-data limit: No full OSCAL resolver was executed. The tutorial alone is insufficient evidence for complete OSCAL compliance.

## P197  one approved decision AST -> independent engine adapters -> bounded decision equivalence

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P54 input enumeration; P55 cross-engine checks; P191-P193
new capability: Hypothesis-generated semantic differential testing across the expanded rule representation

```
approved AST + bounded domain + independent truth-table oracle
-> compile separate engine representations (Jinja2 render to Zen JDM; clingo.Control.add; z3 expressions)
-> generate boundaries, missing values, and contrary evidence (hypothesis.given; strategies; explicit adversarial fixtures)
-> evaluate each supported engine (zen.ZenDecision.evaluate; clingo.Control.solve; z3.Solver.check)
-> normalize result dimensions without losing unknown states (pydantic typed result adapter)
-> compare every result and source explanation with the oracle (pytest assertions; pandas comparison)
-> equivalence report + minimal failing examples
```

contract: Compare only a declared common semantic subset. Rule hit policy, default negation, missing values, and multiple answer sets need explicit mappings. Engines may refuse unsupported cases.

theoretical positive test: For fully known integer counts within the test domain, the count >= 4 rule agrees with an independent arithmetic table at 3, 4, and 5 and throughout enumeration.

theoretical adverse test: A missing count cannot become zero in one engine and unknown in another. A multiple-answer-set case must not be treated as a unique decision. Common compiler errors are tested against the independent oracle.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Differential testing can expose adapter disagreement and oracle mismatch. Agreement on examples is not proof of arbitrary-language equivalence.

public-data limit: Public examples cover simple policies. Complex domain exceptions and deployed engine-version compatibility remain untested.

required implementation and evidence: Implement three adapters; freeze engine versions after tests; preserve failing examples; record supported features and exhaustive versus sampled coverage.

### HARDENING P197 -- The two engines disagree on the same missing count, by construction
one approved AST -> independent engine representations -> compared decisions and counterexamples

## P198  scoped decision rows -> gaps and overlaps -> totality qualification

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P54 enumeration; P192 applicability; P197 adapters
new capability: Solver-backed coverage obligations and generated missing-value cases

```
decision rows + closed request domain + explicit unknown-state policy
-> validate row predicates and output types (P191 Lark AST; pydantic)
-> enumerate small domains (itertools.product)
-> seek uncovered and conflicting inputs symbolically (z3.Solver.check; model extraction)
-> generate threshold-neighbor mutations (Hypothesis strategies)
-> compare row hits under the selected policy (zen.ZenDecision.evaluate with trace)
-> gap/overlap witnesses + qualified coverage statement
```

contract: Totality is relative to the declared domain. An unknown dimension has its own route. Multiple rows may coexist under a collect policy; contradictory outputs still require resolution.

theoretical positive test: Rows n < 4 and n >= 4 partition the declared nonnegative integer domain. An explicit missing-n route covers incomplete input separately.

theoretical adverse test: Changing the second row to n > 4 exposes the gap at n = 4. Adding an incompatible n >= 3 row exposes overlap; unsupported enum values remain outside the certified domain.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Finite enumeration or a decidable solver encoding supports a precise coverage claim, with counterexamples when it fails.

public-data limit: A table can be complete over a wrong domain. Owner acceptance of request dimensions remains required.

required implementation and evidence: Archive domain schema and row policy; run the boundary gap mutation and conflicting-output mutation; retain solver undecided states and sampled-coverage labels.

### HARDENING P198 -- The gap is exactly at the boundary, and totality is relative to the domain

## P199  required controls + dated authority and evidence -> separate gate decisions -> actionable exceptions

Primary family: F07.

new capability: SHACL evidence integrity feeding explicit rule and constraint gates

contract: Use separate evidence, applicability, execution, and authorization fields from G01. A role label does not authenticate a person. A signature check would require a trust policy and credentials.

theoretical adverse test: A draft approval, wrong-role approval, stale evidence, later withdrawal, or modified artifact cannot establish the gate. Missing credentials return unresolved authentication.

## P200  scoped requirements + dependencies + gates -> deterministic resolver -> explained initiation manifest

Primary family: F07.

contract: A manifest lists required, satisfied, missing, conflicting, and unresolved items separately. Completeness requires all mandatory sources and dimensions. The result is a requirement derivation, not case completion.

theoretical positive test: A fully bound public analogue produces an exact control subset with all required dependencies and source explanations. Reordering input rows preserves semantic output.

theoretical adverse test: Delete a mandatory scope dimension or referenced source, introduce a conflicting rule, or omit an evidence link. The resolver must retain a partial manifest and block a complete/ready result.

## P201  records without shared IDs -> probabilistic linkage -> reviewed identity candidates

Primary family: F02.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P6 typed records; P154 ambiguity-preserving reconciliation; P199 gates
new capability: Splink record linkage with an explicit abstention and review band

```
sealed public labeled sample + blocking and comparison settings
-> preserve source record IDs and original fields (pandas.DataFrame; pydantic)
-> build a linkage model with an explicit backend (splink.Linker; splink.DuckDBAPI)
-> train only on the declared training partition (Linker.training methods; settings as versioned data)
-> score candidate pairs (Linker.inference.predict)
-> route matches, nonmatches, and review candidates (P191 controlled threshold rules; clingo)
-> evaluate against held-out labels and record lineage (pandas joins; rdflib PROV-O)
-> pairwise candidate register + review queue + measured linkage report
```

contract: Blocking recall, pairwise precision/recall, cluster errors, and calibration are separate measures. A connected cluster does not prove all pairwise identities. True labels never enter matching features.

theoretical positive test: A noisy duplicate pair above the approved acceptance threshold is a match candidate; ambiguous pairs remain review candidates. Evaluation uses a label-separated holdout.

theoretical adverse test: Two unrelated records with the same name must not be merged by name alone. A-B and B-C similarity cannot bypass an A-C contradiction. An uncalibrated score cannot be described as certified identity.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for linkage evaluation and review routing. Identity proof and production error rates remain unproven.

public-data limit: Synthetic errors in S07 may differ substantially from client and legal-entity data. No production threshold can be inferred from its size or labels.

## P202  legal-entity relationship records -> typed parent graph -> look-through coverage and gaps

Primary family: F02.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

new capability: pySHACL validation with explicit relationship-kind and missing-parent semantics

```
sealed GLEIF relationship and exception records + scope policy

-> traverse declared relationship types with cycle and depth controls (networkx descendants; strongly_connected_components)
-> derive coverage and unresolved endpoints (clingo rules; explicit stop conditions)
-> parent-path register + look-through gap report
```

contract: Accounting consolidation, legal ownership, beneficial ownership, and control are separate predicates. Missing percentages never become zero or 100 percent. Graph depth limits are computational limits, not regulatory thresholds.

theoretical positive test: An explicit direct-parent relationship is retained with source, dates, and type. An exception such as NO_LEI yields an unresolved parent identifier with the exception reason.

theoretical adverse test: Treating NATURAL_PERSONS as a named natural-person owner or treating NON_PUBLIC as no parent produces an invalid inference. A cycle cannot generate an infinite ownership chain.

## P203  typed semantic graph -> replaceable storage adapters -> query-result equivalence

Primary family: F02.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P37 RDFLib; P38 Kuzu; P122 SQLite; P202 parent graph
new capability: Hypothesis-generated adapter tests and SHACL schema checks

```
canonical node and edge tables + query contract
-> validate types, IDs, and multiplicities (pydantic; pyshacl.validate)
-> load independent backends (rdflib.Graph.add; sqlite3.executemany; optional kuzu.Connection.execute)
-> execute equivalent bounded queries (SPARQL; parameterized SQL; optional Cypher)
-> normalize ordering, nulls, and duplicate-edge semantics (typed result adapter)
-> generate graph mutations and compare independent oracle results (Hypothesis; networkx traversal)
-> portability report + backend-specific limitations
```

contract: Use stable business IDs. Distinguish bags from sets and absent values from empty strings. Do not equate RDF blank-node IDs with durable entity IDs. Kuzu is optional and its archived status is recorded.

theoretical positive test: A two-edge parent path and duplicate qualified relationships return the contractually same result in each backend after explicit normalization.

theoretical adverse test: Losing one parallel edge, treating SQL NULL as a matched value, or leaking backend row IDs fails equivalence. An unavailable backend is skipped and remains unqualified.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. A narrow storage abstraction can reduce dependency lock-in. General Cypher/SPARQL equivalence is outside scope.

public-data limit: No performance, concurrency, or migration-at-scale benchmark was run. S04 defines data semantics, not storage guarantees.

required implementation and evidence: Implement only needed queries; run generated edge cases; round-trip the canonical graph; archive query plans and target backend versions; test backup and restoration separately.

## P211  effective dates + recorded dates -> bitemporal selection -> reproducible historical decisions

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

new capability: prov lineage plus a two-clock selection contract

```

-> validate nonempty intervals and time zones (pydantic; datetime)
-> select facts known by the knowledge cutoff (DuckDB parameterized WHERE)
-> select the version effective at the business cutoff (DuckDB ASOF LEFT JOIN or interval join with explicit tie policy)
-> independently recompute a bounded sample (pandas.merge_asof; manual interval oracle)
-> record fact versions used by the decision (prov.model.ProvDocument; entity/activity/used)
-> historical decision snapshot + knowledge and effectiveness gaps
```

contract: Use half-open intervals [from,to) in the assessment model. Source date-only semantics need an approved mapping. Effective time, publication time, and local observation time are different. Ambiguous equal-priority versions are errors.

theoretical positive test: A correction learned after knowledge cutoff K cannot change the answer to what was known at K, even if its effective date is earlier. A present-day reconstruction can differ and must say why.

theoretical adverse test: Selecting the latest row globally introduces future knowledge. ASOF alone is insufficient for two-clock history. An overlap without precedence or a missing initial version returns unresolved.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Explicit interval selection can reproduce decisions against both clocks.

public-data limit: The public schedule has no complete knowledge-time history in this assessment. Injected correction records test mechanics only.

required implementation and evidence: Implement interval integrity and tie rules; test retroactive corrections, publication delays, time zones, and both cutoffs independently; retain selected row IDs.

## P213  withdrawn or corrected evidence -> support recomputation -> precise invalidation and rechecks

Primary family: F32.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P189 withdrawal handling; P199 gates; P212 impact
new capability: Truth-maintenance contract over explicit support sets and provenance

```
current facts + support sets + evidence retractions + dependency rules
-> verify exact support and evidence identities (pydantic; pyshacl.validate)
-> remove or supersede only the targeted fact versions (pandas exact joins)
-> recompute the affected rule component under declared semantics (clingo.Control grounding/solving; explicit fresh solve baseline)
-> preserve alternative valid support and invalidate unsupported conclusions (support-set adapter; networkx dependencies)
-> record invalidation and regeneration lineage (prov.model.ProvDocument)
-> invalidated outputs + retained outputs + targeted recheck set
```

contract: A conclusion with two independent sufficient supports remains supported when only one is withdrawn. Conjunctive and alternative supports differ. Negation-as-failure rules require recomputation beyond a simple descendant walk.

theoretical positive test: If evidence A or evidence B independently supports a fixture control, withdrawing A leaves the conclusion supported by B and updates its explanation.

theoretical adverse test: Deleting every descendant of A incorrectly invalidates B-supported conclusions. A rule depending on absence must be reevaluated when a new fact arrives.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for a declared finite rule fragment. General incremental nonmonotonic truth maintenance needs dedicated adapter tests.

public-data limit: The public examples do not supply actual revocation notices or a real support graph.

required implementation and evidence: Implement explicit sufficient-support groups and fresh-solve comparison; test alternative/conjunctive supports, cycles, negative premises, and backdated withdrawals; verify unaffected hashes where justified.

## P217  retry and review loops -> fair-path analysis -> completion and deadlock obligations

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P29 execution pattern; P34 soundness limits; P216 transition system
new capability: pyModelChecking liveness with explicit environment assumptions

```

-> identify reachable loops and dead ends (networkx.strongly_connected_components; descendants)
-> define the exact liveness formula (pyModelChecking.CTL or LTL constructors)
-> check eventual approved termination under the declared fair-path semantics (modelcheck)

-> attach assumptions and failing cycles to source requirements (prov; typed witness records)

```

contract: Define successful, rejected, cancelled, and timed-out terminals separately. Eventual success cannot be assumed when a client may never reply. Fairness is a modeled assumption with operational ownership.

theoretical positive test: A bounded-retry fixture eventually reaches success or an explicitly permitted exception terminal. The reported property names which outcome is guaranteed.

theoretical adverse test: An infinite self-loop awaiting an unconstrained external response defeats unconditional eventual completion. Hiding that loop with unjustified fairness is rejected.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for model-scoped liveness. Unconditional completion is unsupported without appropriate environmental assumptions.

public-data limit: A finite observed log cannot establish behavior of all infinite execution paths.

theoretical positive test: A paired response within the declared horizon satisfies the fixture obligation after completeness is established. Before the horizon closes, an unanswered trigger is pending.

theoretical adverse test: A response for another case cannot discharge the obligation. An incomplete tail, late arrival, or time-unit mismatch prevents a final pass. Missing samples are not filled silently.

## P222  task precedences + finite capacity -> CP-SAT schedule -> independently verified resource leveling

Primary family: F11.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P142 critical path; P145 capacity detection; P221 plan
new capability: OR-Tools resource-constrained optimization

```
S08 job data or validated P221 tasks + resource capacity
-> validate nonnegative integer durations and exact resource IDs (pydantic)
-> construct interval and precedence constraints (ortools.sat.python.cp_model.CpModel; new_interval_var; add_no_overlap)
-> minimize makespan with a declared solver budget (CpModel.minimize; CpSolver.solve)
-> preserve OPTIMAL/FEASIBLE/INFEASIBLE/UNKNOWN/MODEL_INVALID and objective bounds (CpSolver result adapter)
-> independently verify order, overlap, and objective (networkx DAG; exhaustive machine-order oracle for S08)
-> feasible schedule + verification and optimality scope
```

contract: Finite capacity is explicit. Preemption, setup times, calendars, and alternative resources are excluded unless modeled. A feasible incumbent is not automatically optimal.

theoretical positive test: The S08 sample has a published optimum of 11 units. A bounded exhaustive machine-order oracle should independently recover 11 and validate all eight operations.

theoretical adverse test: A schedule overlapping two tasks on the same machine fails. A solver time limit with FEASIBLE status must not be labeled OPTIMAL. Negative durations and missing resources are invalid input.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. This extends P145 conflict detection to actual proposed resource leveling on an independently checkable small instance.

public-data limit: The synthetic job shop proves no reviewer productivity, staffing need, or real completion commitment.

required implementation and evidence: Implement interval mapping; compare the sample against exhaustive enumeration; inject overlaps and precedence violations; record objective, bound, gap, status, seed, and solver budget.

## P223  task qualifications + reviewer availability -> constrained assignment -> coverage and infeasibility explanations

Primary family: F11.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P146 RACI; P180 shift coverage; P206 authorization; P222 scheduling
new capability: OR-Tools optional assignment intervals and qualification constraints

```
task demands + reviewer skills + calendars + control-role constraints
-> validate the complete eligible-worker inventory (pydantic; pyshacl.validate)
-> create assignment choices and optional intervals (cp_model.CpModel Boolean variables; optional interval constraints)
-> impose coverage, availability, and separation of duties (OR-Tools constraints; P206 authorization predicates)
-> optimize only approved cost or lateness objectives (CpSolver.solve)
-> verify assignments independently (pandas joins; z3.Solver)
-> proposed assignments + uncovered demand + conflicting constraints
```

contract: Past performance of an activity does not establish current qualification. Unknown capacity or skill coverage prevents a definitive feasible/infeasible claim about the real workforce.

theoretical positive test: A fixture task requiring skill A is assigned only to an available, authorized A-qualified reviewer, with no overlap and valid maker/checker separation.

theoretical adverse test: Removing the only qualified reviewer makes the complete fixture infeasible. If the worker inventory itself is incomplete, report a data gap instead of real-world infeasibility.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for assignment mechanics under a complete skill and availability contract.

public-data limit: Public event originators are not a validated staff authorization or qualification register.

required implementation and evidence: Implement eligibility as a separate audited input; test missing skills, revoked roles, double booking, maker/checker violations, and absent coverage; independently verify all assignments.

## P224  multi-calendar obligations + precedence -> allowed time slots -> SLA-feasible schedule

Primary family: F11.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P58 business days; P142 calendar mapping; P215 deadlines; P222 schedule
new capability: Calendar-aware OR-Tools scheduling with explicit time-unit conversion

```
tasks + source calendars + deadlines + handoff delays
-> normalize local calendars to bounded UTC slots (datetime.zoneinfo; numpy business-day functions)
-> validate slot granularity and duration conversions (pydantic; fractions.Fraction)
-> constrain starts and finishes to allowed windows (OR-Tools CP-SAT integer domains and intervals)
-> enforce handoff precedence and configured deadlines (CpModel.add; minimize declared lateness only when soft)
-> map results back to local clock labels (datetime; P215 clock adapter)
-> calendar-feasible plan + unsatisfied or unresolved SLA rows
```

contract: A hard deadline may not be silently softened. Rounding into integer slots is explicit and conservative under the chosen contract. Nonworking client time and reviewer time use distinct calendars.

theoretical positive test: A task ending at a region boundary hands off only to an available slot in the next region; the returned UTC interval and local display agree.

theoretical adverse test: A daylight-saving shift, nonworking holiday, or missing calendar must not create phantom capacity. A one-minute overrun remains an overrun under a one-minute hard boundary.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Bounded time-slot scheduling is feasible with approved calendars and units.

public-data limit: Neither a public release calendar nor the job-shop example defines actual SLAs or regional operating capacity.

required implementation and evidence: Implement time-zone and granularity policies; test daylight-saving folds/gaps, holidays, end boundaries, hard/soft deadlines, and unknown calendars; compare date calculations independently.

## P225  declared arrivals + service times + resources -> queue simulation -> operating-scenario comparison

Primary family: F21.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P12 timing measures with gap limits; P145 capacity; P222 schedules
new capability: SimPy discrete-event queue simulation

```
arrival sequence + service-time model + routing + staffing configuration
-> validate model units and complete scenario parameters (pydantic)
-> construct queues and service processes (simpy.Environment; Resource.request/release; Environment.process)
-> run a defined horizon or until all finite jobs finish (Environment.run)
-> record waiting, service, completion, and unfinished work separately (pandas DataFrame)
-> compare tiny cases with an analytic or hand-enumerated oracle (fractions.Fraction; independent recurrence)
-> scenario metrics + assumptions + unresolved calibration needs
```

contract: Observed gaps between events cannot be used as service times without justification. Specify queue discipline, warm-up, stopping rule, arrivals, and right-censoring. Seeded simulation is not a prediction guarantee.

theoretical positive test: Two simultaneous three-unit jobs with one FIFO resource finish at units 3 and 6; with two resources they finish at 3 and 3. The fixture waiting-time totals are three and zero.

theoretical adverse test: Dropping unfinished jobs at the horizon makes the mean look better and fails conservation. An overloaded arrival scenario must report backlog growth rather than a stable steady-state estimate.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for scenario mechanics. Empirical realism depends on validated arrival, service, and routing inputs.

public-data limit: No calibrated target operating model or actual time saving was established. Public simulation data remains simulated.

required implementation and evidence: Implement event conservation and resource occupancy checks; compare tiny deterministic cases; test queue discipline, censoring, empty demand, and overloaded scenarios; measure repeated-seed uncertainty.

## P226  rework and client-response assumptions -> retry-loop simulation -> effort and elapsed-time decomposition

Primary family: F21.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P36 variants; P215 clocks; P225 queue simulation
new capability: SimPy retry and external-delay modeling with exact small-case oracles

```
process routes + service effort + rework probability + client-delay model
-> separate internal service and external waits (pydantic; explicit state categories)
-> simulate retry cycles and resource release during external waits (SimPy processes, timeouts, and resources)
-> retain attempt IDs and parent-case identity (pandas; provenance)
-> aggregate disjoint time intervals and resource effort (interval union adapter; Fraction arithmetic)
-> compare a bounded independent geometric expectation where assumptions apply (declared formula oracle)
-> rework scenario report + time attribution
```

contract: The geometric expectation applies only to independent retries with constant probability below one. A client wait does not occupy a reviewer unless the model explicitly says so. Parallel elapsed intervals are unioned, not blindly summed.

theoretical positive test: With two service units per attempt and independent retry probability 1/4, expected service is 2/(1-1/4)=8/3 units in the unbounded toy model. A zero-retry variant reduces to one attempt.

theoretical adverse test: Retry probability one has no finite geometric expectation. Counting client wait as reviewer effort or adding overlapping delays twice must fail the accounting checks.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for explicit rework models. Causal or empirical rework reduction is unproven.

public-data limit: The public log descriptions do not identify all independent retry probabilities or true staff effort.

required implementation and evidence: Implement effort/elapsed separation, attempt lineage, queue release semantics, and retry caps; validate deterministic cases and probability endpoints; keep truncation bias visible.

## P227  candidate staffing and policy configurations -> multi-objective evaluation -> approved trade-off set

Primary family: F20.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P179 sensitivity pattern; P222 optimization; P225 simulation
new capability: OR-Tools plus SimPy scenario frontier evaluation

```
candidate configurations + hard controls + objective definitions
-> validate units and distinguish hard constraints from objectives (pydantic; P191 AST)
-> construct capacity-feasible plans for each candidate (OR-Tools CP-SAT)
-> evaluate declared operational scenarios (SimPy; fixed seed plan)
-> retain cost, lateness, backlog, and quality measures separately (pandas)
-> identify dominated candidates under the declared direction of each measure (explicit Pareto comparison; independent bounded oracle)
-> nondominated scenario set + trade-off explanations
```

contract: Do not mix dollars, hours, and error rates without an approved normalization or preference model. Hard controls cannot be traded away. A nondominated set is not a chosen operating policy.

theoretical positive test: A scenario that costs no more and has no worse lateness, with at least one strict improvement, dominates another under those two declared objectives.

theoretical adverse test: A cheaper plan that violates an approval control is infeasible, not a Pareto improvement. Changing objective weights without a recorded decision invalidates comparisons.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Feasible scenario comparison can expose explicit trade-offs and support a human configuration choice.

public-data limit: Public toy data cannot establish actual salaries, economic benefits, service preferences, or regulator tolerance.

required implementation and evidence: Implement objective definitions and feasibility gates; test dominated/equal/incomparable cases; preserve simulation uncertainty and solver status for each point; record selection authority separately.

## P228  late task or new requirement -> dependency-aware plan repair -> minimal-change schedule proposal

Primary family: F32.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P144 schedule variance; P186 observed progress; P212 impact; P222 schedule
new capability: OR-Tools repair objective with prov change lineage

```
baseline plan + explicit actual progress + approved new requirements
-> freeze only genuinely completed or committed actions (pydantic; evidence-bound progress adapter)
-> recompute affected dependencies and requirements (networkx; P212)
-> build a remaining-work scheduling model (OR-Tools intervals and precedence)
-> minimize a declared change objective after satisfying hard constraints (CP-SAT lexicographic stages or recorded weights)
-> independently check frozen work and new controls (z3; pandas joins)
-> repaired proposal + changed-task explanations
```

contract: Completed work is not rescheduled into the past. A previous prediction is not an actual. Minimal change is defined by an explicit metric and solver result, not asserted from appearance.

theoretical positive test: Delaying one unfinished predecessor changes only the tasks needed to restore feasibility under the chosen change objective. Completed task intervals remain unchanged.

theoretical adverse test: An added mandatory review may invalidate activation readiness even if the old schedule was feasible. A repair that moves a completed action or omits the new control fails.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT. Remaining-work optimization can propose a valid repair while preserving actual history.

public-data limit: No live project or operational replanning was executed. Unreported remaining effort remains unknown.

required implementation and evidence: Implement frozen-state semantics and change metric; test new dependencies, missing estimates, completed-task mutations, infeasibility, and objective ties; retain comparison to full recomputation.

## P229  uncertain service and demand parameters -> stress scenarios -> robust-capacity qualification

Primary family: F21.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P179 sensitivity; P225 simulation; P227 frontier
new capability: SimPy stress tests with OR-Tools feasibility under explicit uncertainty sets

```
candidate configuration + bounded uncertainty set + stress cases
-> validate ranges, units, and probability interpretation (pydantic; Fraction)
-> generate deterministic boundary and seeded sampled scenarios (itertools.product; numpy.random.Generator)
-> simulate each scenario and retain unfinished work (SimPy)
-> check critical resource/precedence constraints for required scenarios (OR-Tools; z3)
-> aggregate worst observed cases and sampling uncertainty (pandas; scipy statistics as specified)
-> stress report + coverage of uncertainty set
```

contract: Finite sampled scenarios do not establish worst-case robustness over a continuous space. Parameter ranges and correlations have separate provenance. Tail probabilities need enough evidence and an explicit estimator.

theoretical positive test: A finite two-by-two uncertainty grid evaluates all four declared scenarios and identifies the largest backlog under that exact grid.

theoretical adverse test: Reporting the largest sampled delay as a universal bound fails. Ignoring correlated surges or excluding unfinished cases invalidates the stated robustness claim.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for finite stress testing; GENERAL_ROBUSTNESS_UNPROVEN beyond the specified scenarios.

public-data limit: No empirically validated target uncertainty distribution, rare-event rate, or demand correlation was available.

required implementation and evidence: Implement explicit uncertainty-set coverage, seed recording, censoring, and conservative conclusions; independently check finite grids; document scenarios omitted for computational reasons.

## P230  approved normative process + independent event log -> conformance diagnostics -> actionable deviations

theoretical adverse test: Learning a model from the same log and reporting high fitness as regulatory compliance fails the acceptance contract. An unmapped event or unknown model check remains visible.
