## P231  event lifecycles and approved responsibility mappings -> interval accounting -> attributable and unknown time

Primary family: F17.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P12, P22, P58, P74, P210, P219
new capability: Hypothesis-generated interval mutations and PROV attribution over existing pandas/PM4Py events.

```
P210 normalized events + P219 ordering + approved responsibility map
-> pair start, suspend, resume, and complete events by explicit work-instance keys (pandas.merge; PM4Py event fields; custom lifecycle adapter)
-> intersect intervals with the case observation window and union overlaps per category (datetime; sorted endpoint sweep)
-> partition elapsed time into internal-only, client-only, parallel, and unattributed intervals (custom interval algebra; pandas.groupby)
-> separately sum resource work intervals without labeling them elapsed time (pandas.groupby; fractions.Fraction)
-> mutate boundaries, overlaps, and missing markers (Hypothesis strategies; independent discretized small oracle)
-> publish source-linked time components and coverage (prov.ProvDocument; json.dumps)
```

contract: Half-open intervals and one time zone govern elapsed accounting. Parallel categories are separate; unique elapsed time must conserve the observation window. Resource-hours may exceed elapsed hours. Missing lifecycle markers prevent invented start or completion times.

theoretical positive test: Authored case window [0,10), internal intervals [0,4) and [2,6), client interval [5,8): internal-only=5, client-only=2, parallel=1, unattributed=2. Resource interval sum is 8; elapsed internal union is 6.

theoretical adverse test: Drop the start marker or introduce two possible matching work instances: affected time becomes unresolved. Repeating an identical event ID cannot increase attributed time; a distinct real work instance must remain.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for exact interval accounting. Public log descriptions support event-based testing; a defensible internal/client split requires the extra approved mapping and usable lifecycle fields.

public-data limit: Public resource labels do not establish whether time belongs to the client, the firm, or a waiting queue. Full public files and their marker coverage remain uninspected here.

required implementation and evidence: Implement instance pairing, provenance per interval, clock contracts, partition conservation, censored-boundary tests, and independent small interval enumeration.

## P232  open and completed cases -> right-censored duration model -> qualified remaining-time estimates

Primary family: F18.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P21, P94, P211, P231
new capability: lifelines KaplanMeierFitter plus Hypothesis cohort and censoring tests.

```
case intake times + approved terminal outcome map + observation cutoff
-> construct durations and event_observed without turning open cases into completions (pandas; Pydantic strict types)
-> distinguish target completion, competing terminal events, and missing follow-up (custom cohort contract)
-> fit a declared right-censored cohort only when its censoring assumptions are defensible (lifelines.KaplanMeierFitter.fit)
-> derive conditional survival S(t+u)/S(t) where S(t)>0 and support is adequate (NumPy; explicit undefined states)
-> compare with an exact small risk-set product and test cutoffs (fractions.Fraction; Hypothesis)
-> record cohort, support, uncertainty, exclusions, and model version (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Completion, withdrawal, rejection, and right censoring have distinct meanings. Treating competing events as independent censoring requires justification; otherwise use a competing-risks extension. No tail extrapolation or finite median when the observed curve never crosses one half.

theoretical positive test: Authored durations [2,3,4] with event flags [true,false,true] produce survival 2/3 just after time 2, unchanged at censoring time 3, and zero after time 4 under the simple risk-set contract.

theoretical adverse test: An all-censored cohort cannot claim observed completion events or an observed median. A request beyond supported time, S(t)=0, or incomplete intake date produces unavailable rather than zero remaining time.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for qualified cohort estimates. This extends the retrospective clock proofs; transport to live onboarding and individual predictions needs separate validation.

public-data limit: The public event descriptions alone do not establish independent censoring, complete outcomes, stable cohorts, or the exact archive cutoff. They cannot certify forecast accuracy.

required implementation and evidence: Archive the log, reconcile actual cutoff, preregister cohorts/outcomes, test censoring and competing events, and backtest on later disjoint cohorts with coverage and errors.

## P233  explicit probabilistic model and observed evidence -> exact inference -> advisory diagnostic distributions

Primary family: F18.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P95, P199, P211
new capability: pgmpy DiscreteBayesianNetwork and VariableElimination with independent rational enumeration.

```
sealed model structure + conditional probability tables + typed observed evidence
-> validate state labels, DAG structure, table dimensions, and normalization (pgmpy.DiscreteBayesianNetwork.check_model; Pydantic)
-> retain missing observations as unobserved variables (custom evidence adapter)
-> calculate posterior distributions (pgmpy.inference.VariableElimination.query)
-> independently enumerate a small joint distribution (itertools.product; fractions.Fraction)
-> bind outputs to exact model and evidence versions (prov.ProvDocument; hashlib.sha256)
-> send advisory probabilities to a separate authorization gate (P199; Zen decision table)
```

contract: A posterior is conditional on the declared graph and parameters. It does not establish truth, legal status, wrongdoing, permission, or completed review. Impossible evidence with zero probability must be surfaced explicitly.

theoretical positive test: Authored two-state fixture: prior defect 1/10, signal sensitivity 4/5, false-positive rate 1/5. A positive signal gives posterior (4/50)/(13/50)=4/13. An absent signal observation leaves the prior 1/10.

theoretical adverse test: Non-normalized tables, unknown state labels, cycles, and zero-probability evidence are rejected or explicitly undefined. A 0.99 advisory score with absent approval cannot activate a case.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for model-based uncertainty handling. Exact inference can be checked against enumeration on small networks; meaningful operational probabilities need data and calibration.

public-data limit: The public teaching network supplies mathematical structure, not evidence about onboarding document defects or customers. No real-world diagnostic probability is estimated here.

required implementation and evidence: Archive public example parameters; test state ordering, table products, impossible evidence, model versioning, and strict separation of probabilistic and authorization outputs.

## P234  held-out labeled predictions -> calibration and selective review tests -> approved abstention policy

Primary family: F18.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P201, P233, P199
new capability: scikit-learn calibration_curve and scoring; Hypothesis threshold-boundary tests.

```
predictions + adjudicated labels + entity-group and time split register
-> keep training, calibration, threshold selection, and final test partitions separate (pandas; scikit-learn split utilities with group/time constraints)
-> compare score bins with observed frequencies and proper scores (sklearn.calibration.calibration_curve; sklearn.metrics.brier_score_loss)
-> compute error and coverage for an explicitly bounded abstention rule (NumPy; pandas.groupby)
-> evaluate subgroup support and confidence limits with case-level resampling (scipy.stats; NumPy random.Generator)
-> test exact cutoffs, missing labels, and unobserved classes (Hypothesis)
-> propose a review threshold requiring owner approval and a policy version (P199; prov)
```

contract: Brier loss combines several aspects of prediction quality and alone does not establish calibration. Linkage pairs sharing an entity must not leak across splits. Unlabeled examples cannot be counted as correct. Selective accuracy must be reported with retained coverage.

theoretical positive test: Authored predictions [0.2,0.8] and outcomes [0,1] have binary mean squared probability error 0.04. At a high-confidence threshold of 0.9, coverage is zero and selective accuracy is undefined.

theoretical adverse test: One true entity in both train and test is rejected by the split contract. An empty confidence bin, missing class, or tiny subgroup remains unsupported. Lower loss never automatically approves a production threshold.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for qualification of advisory scores and abstention. The key output is a supported error/coverage tradeoff with held-out evidence.

public-data limit: Synthetic linkage labels cannot establish document-extraction, sanctions-screening, or production reviewer accuracy. Pair sampling and prevalence affect apparent calibration.

required implementation and evidence: Implement case/entity grouping, frozen thresholds, independent score formulas, missing-label accounting, split hashes, and reviewer-approved deployment criteria.

## P235  proposed intervention and causal assumptions -> identification gate -> estimand or nonidentifiability report

Primary family: F18.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P46, P231, P233
new capability: DoWhy causal identification with explicit DAG assumptions and graph validation.

```
declared treatment + outcome + target population + causal assumption graph
-> validate names, temporal roles, and observed versus latent variables (Pydantic; networkx.is_directed_acyclic_graph)
-> construct the causal model (dowhy.CausalModel)
-> identify the effect under the recorded assumptions (CausalModel.identify_effect; noninteractive adapter with no silent confounding waiver)
-> inspect whether an admissible estimand exists before estimating (explicit identification-result contract)
-> check overlap and avoid post-treatment adjustment in the chosen design (pandas; custom variable-role checks)
-> produce an estimand with assumptions, or an unresolved identification record (prov; Jinja2)
```

contract: No automatic proceed-when-unidentifiable shortcut. Assumptions that cannot be established from the data stay explicit. A longest waiting-time edge is descriptive and does not establish the effect of automating that step.

theoretical positive test: Authored DAG Z->T, Z->Y, T->Y with observed pre-treatment Z admits a backdoor adjustment by Z under the graph assumptions. The output names the estimand and those assumptions before any estimate.

theoretical adverse test: Replace Z with an unobserved common cause and provide no valid instrument or frontdoor structure: the design must report the unsupported identification path rather than publish a causal benefit. Conditioning on a treatment-induced mediator changes the estimand and cannot be silent.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for identification discipline; PUBLIC_DATA_INSUFFICIENT to conclude real onboarding intervention benefits from the supplied public log descriptions.

public-data limit: Observed event order does not determine causal direction, absence of confounding, interference between cases, or the population targeted by a staffing intervention.

required implementation and evidence: Test identifiable and nonidentifiable graphs, observed-variable mapping, latent confounding, selection bias, and explicit assumptions without interactive runtime interpretation.

## P236  identified intervention model -> estimates and sensitivity -> conditional benefit evidence

Primary family: F18.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P235, P225, P227
new capability: DoWhy estimation and refutation composed with independent synthetic-effect checks.

```
P235 identified estimand + compatible data + estimator specification
-> fit only the estimator permitted by the identification contract (dowhy.CausalModel.estimate_effect)
-> preserve target population and uncertainty method (typed estimate adapter; pandas)
-> run placebo, added-common-cause, and declared sensitivity checks (CausalModel.refute_estimate; explicit supported methods)
-> compare simulated samples with their known generating effect using prespecified statistical acceptance (NumPy; scipy.stats)
-> translate a supported effect into a conditional operating scenario with all conversions exposed (P225; decimal.Decimal)
-> record estimate, uncertainty, refutation outcomes, and residual assumptions (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Passing refutation tests does not prove all causal assumptions. Finite-sample estimates need not equal the generating coefficient exactly. Deterministic pass/fail thresholds and seeds must be set before inspecting results.

theoretical positive test: On the public generator, beta=10 is the declared target for an average-effect experiment. A separate noise-free authored fixture Y=10*T+3*Z verifies an adjusted coefficient of 10 when the design matrix has full rank.

theoretical adverse test: An omitted confounder, absent overlap, post-treatment feature leakage, or effect interval spanning both benefit and harm blocks a definitive positive benefit claim. Random-placebo failure is retained, not rerun until a favorable seed appears.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for auditable scenario evidence. A controlled prospective evaluation would still be needed to support a claim about a new onboarding process.

public-data limit: The synthetic coefficient is a known mathematical input. It is unrelated to any private cycle-time or revenue objective. Public historical application data alone cannot prove the proposed intervention effect.

required implementation and evidence: Implement estimator-selection contracts, seed registry, refutation and sensitivity results, confidence-interval checks, repeated-sample coverage evaluation, and conversion provenance.

## P237  versioned case metrics -> streaming change detection -> review trigger without policy mutation

Primary family: F34.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P218, P219, P231, P234
new capability: River ADWIN with fixed metric definitions and a separate incident routing gate.

```
metric records + event-time order + lateness policy + baseline model version
-> validate metric ranges and retain missing observations (Pydantic; pandas)
-> freeze a replay order and record resets or policy changes (P219; custom checkpoint state)
-> update one named detector per approved metric and cohort (river.drift.ADWIN.update; drift_detected)
-> bind alarms to the precise input prefix, detector settings, and window (prov; JSON)
-> compare detection delay and false-alarm behavior on seeded stationary/change fixtures (NumPy; Hypothesis generated boundaries)
-> route an investigation without changing business policy or permissions (Zen; P199)
```

contract: A detected statistical change is a review signal. It does not establish its cause or authorize a rule change. Unobserved labels cannot be converted to zero errors. Missing metrics and changed definitions need their own status.

theoretical positive test: The public constructed stream provides a change-point experiment. A deterministic authored 0-to-1 stream must be evaluated for a documented detection range after the switch; the exact alarm index is an observed output of the pinned implementation, not guessed here.

theoretical adverse test: A late event cannot silently change an already published alarm. A metric-definition update must start a named version/reset. A stationary test may still have false alarms; report their rate rather than assert they are impossible.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for drift-aware review and monitored resolver maintenance. Alarm usefulness and false-alarm costs require domain evaluation.

public-data limit: The public synthetic stream supplies controlled changes. It cannot establish production drift prevalence, a fraud event, or any causal explanation of a timing shift.

required implementation and evidence: Archive settings and seeds; serialize/reconstruct detector state through validated replay; test label delay, duplicate events, cohort changes, and alert deduplication.

## P238  formal adapter contract and bounded grammar -> synthesized expression -> independent counterexample check

Primary family: F24.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P191, P197, P198
new capability: cvc5 syntax-guided synthesis, checked independently with Z3 and enumerated boundary oracles.

```
approved typed function specification + allowed grammar + input domain
-> encode a synthesis conjecture without external calls or mutable state (cvc5 Solver; mkGrammar; synthFun; addSygusConstraint)
-> request a candidate and retain solution, no-solution, or unknown status (Solver.checkSynth)
-> extract only grammar-permitted typed expressions (Solver.getSynthSolution; custom restricted AST adapter)
-> substitute the candidate into the independent specification and seek a counterexample (z3.Solver.check on domain AND NOT specification)
-> enumerate a finite witness domain and reject invalid input coercions (itertools; Pydantic; Hypothesis)
-> register the candidate as a reviewed versioned adapter, never self-deploy it (hashlib; P199; prov)
```

contract: The grammar and formal specification define the claim. A finite test gives a bounded result; an unsat counterexample query can prove the encoded property over its stated domain. Unknown is unverified. No arbitrary Python execution of generated text.

theoretical positive test: For integer n>=0 and a specification result iff n>=4, a candidate n>=4 must agree at n=0,3,4,5 and have no counterexample in the encoded domain. The candidate n>4 has witness n=4.

theoretical adverse test: An expression that calls an external function, changes the input domain, omits a missing-value route, or relies on solver unknown is rejected. A malformed normative translation remains malformed even when synthesis succeeds.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for small adapters and decision expressions. This is a bounded synthesis research track, not automatic production policy authoring.

public-data limit: The public rule provides a simple threshold only. It cannot prove synthesis of whole legal texts, arbitrary transformations, or the correct organizational interpretation of exceptions.

required implementation and evidence: Pin the cvc5/Z3 API versions, preserve grammar/specification/candidate hashes, prove adapter semantics, and test a deliberately erroneous candidate with a recorded counterexample.

## P239  approved requirements and engineering estimates -> dependency and budget constraints -> conditional roadmap

Primary family: F20.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P142-P148, P200, P212, P222, P227
new capability: OR-Tools constrained portfolio selection and PROV dependency/cost explanations.

```
approved requirement manifest + work-package graph + explicit estimates
-> bind each work package and dependency to its originating requirement (networkx; Pydantic; P200 source IDs)
-> check graph cycles, units, estimate completeness, and double-counted scope (networkx.is_directed_acyclic_graph; Decimal)
-> encode prerequisite closure, mandatory scope, capacity, and budget constraints (ortools.sat.python.cp_model.CpModel)
-> optimize an explicitly approved value objective or report feasibility only (CpSolver.solve; explicit status and bound)
-> independently check selection closure and exact cost totals (set operations; fractions.Fraction; Z3 small mirror)
-> publish selected, deferred, unknown-estimate, and infeasible scope with provenance (prov; Jinja2)
```

contract: Mandatory controls remain mandatory. A missing engineering estimate cannot be treated as zero cost. Deferring a prerequisite also defers dependent work unless an approved alternative exists. Selection is a proposal, not funding or delivery approval.

theoretical positive test: Authored packages A cost4 and B cost3 with B requiring A: budget7 permits {A,B}; budget6 cannot select B alone. A mandatory B with budget6 yields infeasibility under those fixed estimates.

theoretical adverse test: An unknown-cost prerequisite blocks a claim of budget feasibility. Overlapping work packages must not receive duplicate benefits. A time-limited feasible solution must retain its gap and cannot be called optimal.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for dependency-ordered engineering scope and explicit cost scenarios. Public structures can test mechanics but not validate the program roadmap or estimates.

public-data limit: Neither the control example nor the job-shop example contains private engineering costs, funding authority, actual capacity, or economic benefit estimates.

required implementation and evidence: Implement requirement-to-epic coverage, cost provenance, mandatory-scope controls, optimizer status tests, independent budget recomputation, and rejected-scope explanations.

## P240  measures and conditional effects -> nonduplicating benefit ledger -> evidence-qualified business case

Primary family: F14.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P147, P150, P187, P231, P232, P236, P239
new capability: PROV benefit lineage plus Hypothesis conservation and counterfactual-assumption tests.

```
observed baseline + conditional intervention effect + eligible population + valuation assumptions
-> align units, dates, cohorts, currencies, and uncertainty categories (Pydantic; pandas; Decimal)
-> assign each benefit component to exclusive scopes or a declared interaction model (networkx; explicit overlap sets)
-> calculate operational and monetary scenarios separately (Decimal; NumPy scenario arrays)
-> reconcile contributors and prevent double-counting direct and downstream effects (set unions; Hypothesis mutations)
-> preserve unsupported valuation, missing population, and unidentifiable effects as unknown totals (P235 status gate)
-> issue a source-linked business-case scenario with signoff and limitations (prov; Jinja2; P189-style acceptance gates)
```

contract: Elapsed time saved is not automatically labor cost saved or realized revenue. A common case population cannot be counted twice through two improvement initiatives. Uncertainty and causal assumptions accompany derived monetary values.

theoretical positive test: Authored scope fixture: initiative A reaches cases {1,2}, B reaches {2,3}; unique coverage is 3, not 4. A declared 2-hour saving for 3 distinct cases and rate50 per hour yields a conditional 300 currency-unit valuation, with all three assumptions identified.

theoretical adverse test: A missing conversion rate keeps monetary value unknown. A timing-only baseline cannot supply headcount savings. Adding an overlapping initiative cannot silently add the same case benefit again; an unidentifiable causal effect cannot become a realized return.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for an honest, reproducible scenario business case. PUBLIC_DATA_INSUFFICIENT to establish private cycle-time, revenue, or client-issue targets.

public-data limit: Public cross-domain examples establish arithmetic, lineage, and double-count protection. They do not demonstrate actual banking outcomes or cash realization.

required implementation and evidence: Implement dimensional checks, cohort reconciliation, interaction assumptions, independent exact valuation, unknown propagation, and evidence-bound approval of the stated business-case scope.

## P241  approved workflow and saved history -> deterministic replay -> durable case execution state

Primary family: F09.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P29, P199, P208, P220, P221
new capability: Temporal Python durable workflows with recorded activity results and replay tests.

```
approved process definition + typed case manifest + versioned activity interfaces
-> compile a deterministic workflow with explicit state transitions (temporalio.workflow.defn/run; Pydantic adapters)
-> perform external I/O only in declared activities with recorded results (temporalio.activity.defn; workflow.execute_activity)
-> accept authorized case events through typed handlers (workflow.signal/update; P199 gate)
-> persist workflow history and reconstruct the same decisions (Temporal service; temporalio.worker.Replayer.replay_workflow)
-> test timer advance, worker restart, activity failure, and history compatibility (temporalio.testing.WorkflowEnvironment; pytest)
-> expose current state, outstanding work, and evidence references (workflow.query; prov-linked records)
```

contract: Durable execution requires a service and workers. Workflow determinism, activity idempotency, business outcome verification, and authorization are separate obligations. Recorded success of an activity cannot establish a policy interpretation.

theoretical positive test: Adapt a public hello-activity example into an authored submit/review gate. A restart after recorded review must retain that decision and the same pending next action when the identical history is replayed.

theoretical adverse test: An unversioned workflow change that schedules a different command for the same history must fail replay qualification. A missing activity result or approval remains pending; a direct current-time or network read in workflow decision code is rejected by the design contract.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for long-lived case orchestration. The service deployment, selected SDK, and business adapters need actual integration and fault tests.

public-data limit: Public samples prove example API use only. This assessment did not start a Temporal service, archive its histories, or execute any workflow against a banking system.

required implementation and evidence: Build the approved state machine, isolate I/O activities, pin service/SDK versions, save history bytes, replay old and new histories, and verify all recorded side-effect receipts.

## P242  approved action intent and retries -> outbox, inbox, and receipt reconciliation -> duplicate-safe effects

Primary family: F13.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P199, P210, P241
new capability: Temporal retries plus transactional outbox/inbox adapters and Hypothesis fault sequences.

```
authorized action intent + stable semantic action ID + expected state version
-> commit intent and local state atomically (sqlite3.Connection transaction; UNIQUE action_id; transactional outbox)
-> dispatch with an idempotency key bound to destination, payload, and logical action (Temporal activity; fixed adapter)
-> reconcile timeout/unknown outcomes with the destination receipt contract before retry policy decides (typed status query; activity retry rules)
-> reject reused keys with different payloads and deduplicate incoming receipts (hashlib; transactional inbox)
-> inject crashes before commit, after send, after remote commit, and before acknowledgment (Hypothesis state machine; pytest service doubles)
-> publish committed, pending, duplicate, conflicting, and unknown outcomes separately (prov; JSON)
```

contract: A local unique key does not make a remote effect exactly-once. The destination must honor the key or offer a safe reconciliation protocol. No definitive completed status without an outcome receipt. Intent atomicity covers only its actual transaction boundary.

theoretical positive test: An authored destination stores receipts by action ID. A lost acknowledgment followed by the same intent must yield one destination effect and the same receipt. Duplicate inbound receipts leave one committed local transition.

theoretical adverse test: A provider without idempotency or outcome lookup returns unknown after a post-send timeout and routes to reconciliation. Reusing a key for altered account attributes is a conflict, never a cached success.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT when each destination supports the required protocol; PARTIAL_FIT where outcomes cannot be reconciled safely.

public-data limit: The public examples do not establish the guarantees of actual onboarding APIs or their retention period for idempotency keys. Local simulation alone cannot prove remote exactly-once behavior.

required implementation and evidence: Implement payload-bound keys, durable transaction tests, remote simulator fault injection, real provider contract verification, receipt retention, and duplicate/conflict accounting.

## P243  partially completed workflow -> declared compensation dependencies -> recoverable outcome

Primary family: F13.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P29, P196, P221, P241, P242
new capability: Temporal saga orchestration with explicit compensation preconditions and idempotency.

```
forward action contracts + registered compensation contracts + observed receipts
-> register recovery intent before potentially partial effects (custom saga register; Temporal workflow state)
-> execute the authorized forward activity and retain its actual receipt (P242; workflow.execute_activity)
-> on failure, select compensations whose preconditions and authority hold (Zen; P199; networkx dependency order)
-> invoke idempotent recovery activities and reconcile unknown results (Temporal activities; P242 receipt adapter)
-> test no-effect, partial-effect, duplicate compensation, and compensation-failure paths (Hypothesis; pytest)
-> preserve irreversibility, unresolved recovery, and manual interventions in the audit trail (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Compensation changes the current world according to a specific contract. It may not restore an earlier world exactly. The reverse of a workflow order is only valid when the compensation dependency model says so. An irreversible action needs an approved alternative recovery path.

theoretical positive test: Authored reserve/release fixture: a reservation followed by failure is released once; calling the release twice produces the same final state. A release after no reservation is a documented no-op.

theoretical adverse test: A document already delivered cannot be made undelivered. Its remedy is a separately authorized correction or withdrawal process. A failed compensation stays unresolved and cannot create a fully rolled-back flag.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for explicit recovery in distributed lifecycle execution. This strengthens the legacy reversed-step presentation into a separately verified recovery mechanism.

public-data limit: The samples do not describe actual legal remedies, account closure rights, or external cancellation guarantees. Those must be supplied by responsible owners.

required implementation and evidence: Define recovery-specific actions and authorizers; verify invariants under partial effects, compensation retries, and permanently failed destinations; retain durable unresolved-state evidence.

## P244  verified proposal and changing case state -> freshness fence -> authorized dispatch or stale rejection

Primary family: F13.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P199, P211, P214, P218, P242
new capability: Temporal update validators, compare-and-swap, and Z3 freshness invariants.

```
candidate action + evidence hash + policy version + observed case version
-> validate the submitted message without mutating workflow state (Temporal update validator; Pydantic)
-> reevaluate evidence freshness, withdrawal, actor authority, and policy at dispatch (P199; P211; fixed clock contract)
-> atomically bind intent to the checked local version (SQL UPDATE WHERE version=expected; outbox transaction)
-> pass a conditional-write token to destinations that support one (typed activity adapter; P242)
-> model and test approve/revoke/dispatch interleavings (Z3 bounded transition model; Hypothesis)
-> record accepted, stale, denied, and remote-fence-unavailable outcomes (prov; JSON)
```

contract: A preflight check alone cannot eliminate a check/use race. The dispatch fence must cover the actual mutation boundary. A local transaction cannot fence an independent remote service unless the protocol supports that contract.

theoretical positive test: Authored case version1 permits a review proposal. A withdrawal moves the case to version2 before dispatch; a compare-and-swap expecting version1 changes zero rows and creates no authorized activation intent.

theoretical adverse test: Concurrent approval and withdrawal without a defined order must not select whichever event arrives conveniently. If a destination lacks a required conditional mutation, flag the remaining race rather than claim atomic end-to-end authorization.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for version-fenced execution; unresolved external race conditions remain explicit deployment blockers.

public-data limit: Public handler documentation supplies API behavior, not authority or atomicity guarantees across the private system estate.

required implementation and evidence: Test all bounded interleavings, validator nonmutation, stale tokens, expiry boundaries, revoked evidence, provider conditional writes, and atomic outbox behavior.

## P245  historical event schemas and workflow versions -> explicit migrations -> compatible replay

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P95, P101, P208, P211, P241
new capability: Temporal versioned execution and Hypothesis schema-migration properties.

```
old event bytes + schema registry + migration functions + supported workflow histories
-> validate each payload using its recorded source schema (jsonschema.Draft202012Validator; Pydantic versioned models)
-> apply only named migration functions preserving source identity (custom deterministic upcasters; hashlib)
-> retain original bytes and attach new representations without overwriting history (PROV derivation; append-only store)
-> test semantic equivalence for the claimed subset and reject lossy unsupported states (Hypothesis; explicit oracle)
-> replay workflow histories against the approved versioning strategy (Temporal Replayer; documented workflow patch/version interfaces)
-> publish compatible, migrated, lossy, and unsupported cases separately (JSON; Jinja2)
```

contract: Field defaults have semantics. Missing old approval cannot become approved. A migration may be intentionally noninvertible, but then loss must be declared and original bytes retained. Data upcasting does not itself prove workflow replay compatibility.

theoretical positive test: Authored Boolean approval true/false/null becomes approved/rejected/unknown in schema2. A replay must preserve the same authorization decisions for these three historical cases.

theoretical adverse test: An old free-text status with no approved mapping is unsupported. Deleting an old workflow branch while histories still require its commands must fail replay qualification even when all payloads validate structurally.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for controlled evolution of long-lived cases. Each schema and workflow version pair needs explicit tests.

public-data limit: Public example histories do not cover the private status vocabulary or all future version combinations. Generic JSON compatibility is weaker than semantic compatibility.

required implementation and evidence: Build per-version schema fixtures, loss accounting, retained-byte hashes, migration decision oracles, and real SDK history replay across supported deployment versions.

## P246  engine-independent rule subset -> production-rule adapters -> replaceable decision backend

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P4, P55, P191, P197, P213
new capability: clipspy as a candidate production-rule backend alongside clingo, Zen, and legacy Experta.

```
approved restricted rule AST + explicit closed/open-world contract + ordered input events
-> compile the supported subset into typed CLIPS templates and rules (clips.Environment.build; template adapters)
-> assert input facts and run under declared agenda settings (Environment.assert_string or Template.assert_fact; Environment.run)
-> collect result facts with provenance and an explicit terminal condition (Environment.facts; bounded activation counter)
-> compare with a declarative or decision-table oracle for the same supported semantics (clingo; Zen; P197)
-> test retractions, duplicate facts, agenda ties, and rule ordering (Hypothesis state machine)
-> record parity coverage and unsupported constructs before a backend replacement (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Forward-chaining agenda semantics differ from answer-set and decision-table semantics. Replacement is limited to a declared common subset. A halted rule engine is not necessarily a completed fixpoint. Legacy snapshots remain attributable to their original engine.

theoretical positive test: A pure finite threshold rule without side effects produces the same required/not-required output across adapters on n=0..8. Permuting input assertions must preserve the result within this order-independent subset.

theoretical adverse test: Rules that depend on salience, side-effect order, nonmonotonic negation, or retraction behavior must fail parity qualification unless separately specified. A loop hitting the activation budget reports incomplete.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for a bounded replaceable backend. It does not support a blanket claim that clipspy, Experta, clingo, and Zen are interchangeable.

public-data limit: The public rule subset does not cover arbitrary expert systems, native callbacks, or the complete private policy corpus.

required implementation and evidence: Implement AST translations, agenda settings, termination status, event/retraction semantics, differential tests, and a rollback-safe shadow deployment with no live effects.

## P247  signed evidence envelope and trusted-key register -> cryptographic and authority checks -> bound approval evidence

Primary family: F06.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P17, P189, P199, P211
new capability: cryptography Ed25519 verification plus separate provenance and authority resolution.

```
exact envelope bytes + detached signature + key ID + versioned trust/authority registry
-> validate envelope schema, algorithm, purpose, scope, artifact hash, and nonce (Pydantic; explicit canonicalization contract)
-> verify the signature against the exact bytes and public key (Ed25519PublicKey.from_public_bytes; verify)
-> separately resolve the key to a trusted identity and authorized role (P199; P211 bitemporal authority records)
-> check validity, withdrawal, replay scope, and evidence freshness (fixed predicates; explicit clock source)
-> run public algorithm vectors plus altered-byte and wrong-scope tests (pytest; Hypothesis)
-> record cryptographic validity and business authorization as separate dimensions (prov; JSON)
```

contract: A valid signature establishes a relation to a key, not automatic trust in that key or legal authority. A claimed signing time is not an independently trusted timestamp. Do not confuse signing a digest envelope with Ed25519ph or substitute unapproved encodings.

theoretical positive test: The two selected RFC vectors must verify. An authored correctly signed envelope from a key whose role is approved for the exact scope can pass the combined gate only with all other evidence checks satisfied.

theoretical adverse test: An altered message or signature must fail verification. A valid signature from an unrecognized key, revoked role, wrong artifact, or replayed approval for another case cannot establish authorization.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for authenticated evidence bytes and separate authority checks. This adds a mechanism beyond the legacy fixture-only signoff records.

public-data limit: Public vectors authenticate no real person, organization, KYC decision, legal signature class, or trusted timestamp. Production trust management remains untested.

required implementation and evidence: Archive vector source selectors, implement a purpose-bound envelope, key rotation/revocation rules, exact-byte checks, invalid-signature handling, and real trust-store acceptance tests.

## P248  untrusted structured proposal -> typed verifier and permitted action map -> accepted intent or explicit exception

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P191, P194, P199, P200, P221, P238, P244
new capability: pySHACL, Z3, and Temporal intent dispatch composed behind a strict proposal boundary.

```
sealed upstream proposal + cited source identities + typed current case state
-> parse data only and reject executable text, unknown fields, and oversized input (Pydantic; jsonschema; fixed limits)

-> validate action preconditions, hard constraints, and supported semantic claims (Z3; approved rule AST; P199)
-> map the approved action ID to an allowlisted deterministic implementation (fixed dispatcher; no eval or exec)
-> recheck authority and freshness at the effect boundary (P244; Temporal activity intent)
-> record verifier result and route unsupported or conflicting proposals (prov; explicit exception states)
```

contract: The proposal may have been authored upstream by a person or model. The zero-LLM runtime mode never calls a model between functions. Verifier coverage is explicit; an uncoded domain judgment stays unresolved. A matching citation does not prove the proposal faithfully interprets it.

theoretical positive test: An authored request_review proposal citing the exact case and evidence is accepted into an intent queue when all declared gates hold. No review completion or activation is inferred from queue acceptance.

theoretical adverse test: Injected instructions, an unknown activate action, a fabricated citation, an unsupported exception, or a verifier timeout cannot dispatch an effect. Two agreeing solvers based on the same faulty translation are insufficient semantic assurance.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for bounded structured actions and claims covered by explicit validators. Open-ended language verification remains outside the certified subset.

public-data limit: Public samples cannot establish that every future proposal is correctly interpreted or every production action is safe. This assessment does not qualify a runtime agent loop.

## P249  sealed runtime bundle and dependency manifest -> isolated execution checks -> qualified execution boundary

Primary family: F26.

new capability: Hypothesis adversarial boundary cases plus external isolation attestation; Python hooks remain supplemental.

contract: Import hooks, audit hooks, and the Temporal workflow sandbox are not a complete hostile-code security boundary. Native code, subprocesses, credentials, and egress require external controls. A test run in this notebook cannot certify an untested production isolation policy.

theoretical positive test: Within a deliberately configured future isolated worker, an allowlisted local transform succeeds while a benign request to a denied test endpoint is blocked and logged by the external enforcement layer.

theoretical adverse test: A native subprocess that escapes Python hooks must still be denied externally. Missing isolation logs, unsealed dependencies, unbounded resource consumption, or missing credentials policy prevent a qualified boundary claim.

## P250  proof requirements and fresh scoped evidence -> promotion and gap ledger -> auditable open-ended qualification register

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P53, P89, P95, P148, P189, P191-P249
new capability: pySHACL and PROV evidence graph with explicit result-state propagation and generated negative tests.

-> validate every proof ID, function handoff, expected output, and input contract (Pydantic; markdown-it-py; networkx dependency graph)
-> bind fresh execution evidence to exact input, implementation, environment, and output hashes (hashlib; JUnit parsing; prov)
-> validate required evidence shapes and independent coverage inventory (RDFLib; pySHACL; P194)
-> aggregate states without converting specified, skipped, partial, or unknown into passed (explicit status algebra; P189-style gates)

-> issue a checkpoint register with qualified claims, gaps, open frontier, and reproducible source links (JSON; Jinja2; append-only TXT)
```

contract: A reference oracle does not execute its associated engine chain. Full-chain qualification requires every mandatory stage to have matching fresh evidence. Business acceptance is an additional scoped authorization decision. A checkpoint is a bounded delivery, not completion of the open-ended proof program.

theoretical positive test: An authored proof record with all mandatory stages actually passed and exact matching hashes may become technically qualified. A separately verified signoff may then satisfy acceptance for its stated scope.

theoretical adverse test: Replacing one required result with not_run blocks qualification even when a signoff exists. A report that counts an empty target set, uses stale source hashes, or silently drops missing-stage records is rejected.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for honest evidence promotion and ongoing proof expansion. In the current deliverable all P191-P250 full chains remain SPECIFIED_NOT_EXECUTED; only the separately reported bounded reference suite has actual execution evidence.

public-data limit: Public analogues cannot establish production policy correctness, proprietary data coverage, live integration guarantees, or private business benefits. Every unresolved dependency remains visible.

## P251  concurrent intakes and identity candidates -> purpose-bound consolidation -> linked request families

Primary family: F02.

new capability: Splink candidates + Temporal concurrency + PROV request lineage

contract: Identity equivalence, request duplication, and permission to share work are separate relations. Consolidation never discards a member request, legal-entity scope, deadline, or unresolved identity. Exactly-once local insertion requires a transactional unique key; remote effects still require reconciliation.

## P252  existing member and receiving-entity configuration -> scoped reuse and new obligations -> entity-add package

Primary family: F07.

```

contract: Every receiving-member requirement appears exactly once in the partition. A reused document can satisfy evidence needs only under explicit permission. Prior-case approval, signed-agreement parties, and activation state never transfer merely because client identity matches.

theoretical positive test: For an authored receiving manifest of five obligations, two eligible evidence items produce two reusable and three outstanding obligations. The prior member stays unchanged; the new member cannot activate until its three obligations and current approvals are evidenced.

## P253  direct ownership interests and declared summaries -> exact path accounting -> bounded look-through result

Primary family: F02.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P202 relationship limits; P208 object graph; P211 dated selection; P184 exact interval discipline
new capability: BODS adapter + NetworkX path accounting + rational Z3 bounds

```
versioned BODS statements + share-class and scope contract + evaluation cutoff
-> validate the pinned schema and resolve publisher/declaration-scoped record IDs (jsonschema; Pydantic)
-> separate primary indirect summaries from direct component interests and preserve dates (RDFLib; P211)
-> reject unsupported cycles and incompatible share classes before path aggregation (NetworkX; strongly_connected_components)
-> multiply exact direct shares and add only distinct additive holdings under the declared model (fractions.Fraction; NetworkX paths)
-> solve constrained share bounds where intervals or missing edges prevent an exact result (Z3; explicit unknown/timeout handling)
-> compare derived amounts with independently declared summaries and emit discrepancies with component lineage (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Compute economic share only for the declared acyclic, compatible, additive holdings model. Control rights and trust roles stay separate. An indirect summary and its components are alternate descriptions of one interest. A threshold is an approved input, never selected from a demonstration jurisdiction.

theoretical positive test: Authored direct interests P->A=1/2, A->T=3/5, and P->T=1/10 imply 2/5 economic share. An additional declared indirect summary of 3/10 is compared with the computed indirect share and is not added again.

theoretical adverse test: Adding that summary again would produce 7/10 and must fail. A cycle, incompatible voting/economic classes, overlapping duplicate holdings, or missing interest does not produce an asserted exact ownership result. An interval crossing a configured threshold remains unresolved.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for exact, bounded look-through arithmetic and declaration consistency. Control classification and policy conclusions require a separate approved rule layer.

public-data limit: BODS examples illustrate declarations. They do not verify the underlying interests or guarantee a complete ownership network. GLEIF accounting-parent data alone remains insufficient for this proof.

required implementation and evidence: Archive the BODS schema and examples; qualify the direct/component adapter; run rational and independent solver oracles; retain completeness assumptions and all excluded summary records.

## P254  trust and nominee structures -> typed party roles and unknowns -> role-specific evidence requirements

Primary family: F02.

contract: One person may hold several roles. A combined role does not create a second person or erase a control duty. Unknown, undisclosed, exempt-from-disclosure, and absent-from-data have different meanings. Representation of legal personality must come from an explicit source.

theoretical positive test: An authored person who is both settlor and trustee retains one identity and two role edges. A nominee and nominator remain distinct roles connected through the arrangement. Separate approved role rules create independently traceable evidence requirements.

## P255  dated ownership update and periodic review clock -> scoped re-entry -> review episode with retained history

Primary family: F07.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P211 bitemporal facts; P212 new-scope impact; P215 review clocks; P241 durable history
new capability: BODS updates + Temporal review episodes + PROV derivation lineage

```
dated statement updates + as-known case state + periodic clock + approved change triggers
-> identify affected entities and facts without rewriting earlier knowledge (P211; DuckDB interval selection)
-> distinguish no-change confirmation, substantive change, correction, and periodic due event (Pydantic; versioned predicates)
-> derive the requirements affected by the change, including newly applicable ones (P212; clingo; NetworkX)
-> create an idempotent review episode linked to the existing lifecycle (Temporal; immutable trigger key)
-> preserve unaffected evidence and reassess changed-scope approvals under explicit rules (Casbin; pySHACL; P205)
-> emit the episode, next-clock calculation, and retained historical decision basis (prov; dateutil; JSON)
```

contract: A periodic confirmation and a substantive change are distinct events. A changed ownership fact may trigger work before the next periodic date. Whether confirmation resets a clock is a versioned rule input. Earlier review completion remains historical and does not approve new facts.

theoretical positive test: An authored no-change confirmation preserves the requirement set. A later qualifying ownership update starts one change-review episode, links to the old episode, and reassesses only the explicitly affected requirements plus new-scope requirements.

theoretical adverse test: Replay the same update twice: one episode results. A late-recorded correction cannot change what was known at an earlier cutoff. Moving the periodic date must not suppress a separately due substantive-change trigger.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for one lifecycle with explicit review re-entry and preserved decision history.

public-data limit: The fictional ownership timeline supplies no institution-specific review cadence, materiality trigger, or permission to retain a prior approval.

required implementation and evidence: Run no-change, change, correction, and duplicate-event scenarios; qualify the durable workflow and as-known selection; prove no hidden overwrite or lost trigger across restarts.

## P256  offboarding instruction and dependencies -> controlled deactivation -> closure evidence and outstanding holds

Primary family: F07.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P216 safety; P221 planning; P243 compensation; P244 stale-state fencing
new capability: SCIM lifecycle analogue + Unified Planning + Temporal + temporal safety checks

```
authorized offboarding instruction + current resource inventory + obligations and retention holds
-> validate case, account, receiving system, and instruction authority at the current version (Pydantic; Casbin; P244)
-> derive reversible restrictions, permitted deactivations, and blocked operations (clingo; Unified Planning)
-> execute only approved operations with durable per-resource receipts (Temporal; outbox/inbox; P242)
-> read back resource state and reconcile partial failures or already-absent resources (SCIM-profile adapter; explicit coverage)
-> verify that audit/evidence retention obligations survive operational deactivation (pySHACL; pyModelChecking)
-> report operational closure separately from legal, settlement, and retention completion (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Deleting a technical identity resource does not discharge business obligations. A 404 can mean absent or unavailable under the service's semantics; it needs contextual reconciliation. Retention rules are explicit inputs. Historical evidence is never deleted as a convenience of closing an operational case.

theoretical positive test: An authored three-resource offboard operation deactivates two eligible resources, retains one on an explicit hold, and reports partial closure. The case remains incomplete until every required closure condition has matching evidence.

theoretical adverse test: One 204 response cannot close all three resources. A resource belonging to another tenant, a stale authorization, an open mandatory dependency, or an active retention hold blocks its prohibited operation.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for mechanically controlled offboarding and honest partial completion.

public-data limit: SCIM examples test identity-resource operations. They do not demonstrate settlement, financial account closure, record-retention law, or the semantics of proprietary downstream systems.

required implementation and evidence: Qualify each provider's observed delete/deactivate/readback behavior in a test environment, formalize closure criteria, test holds and restarts, and retain per-operation evidence.

## P257  configuration operations and provider receipts -> object-level readiness barrier -> qualified activation state

Primary family: F07.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P199 evidence gates; P207 interfaces; P210 event scope; P242 retries; P244 commit fence
new capability: OpenAPI contract tests + Schemathesis + Temporal + SHACL readiness barrier

```
configuration manifest + permitted operations + individual provider receipts + current approvals
-> validate endpoint, operation, tenant, object ID, request version, and response schema (Pydantic; local OpenAPI profile)
-> exercise request and response edge cases against an offline test application (Schemathesis.openapi.from_asgi; pytest; custom invariants)
-> join every response to its exact requested operation, preserving missing and duplicate responses (pandas merge validation; P210)
-> reduce per-operation outcomes separately from the enclosing HTTP transport status (explicit SCIM-profile adapter)
-> require all mandatory verified configurations and current approvals at the barrier (pySHACL; Casbin; Z3; P244)
-> publish a versioned readiness assertion with expiring validity and failure explanations (Temporal; prov; JSON)
```

contract: Readiness is a conjunction over the manifest's required operations, plus current authority and evidence. Empty or incomplete manifests cannot pass. Transport success, accepted work, and verified business configuration remain distinct. Readiness may be invalidated by later changes.

theoretical positive test: An authored two-operation manifest with exact matching success receipts and current approvals satisfies the modeled barrier. The public SCIM all-failed bulk example yields failed operations despite its enclosing 200 response.

theoretical adverse test: A 200 response with one failed operation, a missing second receipt, a wrong-object receipt, or a stale version cannot produce ready status. A configuration change after the assertion invalidates or requires revalidation of that assertion.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for a measurable ready-to-trade exit contract once the actual required configurations and provider guarantees are supplied.

public-data limit: OpenAPI and SCIM do not define financial trading readiness. Public examples cannot verify actual booking flags, account profiles, agreements, or production observability.

required implementation and evidence: Build the typed operation adapter and offline API harness, run per-operation negative tests, qualify provider readback and conditional updates, and define owner-approved readiness validity rules.

## P258  product and jurisdiction request -> eligibility and duty evaluation -> scoped intake decision

Primary family: F07.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P192 applicability; P193 contradictions; P198 coverage; P200 resolver; P206 authority
new capability: Zen/clingo/Z3 parity over separate eligibility, prohibition, and obligation dimensions

```
typed intake dimensions + effective rule set + relevant evidence + permitted evaluator role
-> validate product, market, receiving entity, client class, and evidence state (Pydantic; pySHACL)
-> evaluate scope without coercing missing attributes to a default class (clingo; P192)
-> derive allowed actions, prohibitions, required evidence, and unresolved conflicts in distinct fields (Zen; Clorm; P199)
-> search the declared finite configuration space for overlaps and uncovered assignments (Z3; explicit domain constraints)
-> cross-check supported decisions with an independently authored truth-table oracle (Hypothesis; itertools.product)
-> issue a reasoned intake decision and the requirements needed before any activation (prov; Jinja2; P200)
```

contract: Eligibility to request a product, duty to collect evidence, and permission to activate are different predicates. A specific permissive rule does not automatically override a prohibition. Rule precedence must be explicit, scoped, versioned, and approved.

theoretical positive test: In an authored model, a qualified client in market A can submit product X, yet activation remains blocked until evidence and approval requirements pass. An otherwise identical market-B request receives its independently configured rule outcome.

theoretical adverse test: An unknown jurisdiction remains unresolved. Simultaneous applicable permit/prohibit rules without an approved precedence produce a conflict. Passing an evidence checklist alone cannot establish product eligibility.

assessment: MECHANICAL_FIT_ONLY from public analogues. The chain is a plausible requirements target; substantive financial eligibility is blocked pending approved rules and domain evidence.

public-data limit: No public example selected here states the private program's trading eligibility rules. The fixture must never be represented as a legal or compliance determination.

required implementation and evidence: Obtain approved applicability and precedence records, define the supported rule subset, execute finite-domain and mutation tests, and validate at least one authorized real intake per supported scope.

## P259  agreement versions and structured terms -> party- and version-bound checks -> negotiation and execution register

Primary family: F08.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P120 content comparison; P176 draft order discipline; P185 structured terms; P204 manifests; P247 signed bytes
new capability: Lark term schema + PROV version lineage + signature and authority gate composition

```
agreement manifest + archived draft/executed versions + typed terms + approved execution records
-> preserve source bytes and compare exact versions without treating textual similarity as legal equivalence (hashlib; difflib; P120)
-> parse only supported controlled term forms, preserving party, entity, product, and exception scope (Lark; Pydantic)
-> resolve amendment lineage and the effective term set with explicit supersession rules (NetworkX; P211; RDFLib)
-> verify signature bytes separately from signer authority, required parties, and matching agreement scope (cryptography; Casbin; P247)
-> validate required clauses and execution evidence against the original manifest (pySHACL; P204)
-> export draft, negotiation, execution, expired, and unresolved states without changing the source agreement (prov; Jinja2; JSON)
```

contract: A draft, a metadata field saying signed, a valid cryptographic signature, and an enforceable executed agreement are distinct. This proof checks recorded evidence under the declared contract; it does not make an enforceability determination. Arbitrary natural-language clauses stay unresolved.

theoretical positive test: An authored amendment signed by the required authorized parties changes only its named term and scope. Both earlier and revised versions remain traceable. A declared missing signature keeps execution incomplete.

theoretical adverse test: A signature over the prior draft cannot authenticate changed bytes. An executed agreement for another receiving party cannot satisfy this manifest. An unsupported negated exception is routed for review rather than silently simplified.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for version- and party-bound document controls and honest negotiation status.

public-data limit: OCDS public records cannot authenticate the private agreement or establish the signers' authority. Public metadata cannot substitute for the executed document and trust evidence.

required implementation and evidence: Archive the permitted agreement test corpus, approve the term schema, implement amendment and signature bindings, and test changed bytes, wrong parties, missing exceptions, and revoked authority.

## P260  member configurations and shared controls -> constrained commonality analysis -> variation and lead-owner register

Primary family: F37.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P49 shared actions limits; P146 explicit RACI; P192 scopes; P198 coverage; P238 constrained generation
new capability: Z3 valid-configuration enumeration + clingo commonality + SHACL ownership coverage

```
base lifecycle + member configurations + variation definitions + explicit lead-owner rule
-> validate each dimension and reject undeclared or impossible combinations (Pydantic; Z3)
-> derive complete requirements for every admitted configuration in the bounded test domain (clingo; P200)
-> compute shared and variant obligations by semantic rule identity and scope, preserving exceptions (set operations; RDFLib)
-> assign one lead owner only where the explicit ownership rule has a unique authorized result (Zen; Casbin; pySHACL)
-> test boundary combinations and cross-member invariants against exhaustive small-domain enumeration (Hypothesis; itertools.product)
-> export commonality, legitimate variants, conflicts, and unresolved ownership with source lineage (prov; JSON; Jinja2)
```

contract: A shared action word does not establish a shared control. A shared lifecycle does not force all member configurations to have identical requirements. Invalid combinations are counted separately from uncovered valid combinations. A leading owner coordinates work; the choice does not transfer legal authority.

theoretical positive test: In an authored domain, two members share identity verification and diverge on one agreement and one structure-specific review. The register preserves the two common obligations and both scoped variants. A unique approved lead rule selects the coordinator.

theoretical adverse test: Two equally eligible leads produce an unresolved owner rather than a lexicographic winner. Removing a scope dimension cannot collapse conflicting requirements into a false common control. An impossible member/product pair is rejected before evaluation.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for a single lifecycle with explicit configurations and a source-bound commonality statement.

public-data limit: Public profiles and structure codes do not establish equivalence between private divisional policies or approve a lead-division rule. The full valid configuration domain must come from the program.

required implementation and evidence: Implement semantic control keys, enumerate the bounded qualification domain, retain invalid/uncovered counts, verify owner authority separately, and publish the remaining untested dimensions.

## P261  versioned source schemas -> dialect-specific validation -> explicit migration and discrepancy register

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P95 typed schemas; P195 source diffs; P245 upcasters; P253 BODS adapter
new capability: JSON Schema dialect dispatch + pinned referencing registry + Hypothesis migration counterexamples

```
source payload + declared format version + archived schema and meta-schema + approved migration
-> resolve only the exact registered schema dialect and version (referencing.Registry; explicit version map)
-> validate the schema against its own meta-schema before using it (jsonschema.Draft4Validator.check_schema; Draft202012Validator.check_schema)
-> validate payload structure without silently filling missing version or renamed fields (Pydantic; jsonschema)
-> execute only the explicit version-to-version transformation and retain old bytes (versioned adapter; P245; hashlib)
-> compare preserved meaning and required-field coverage with independent cases (Hypothesis; Lark AST comparisons; pySHACL)
-> emit migrated records, original pointers, and documentation/schema discrepancies for adjudication (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Unknown versions block processing. Identically named keywords can have different dialect semantics. BODS instance share fields must not be confused with JSON Schema validation keywords. Published examples and prose do not override a contradictory pinned schema without an explicit reviewed resolution.

theoretical positive test: The authored Draft 4 numeric schema minimum=25 with exclusiveMinimum=true and the 2020-12 schema exclusiveMinimum=25 both reject 25 and accept 26 under their own validators. A declared 0.4 relationship resolves recordId references using its 0.4 contract.

theoretical adverse test: Feeding Boolean exclusiveMinimum to the 2020-12 meta-schema, or numeric exclusiveMinimum to the Draft 4 meta-schema, fails. Removing the source version or mixing legacy and new relationship-reference names blocks migration.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for safe schema evolution and preserved source meaning. A field-renaming script alone cannot qualify a semantic migration.

public-data limit: The full public BODS schemas and repositories were not executed in the bounded suite. The numeric examples test dialect behavior only; full data-model conformance remains open.

required implementation and evidence: Archive schema dependencies, implement the approved mapping, run complete publisher examples plus malformed variants, and preserve discrepancies instead of silently correcting them.

## P262  typed decision envelopes -> strict canonical bytes -> interoperable signatures and semantic fingerprints

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P17 hashes; P51 canonical packages; P195 Unicode mapping; P247 signatures
new capability: rfc8785 canonicalization + strict JSON decoding + cross-implementation test vectors

```
raw decision JSON + versioned numeric/string profile + canonicalization standard identifier
-> reject duplicate keys, nonfinite extensions, invalid Unicode, and unsupported input types before information loss (json object_pairs_hook; strict typed decoder)
-> encode exact decimal/rational fields by the declared schema without silent binary-float conversion (Decimal; Fraction; Pydantic)
-> canonicalize only supported values with the selected standard implementation (rfc8785.dumps; explicit domain-error handling)
-> compute separate source-byte and canonical-envelope hashes (hashlib.sha256; P17)
-> sign and verify the canonical envelope while retaining scope and authority checks (cryptography; P247; Casbin)
-> compare standard vectors and boundary cases with an independent implementation (pytest; Hypothesis; provenance manifest)
```

contract: Sorted keys from json.dumps do not establish RFC 8785 conformance. Unicode normalization is a separate transformation and cannot silently change signed strings. Decimal strings need an explicit scale/value contract; source-byte identity and semantic-equivalence claims are separate.

theoretical positive test: Equivalent property orderings within the supported profile produce identical canonical bytes. RFC vectors must agree byte for byte across implementations. An exact decimal represented by the approved schema remains exact after roundtrip and signature verification.

theoretical adverse test: Duplicate allow keys are rejected before last-value-wins parsing. Unsupported large integers, NaN, unpaired surrogates, and forbidden numeric coercions fail. A Unicode ordering counterexample must expose a naive code-point sorter that differs from the required ordering.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for portable decision-envelope signatures and explicitly defined fingerprints.

public-data limit: The current bounded tests exercise a restricted local JSON profile and a Unicode-order counterexample. They do not run rfc8785 or establish cross-language interoperability.

required implementation and evidence: Pin both independent implementations, archive public vectors, define exact numeric encodings, and run invalid-input and signature-binding tests before adopting a canonical envelope contract.

## P263  append-only event commitments -> inclusion and consistency checks -> auditable checkpoint with coverage limits

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P53 append ledger; P210 event completeness; P220 replay; P247 signed checkpoints
new capability: pymerkle inclusion/consistency + signed size/root checkpoints + explicit ingestion reconciliation

```
ordered immutable event bytes + trusted prior checkpoint + new signed checkpoint + receipt inventory
-> validate event identities, partition sequence, and explicit source coverage (Pydantic; P210)
-> commit canonical event leaves and produce the declared tree state (pymerkle append_entry/get_state; P262)
-> verify individual inclusion and append consistency against independently trusted roots and tree sizes (pymerkle.verify_inclusion; verify_consistency)
-> verify checkpoint signature, authority, age, and expected log identity separately (cryptography; Casbin; P247)
-> reconcile accepted source receipts to included events and report missing or late partitions (DuckDB; anti-joins; P219)
-> publish the verified commitment, coverage gaps, and equivocation or truncation evidence (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Inclusion proves membership in a particular committed tree. It does not prove that every required event was ingested. A self-consistent old prefix needs a trusted newer checkpoint to expose tail truncation. A valid signature does not prevent a publisher from signing conflicting histories. The library needs security review before production use.

theoretical positive test: An authored checkpoint growing from three to five leaves verifies consistency, and each required receipt has a matching included leaf. Separate root/size checks identify exactly which checkpoint was verified.

theoretical adverse test: Changed leaf bytes, reordered events, invalid sizes, incompatible old/new roots, or a stale checkpoint fail their respective gates. A receipt absent from the tree keeps ingestion coverage incomplete even when all supplied inclusion proofs pass.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for stronger audit commitments, subject to independent security review and a trusted checkpoint distribution design.

public-data limit: The current bounded tests use a simple local hash-chain and sequence oracle. They do not execute pymerkle, establish Byzantine resistance, or prove receipt completeness for a live provider.

required implementation and evidence: Qualify pymerkle against independent vectors and review its security properties. Specify checkpoint trust, partition sequencing, retained receipts, consistency proofs, and conflict reporting.

## P264  authorized source views -> field and aggregate filtering -> nonleaking presentation contract

Primary family: F06.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P67 typed interface; P75 filters; P206 authorization; P207 sharing; P248 untrusted inputs
new capability: Casbin field/scope policy + Hypothesis paired-world tests + source-linked redaction

```
audience identity + purpose + scoped access policy + full internal model
-> authorize records, fields, relationship edges, and allowed aggregates before presentation (Casbin; explicit policy predicates)
-> construct a separate audience-safe view rather than masking a full embedded payload (Pydantic; controlled projection)
-> recompute counts, facets, pagination, errors, and source-link visibility from the authorized view (DuckDB; Jinja2; existing interface handlers)
-> test paired inputs that differ only in unauthorized information (Hypothesis; byte/model equality under the declared observer)
-> check exports, embedded JSON, diagnostics, and provenance links against the same audience policy (pySHACL; source inventory)
-> produce a view manifest and observed noninterference counterexamples (prov; JSON)
```

contract: An authorized row can still contain forbidden fields. Hidden HTML, chart caches, export payloads, global counts, and source URLs are part of the observable output. Functional noninterference covers the declared output interface; timing and resource side channels need separate analysis.

theoretical positive test: Changing or adding unauthorized records leaves the authorized output rows, count, pagination, and links unchanged. The internal evidence store retains complete provenance while the external view contains only permitted references.

theoretical adverse test: A global total that changes when hidden records are added fails. Embedding the full model behind a hidden panel fails. An error revealing an unauthorized case ID or a redacted source link is a disclosure defect.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for controlled sharing and presentation across audience boundaries.

public-data limit: Public protocol rules do not define the private program's field-level entitlements or permitted aggregates. The bounded paired-world tests do not qualify all side channels or a browser deployment.

required implementation and evidence: Approve an audience/purpose policy, inventory every observable surface, implement one view constructor, and run paired-world tests across text, HTML, JSON, exports, errors, and source references.

## P265  source and schema dependencies -> sealed offline registry -> bounded resolution without hidden retrieval

Primary family: F26.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P61 references; P71 guards; P196 closure; P249 external isolation
new capability: referencing.Registry + pinned RDF/OSCAL dependencies + controlled acquisition manifest

```
approved source manifest + archived bytes + transitive schema/context/import references
-> enumerate dependency references with origin and expected content identity (Lark or format parser; NetworkX; P196)
-> acquire permitted dependencies in a separately authorized phase with size, depth, redirect, and media-type limits (acquisition adapter; explicit allowlist)
-> validate hashes and register immutable local resources with their exact dialects (hashlib; referencing.Registry.with_resource)
-> resolve only registered references during the offline computation (jsonschema; configured RDFLib/OSCAL adapters; rejecting retrieval callback)
-> reject unresolved, cyclic unsupported, oversized, redirected, or conflicting dependencies before decision evaluation (Pydantic; Hypothesis)
-> record closure completeness and enforce independent operating-system egress restrictions (external sandbox; P249; prov)
```

contract: Declared remote references are data. They do not authorize network access. Retrieval failure cannot substitute an empty schema, default context, or permissive rule. Hash checking after an unrestricted request does not prevent an unsafe request. Every parser and native subprocess belongs in the isolation boundary.

theoretical positive test: An archived schema reference resolves from the in-memory registry and produces the declared validation result with no retrieval callback. A complete allowlisted profile closure supports offline replay from identical source bytes.

theoretical adverse test: An unregistered remote $ref, external JSON-LD context, unexpected redirect, excessive nesting, or changed content hash blocks the dependent proof. A permitted parent resource does not automatically permit arbitrary child URLs.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for explicit source dependency closure and reproducible offline evaluation.

public-data limit: Actual source acquisition into this runtime failed. The bounded check verifies an in-memory JSON Schema registry and a rejecting callback only. It does not qualify every RDF, XML, native, or subprocess path.

required implementation and evidence: Implement the acquisition boundary and all parser resolvers, archive test resources, verify process-level egress denial, and fault-test missing dependencies without silently broadening the allowlist.

## P266  API specification and stateful cases -> contract and invariant tests -> adapter qualification evidence

Primary family: F25.

reuses: P207 typed flows; P244 version gates; P257 readiness; P265 offline dependencies

contract: Schema validity does not establish correct state transitions, authorization, or business effects.
