## P267  rule compiler and tests -> semantic mutants -> counterexample-driven assurance gaps

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P191 grammar; P197 independent parity; P199 gates; P238 synthesis; P250 qualification
new capability: mutmut + Hypothesis + hand-specified semantic mutation operators

```
compiler/verifier implementation + independent acceptance tests + explicit mutation catalogue
-> run the unmodified baseline and record all required tests (pytest; P148/P250-style evidence)
-> generate bounded code mutations and separate domain-semantic mutations (mutmut; authored AST operators)
-> execute mutations in isolated copies with fixed time/resource budgets (pytest; external sandbox)
-> classify killed, survived, timed_out, invalid, equivalent-under-declared-domain, and untested mutations separately (Pydantic; explicit result algebra)
-> minimize surviving counterexamples without changing the independent requirement oracle (Hypothesis; Z3 bounded search)
-> append assurance gaps and new regression requirements with full source lineage (prov; JSON)
```

contract: A mutation score is conditional on the selected operator set and test domain. Timeouts are not killed mutants. Equivalent mutants need a justified domain-specific classification. Shared compiler-generated expectations cannot serve as the sole independent oracle.

theoretical positive test: An authored >=25 rule has a boundary test at 25 that kills a >25 mutation. A two-prerequisite gate has a one-present/one-missing test that kills an AND-to-OR mutation. Correct baseline behavior still passes.

theoretical adverse test: A negation-removal, scope-widening, wrong-case evidence join, or ignored-version mutant that survives must remain an assurance gap. A skipped test cannot count as a killed mutant. Mutating both implementation and expected output together is rejected as circular evidence.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for discovering weaknesses in otherwise passing compiler and verifier tests.

public-data limit: The bounded suite executes two hand-authored mutant comparisons only. No automatic mutmut campaign or broad semantic mutation coverage has been established.

required implementation and evidence: Implement a mutation catalogue tied to failure modes, run isolated baseline and mutant tests, define timeout accounting, and add independently reviewed counterexamples for survivors.

## P268  qualified implementations and fixed workloads -> accuracy-gated measurements -> reproducible scaling envelope

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P145 capacity limits; P229 stress scenarios; P237 drift; P250 evidence gate
new capability: pyperf multi-run benchmarks + exact workload lineage + explicit timeout/correctness accounting

```
technically qualified adapter versions + archived public workloads + declared hardware and run budgets
-> validate common task semantics, workload identities, and correctness oracles before comparison (P197/P246 parity; Pydantic)
-> separate load, compile, cold execution, warm execution, serialization, and verification phases (pyperf.Runner; explicit stage boundaries)
-> run replicated measurements with recorded environment, seeds, thread counts, and resource limits (pyperf; process metadata)
-> retain wrong results, errors, timeouts, and missing observations in the complete denominator (DuckDB; explicit status fields)
-> compare distributions and solver quality/bounds only for matched problem instances and semantics (NumPy; SciPy; paired identifiers)
-> publish observed performance limits and untested scales without claiming production service levels (JSON; Jinja2; prov)
```

contract: Fast incorrect output is a failure. Solver feasible, optimal, and timed-out results cannot be compared as equivalent outcomes. Means conditional on success must carry the success denominator. Unit, concurrency, hardware, and cold/warm differences remain visible. Microbenchmarks do not measure client cycle time.

theoretical positive test: For authored measurement records of two completed runs at 10 and 20 ms and one timeout, report three attempts, two completions, one timeout, and 15 ms conditional mean. A real qualification run must reproduce the corresponding measurement contract.

theoretical adverse test: Mixed seconds/milliseconds, excluded timeouts, changed workload semantics, an unverified solution, or missing hardware metadata block a headline comparison. An all-timeout cohort cannot report zero latency.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for reproducible engineering performance evidence after correctness qualification.

public-data limit: The numeric reference check uses authored measurement rows. It establishes accounting mechanics only. No pyperf benchmark, scalability curve, infrastructure capacity, or private SLA has been measured.

required implementation and evidence: Archive workloads, qualify correctness first, pin runner and hardware settings, collect repeated measurements with all outcomes, and retain raw runs plus uncertainty and selection limits.

## P269  sealed checkpoint and event suffix -> verified restore and reconciliation -> recoverable lifecycle state

Primary family: F33.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P220 exact replay; P241 workflow histories; P242 external effects; P245 migration; P263 checkpoints
new capability: Temporal replay + database snapshot recovery + source-receipt reconciliation

```
trusted checkpoint + versioned event suffix + workflow history + external effect receipts
-> verify snapshot hash, schema version, policy version, event position, and checkpoint authority (hashlib; P247; P261; P263)
-> restore the database and workflow history using the qualified version/migration path (SQLite or production database restore; Temporal replay)
-> replay the complete suffix with immutable event IDs, detecting overlap, gaps, and conflicts (P210; P242; explicit replay reducer)
-> reconcile already-executed remote effects before retrying outstanding work (outbox/inbox; provider receipt adapter)
-> compare restored state and as-known decisions with uninterrupted reference execution (independent fold; pySHACL; Hypothesis)
-> record actual recovery gaps and observed restoration evidence (prov; pytest JUnit; JSON)
```

contract: A valid snapshot hash proves only snapshot bytes. Completeness requires the full needed event suffix and trusted position. Local replay must not duplicate remote business effects. A restore cannot claim a recovery point or recovery time unless measured with explicit clocks and loss accounting.

theoretical positive test: An authored snapshot after events 1-2 plus an overlapping replay of events 2-4 produces the same four-event state as uninterrupted execution. Existing event 2 is retained once and matching remote receipts suppress duplicate dispatch.

theoretical adverse test: A modified snapshot, missing event 3, reused event ID with changed content, incompatible upcaster, or an unconfirmed remote effect prevents a fully reconciled recovery claim. A local successful restore does not prove remote-state recovery.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for evidence-backed recovery of the composed lifecycle.

public-data limit: The bounded suite tests SQLite memory serialization/deserialization and deterministic replay only. No distributed outage, Temporal restoration, production backup, remote effect, recovery time, or recovery-point target was tested.

required implementation and evidence: Run isolated kill/restart and storage-loss scenarios, archive service histories and receipt inventories, validate schema migrations, and compare full state plus external effects to an uninterrupted control run.

## P270  policy change during entity-add -> invalidated gates and scoped rework -> newly verified readiness

Primary family: F32.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P200 resolver; P212 impact; P244 stale-state fence; P252 entity-add; P253 look-through; P257 readiness; P259 agreements
new capability: End-to-end clingo/Z3/SHACL/Temporal composition with independent finite-state oracle

```
active entity-add episode + version-1 policy and evidence + newly effective policy or ownership event
-> preserve the earlier decision basis and select newly applicable rules at the declared cutoff (P211; P212; clingo)
-> derive the entire new requirement manifest and its delta, including newly affected cases (P200; Z3 scope witness)
-> invalidate only the dependent current approvals/readiness assertions, preserving historical records (pySHACL; prov; P244)
-> plan and execute authorized outstanding work with stable episode and effect identities (Unified Planning; Temporal; P242/P252)
-> reconcile exact agreement, ownership, evidence, and configuration versions at the final barrier (P253; P257; P259; Casbin)
-> compare interleavings and restart variants with an independent bounded state machine (Hypothesis; pyModelChecking; pytest)
-> emit current readiness, retained old basis, and remaining unresolved dependencies (JSON; Jinja2; prov)
```

contract: An old approval does not approve a new policy or materially changed evidence set. A completed document task does not prove configuration. Rework scope derives from semantic dependencies and newly applicable conditions, not just previous output links. Historical approvals remain historical records even when current readiness is invalidated.

theoretical positive test: An authored version-1 case satisfies its identity requirement. Version 2 adds ownership review. The case becomes blocked; completing the added evidence still leaves the old approval stale. A new scope-matched approval plus all verified configurations produces version-2 readiness.

theoretical adverse test: Deliver the old approval late, replay the change twice, complete the wrong entity's document, omit one configuration receipt, or crash after a remote effect. Every ordering must preserve the same safety invariant: no current activation without the current manifest, evidence, authority, and verified configuration.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for the central evolving-onboarding use case as one composed system. The theoretical chain is coherent under the stated contracts; implementation, data completeness, authority, and all engine-boundary tests remain required.

public-data limit: No selected public dataset joins real bank policy changes, ownership, agreements, approvals, and configuration receipts for the same case. Public sources support separate mechanics. End-to-end domain validity needs an authorized, owner-approved case pack.

required implementation and evidence: Build all typed adapters, qualify the complete isolated chain, enumerate a bounded interleaving domain with explicit fairness, run restarts and negative tests, and bind each stage to fresh execution evidence before any promotion.

## P271  source-bound contradiction -> checked irreducible subset -> smaller conflict explanation with explicit minimality status

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P193 scoped contradictions; P197 independent engine checks; P212 change impact
new capability: Z3 assumption-based cores + deletion reduction + independent finite-domain subset oracle

```
approved scoped rule ASTs + immutable case configuration + explicit finite-domain contract
-> assert separately named rule constraints and frozen background assumptions (Lark AST adapter; z3.Solver.assert_and_track)
-> obtain a core only after an actual unsat result (z3.Solver.check; unsat_core)
-> remove one named rule at a time and retest under the same background (Z3 push/pop/check; deterministic deletion order)
-> retain any rule whose removal is satisfiable or undecided; record every test status (typed reduction log)
-> verify unsatisfiability of the result and satisfiability after each single removal (independent bounded enumeration or separately qualified solver)
-> render original rule sources, shared applicability, and the exact explanation guarantee (prov; Jinja2; P56)
```

contract: Irreducible means that removing any one member makes the reported subset satisfiable relative to the fixed background. It does not mean globally smallest cardinality. Background assumptions and domain bounds are part of the explanation. A timeout leaves reduction incomplete. Two different irreducible cores can be valid.

theoretical positive test: For the authored integer domain -1 through 6, minimum q>=4 and cap q<=3 remain a conflict while distractor q>=0 is removed. Each surviving rule is required for that subset. A second fixture deliberately returns an irreducible three-rule core while another two-rule core exists, preventing a smallest-core claim.

theoretical adverse test: Changing scope during reduction, hiding a background constraint, treating unknown as satisfiable, or labeling the first returned core globally minimum fails. A conflict that disappears only because the case inputs were changed is a different assessment.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for readable harmonization explanations that preserve the source and the exact conflict context.

public-data limit: Public norms and authored contradictions exercise explanation mechanics. No private requirement conflict or human resolution authority is verified. The local reference checks enumerate finite cases; Z3 core extraction itself was not run.

required implementation and evidence: Implement assumption labels and background snapshots, exercise real solver timeouts, compare the bounded core with exhaustive subsets, and obtain owner review before recording any policy resolution.

## P272  blocked requirement manifest -> authorized corrective actions -> least-cost repair proposal with controls protected

Primary family: F32.

reuses: P199 evidence gates; P221 valid planning; P223 qualified reviewers; P271 conflict explanation

```
-> emit a proposal for authorized review without changing evidence, policy, or business state (Jinja2; prov; P199)
```

contract: A repair can obtain evidence, correct a verified error, or assign eligible capacity. It cannot waive a mandatory control merely because that is cheaper. Costs, candidate actions, and authority are input data. An optimization result changes no source fact. Infeasible and not-yet-proven-feasible are separate outcomes.

theoretical positive test: In the authored fixture, address evidence costs 1 and an eligible review costs 3; acquiring both costs 4 and satisfies the unchanged requirements. A combined service costing 7 is dominated for that objective. A zero-cost waiver is excluded before optimization.

theoretical adverse test: Allowing the solver to rewrite identity, ownership, jurisdiction, evidence freshness, or mandatory approval fails. Missing cost does not become zero. An unqualified reviewer, negative cost, unavailable action, or invalid plan blocks the proposed repair.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for operational remediation suggestions with a protected policy boundary.

required implementation and evidence: Approve the action catalog and hard constraints, encode real effects and rollback limits, verify the proposal independently, and require separate authorization and observed completion for each selected action.

## P273  finite variation dimensions -> decision diagram -> counted and explainable valid configuration space

Primary family: F37.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P198 rule coverage; P200 resolver; P260 member commonality
new capability: dd.autoref.BDD + explicit finite-value encoding + Z3 and exhaustive-count parity

```
-> declare all represented variables and compile the valid-configuration predicate (dd.autoref.BDD.declare; add_expr)
-> restrict by known case values without inventing values for unknown dimensions (BDD.let; P200)

-> compare projected counts and satisfiable assignments against an independent representation (Z3; itertools.product; finite oracle)
-> persist variable order, encoding, source constraints, counts, and unresolved domains (BDD.dump with safe JSON; prov)
```

contract: A Boolean representation is only meaningful with its encoding and counted variable universe. Irrelevant but declared variables can multiply complete assignments. Projection removes dimensions deliberately. Variable ordering may change memory use; this proof does not promise polynomial size or superior performance. Untrusted pickle is excluded.

theoretical positive test: The authored constraint a implies b has six assignments over a,b,c and three assignments over the projection a,b. A bijective rename preserves the complete assignment set after decoding. Each invalid one-hot categorical combination is excluded.

theoretical adverse test: Dropping c while continuing to report complete configurations, counting unused bit patterns as categories, conflating absent values with false, or loading a diagram with a mismatched variable map fails. Resource exhaustion reports incomplete processing.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for compact variation resolution, coverage accounting, and change-impact enumeration on qualified finite domains.

required implementation and evidence: Pin the dd backend, define all encodings, compare every decoded model on small domains, benchmark representative large constraints, and retain a solver fallback with explicit resource limits.

## P275  unresolved request dimensions -> authorized question partitions -> next evidence request without invented answers

Primary family: F19.

```
-> accept a new answer only as a separately sourced and validated evidence event (Pydantic; P199; P211)
-> recompute the requirement manifest and retain unanswered or unverifiable states (P200; prov)
```

contract: Question selection is a proposal to gather evidence. It cannot choose the answer that makes a case pass. Unknown answers preserve the applicable uncertainty. theoretical positive test: For four authored feasible cases, a permitted question splitting them 2+2 beats one splitting them 1+3 under the declared minimax objective. A question with no verifiable answer leaves all four possible cases available. Equal scores use a recorded deterministic tie rule.

theoretical adverse test: A forbidden question is excluded even if it has the largest possible information gain. Missing priors block probability-weighted ranking. Defaulting an absent response to the most favorable class, or selecting questions from unauthorized data, fails.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for focused intake and follow-up requests that expose the reason for each requested item.

## P276  typed service dependencies and uncertain failure rates -> Boolean failure model -> scenario bounds with dependence explicit

Primary family: F16.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P207 typed interfaces; P229 stress tests; P257 readiness; P273 diagrams
new capability: SymPy Boolean formulas + Fraction scenario evaluation + SciPy linear-program bounds

```
source-bound service dependency types + declared failure formula + probability assumptions
-> distinguish all-required services, alternatives, and shared dependencies (Pydantic; NetworkX; explicit AND/OR semantics)
-> compile and simplify the Boolean failure event without duplicating shared causes (SymPy And/Or/simplify_logic; optional BDD)
-> evaluate joint worlds under an explicitly supplied dependence model (Fraction; itertools.product; pgmpy when qualified)
-> when only marginals are known, optimize event probability across feasible joint masses (scipy.optimize.linprog(method="highs"))
-> verify probability conservation, marginal constraints, feasibility, and numerical tolerance (NumPy; independent sums; solver status)
-> record scenario assumptions, lower/upper bounds, and missing failure-mode coverage (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Graph reachability alone does not define system failure. Independence is an assumption that must be declared. Known marginal failure rates generally leave a range for a compound event. Exact rational reference values and floating solver results keep separate precision descriptions. Unmodeled common causes remain a model limit.

theoretical positive test: For authored event probabilities A=0.1, B=0.2, C=0.05 and failure C OR (A AND B), independent joint enumeration gives 69/1000. With only those marginals, an eight-world linear program gives bounds [0.05,0.15]. The local SciPy result must satisfy all mass and marginal constraints.

theoretical adverse test: Adding failure probabilities as if events were disjoint, multiplying them without an independence assumption, double-counting a shared provider, or accepting negative/inconsistent joint masses fails. A solver failure leaves bounds unresolved.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for transparent resilience and capacity scenarios with explicit dependence uncertainty.

public-data limit: The published event logs and scheduling examples do not supply operational outage rates, causal failure independence, or complete dependency semantics. The bounded local calculation is an authored scenario, not an estimated production reliability figure.

required implementation and evidence: Approve the failure formula and coverage boundaries, obtain dated evidence for probabilities or label assumptions, validate solver tolerances, and compare scenarios against observed failures before any operational reliability claim.


contract: The chosen method needs its stated assumptions. Ordinary split-conformal calibration cannot simply treat censored durations as known final targets. theoretical positive test: For authored absolute residual scores 1 through 9 with alpha=0.1, the finite-sample rank is ceil(10*0.9)=9. A point estimate of 10 has radius 9 and interval [1,19]. A complete implementation must match that independent oracle for its declared simple score profile.

theoretical adverse test: Using the training residuals as calibration evidence, overlapping case IDs, silently dropping difficult unresolved outcomes, selecting an out-of-range quantile as finite, or reporting an interval as an SLA commitment fails. assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for uncertainty-aware completion estimates after target, sampling, and calibration qualification.

required implementation and evidence: Archive the event log and label recipe, qualify the censoring strategy, pin the MAPIE/base-model environment, run held-out coverage with subgroup diagnostics, and define drift-triggered abstention rather than automatic policy updates.

## P278  four-state evidence rules -> paired Boolean rails -> cross-engine semantic checks without unknown-value collapse

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P191 rule AST; P197 cross-engine parity; P199 evidence gates; P246 engine migration
new capability: SymPy paired-support algebra + Z3/clingo/clipspy adapters + complete small truth tables

```
approved rule AST + evidence pairs (support, refutation) + declared four-state operators
-> preserve neither=(0,0), supported=(1,0), refuted=(0,1), and both=(1,1) (Pydantic; explicit tagged values)
-> compile each supported operator into separate support and refutation expressions (Lark Transformer; SymPy Boolean symbols)
-> encode the same declared rails through engine-specific adapters (Z3 Booleans; clingo facts; clipspy templates)
-> enumerate all input states and compare the decoded result with an independent table (itertools.product; pytest; Hypothesis)
-> verify selected algebraic identities on both rails and retain counterexamples to invalid classical rewrites (SymPy simplify_logic; solver checks)
-> emit semantic-coverage and unsupported-operator records before engine substitution (prov; P246)
```

contract: For this declared algebra, negation swaps rails; conjunction uses (t1 AND t2, f1 OR f2); disjunction uses (t1 OR t2, f1 AND f2). Applicability and authorization remain separate dimensions. Classical simplification cannot be applied directly to a four-state proposition. The semantics must be approved before compilation.

theoretical positive test: All four unary negations and sixteen ordered binary input pairs match the independent truth table. Double negation and the declared De Morgan identities hold. For neither, p OR NOT p remains neither. Contradictory evidence remains both and is routed under the separate decision policy.

theoretical adverse test: Casting unknown to Python False, erasing refutation once support appears, rewriting every p OR NOT p to supported, or allowing an unsupported temporal/deontic operator through an unqualified adapter fails. Engine agreement without the independent table is insufficient.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for consistent missing and contradictory evidence across rule-engine boundaries.

public-data limit: Public library documentation supports Boolean manipulation and engine operations. It does not mandate this evidence logic. The local suite actually checks the finite algebra and SymPy identities; Z3, clingo, and clipspy adapters remain unexecuted.

required implementation and evidence: Approve the algebra and designated decision outcomes, qualify every supported AST operator, exhaust small truth tables per backend, and reject constructs whose temporal or exception semantics have not been specified.

## P280  review population and observed labels -> qualified sample assessment -> uncertainty-aware quality report

Primary family: F18.

reuses: P148 test linkage; P189 acceptance gates; P211 cutoff history; P240 benefits; P277 sampling limits
new capability: NumPy reproducible sampling + SciPy exact proportion intervals + explicit population and stratum coverage

```
-> freeze the population, strata, exclusions, and case identifiers before sampling (DuckDB; Pydantic; P211)
-> select reproducible cases under the approved design and retain inclusion probabilities where needed (numpy.random.Generator.choice; explicit seed)
-> bind each observed pass/error to the actual reviewed case, reviewer, criterion, and evidence version (P189; prov)
-> compute a method appropriate to the sampling design (scipy.stats.binomtest(...).proportion_ci for the declared binomial model; separately qualified finite-population method when needed)

```

contract: A sampling interval is conditional on its statistical model and sampling design. Repeated or clustered cases are not automatically independent Bernoulli trials. Sampling acceptance is separate from case-level control completion.

theoretical positive test: For an authored iid-binomial sample with zero errors in 50 reviews, the exact 95 percent two-sided interval has lower bound zero and upper bound approximately 0.071121736464. theoretical adverse test: An empty sample cannot produce a finite quality estimate. Excluding difficult cases after seeing their labels, treating missing review outcomes as passes, merging duplicate case reviews as independent trials, or claiming population-wide quality from an unreviewed stratum fails.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for transparent QA reporting and sample-design qualification. It supplies no automatic compliance or individual-case approval.

public-data limit: No full public case population or independently reviewed public labels were ingested. The exact interval is an authored mathematical test; it is not a private-program defect rate or assurance result.

required implementation and evidence: Approve the population and sampling design, obtain genuine reviewed labels and authority records, qualify the design-specific estimator, report missing strata, and keep the audit trail from selected case to reviewed evidence.
