## P283  event timestamps and clock uncertainty -> interval order constraints -> honest conformance and deadline outcomes

Primary family: F34.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P219 partial event order; P224 calendars; P231 time attribution; P218 temporal monitoring
new capability: intervaltree indexing + explicit closed-bound adapter + Z3 interval constraints

```
events with observed timestamps + clock-source identities + explicit uncertainty bounds + precedence requirements
-> normalize known time zones and retain the original timestamp precision (datetime; dateutil; P224)
-> derive a possible true-time interval only from supplied clock bounds (Pydantic; exact integer-time arithmetic)
-> index overlaps through a tested conversion to the library's half-open interval convention (intervaltree.IntervalTree; explicit endpoint contract)
-> assert only event orders that hold throughout the supplied bounds (Z3 inequalities; P219)
-> classify deadline satisfaction as certain, impossible, or unresolved under the interval and boundary policy (RTAMT adapter or exact interval oracle)
-> report nominal order separately from supported order, with clock gaps and source references (PM4Py input adapter; prov)
```

contract: Closed uncertainty bounds describe possible instants. Strict A-before-B is established when the upper bound of A is below the lower bound of B. An overlap leaves the order undecided unless other trusted evidence constrains it. A clock bound is an assumption or measurement with provenance, not a confidence interval unless explicitly justified. No uncertainty is invented when absent.

theoretical positive test: Authored intervals A=[98,102] and B=[101,105] leave their true order unresolved despite ordered nominal times. [0,1] is strictly before [2,3]. Completion [90,100] meets an inclusive deadline 100; [99,101] remains unresolved.

theoretical adverse test: Using ingest order or nominal timestamps to certify an ambiguous sequence fails. Closed [4,4] must not disappear when adapted to a half-open index. Reversed bounds, unsupported units, unknown zones, or missing clock evidence block the relevant comparison.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for event conformance that accounts for clock and precision limits.

public-data limit: Public event descriptions establish timestamp fields, not clock calibration or causal order. The local suite checks exact interval arithmetic only. intervaltree, RTAMT, Z3, and full PM4Py composition remain unexecuted.

required implementation and evidence: Obtain clock and precision contracts, implement endpoint conversion with overflow checks, compare interval results against an independent oracle, and preserve unresolved orders in every downstream conformance report.

## P284  identity adjudications and withdrawals -> reconstructed equivalence classes -> reversible merges and targeted rechecks

Primary family: F02.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P201 linkage candidates; P203 storage parity; P211 bitemporal history; P212 impact; P213 retractions
new capability: Versioned equivalence-edge ledger + NetworkX component reconstruction + provenance-based split impact

```
immutable source records + dated identity decisions + permitted equivalence semantics
-> separate candidate similarity, confirmed same-entity, and other relationship types (Splink adapter; Pydantic; P201)
-> select effective confirmed identity edges at the requested valid and recorded times (DuckDB; P211)
-> reconstruct equivalence classes from surviving edges without deleting source identities (NetworkX connected_components; stable membership serialization)
-> compare old and new classes after a correction or withdrawal (exact set differences; P213)
-> find derived cases that used affected membership decisions, including prior cluster members (prov relationship graph; P212)
-> emit a split/merge record and revalidation queue while retaining historical decisions (JSON; P220 replay)
```

contract: Only an approved same-entity relation participates in equivalence closure. Parent, trustee, nominee, shared address, and probable match are different relations. A union-find structure without a qualified deletion strategy cannot implement arbitrary identity withdrawals. Canonical display IDs must not erase immutable source IDs.

theoretical positive test: Authored confirmed edges A-B and B-C form {A,B,C}. Withdrawing A-B reconstructs {A} and {B,C}. Every source record remains present, and dependent results using the old merged identity are queued for recheck. The historical snapshot can still reproduce the earlier cluster.

theoretical adverse test: Merging a probable candidate automatically, treating parent_of as equivalence, silently keeping a removed union edge, or reassigning all historical records to a newly chosen identity fails. Unknown adjudication authority blocks the merge event.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for corrected entity reconciliation and cross-member case impact without destructive identity rewriting.

public-data limit: Public entities and relationship examples contain no private adjudication decisions or complete identity truth. The local suite tests graph reconstruction on authored records; it does not measure real-world linkage precision.

required implementation and evidence: Approve the equivalence policy and decision authority, retain atomic source records, implement dated withdrawals, test adversarial transitive merges, and revalidate every dependent case against the reconstructed membership.

## P285  controlled business question -> authorized query plan -> exact source-linked answer with coverage state

Primary family: F04.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P37 RDF queries; P38 graph queries; P123 search; P200 requirement manifest; P264 safe views
new capability: Lark question grammar + SQLGlot AST construction + parameter-bound read-only query adapters

```
controlled question + authenticated audience + source schema and snapshot coverage
-> parse only the declared question language into a typed query AST (Lark; Pydantic)
-> authorize requested records, fields, aggregates, and source references before compiling (Casbin; P264)
-> build SQL from allowlisted identifiers and bound literal parameters, with the target dialect explicit (SQLGlot expression objects; fixed adapter)
-> execute against a read-only snapshot with external functions and file/network access disabled (DuckDB or sqlite3; external isolation)
-> compare equivalent relational or RDF answers with declared duplicate/null semantics (RDFLib; independent Python oracle; P203)
-> return answer rows, contributors, cutoff, and incomplete-source status through fixed templates (Jinja2; prov)
```

contract: A parsed SQL AST does not establish validity, authorization, or safe execution. This proof accepts a bounded controlled question language. Identifiers are schema-bound and literals remain parameters. An empty result describes the available authorized snapshot; it does not establish that the real-world population is empty.

theoretical positive test: For authored tenant T1, asking for missing cases returns only C1 and its recorded status. The missing case C3 from another tenant is absent from rows, counts, and source links. The result agrees with the independent expected row set.

theoretical adverse test: A literal containing an SQL fragment remains a literal or is rejected by the question schema. Unknown identifiers, arbitrary functions, external data readers, multiple statements, unauthorized aggregates, or mixed snapshot cutoffs fail. Incomplete source coverage remains visible.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for repeatable business questions over the resolver and lifecycle with exact traceability.

public-data limit: Public schemas can support a mechanical query demonstration but do not define private vocabulary or entitlements. The local suite executes a fixed parameter-bound SQLite query and read-only check; SQLGlot, DuckDB, and RDF parity remain unexecuted.

required implementation and evidence: Approve the question grammar and mappings, qualify dialect/null/multiplicity behavior, enforce isolation outside the parser, and compare every supported question type with an independent source-level oracle.

## P286  shared lifecycle and member binding -> observable trace comparison -> refinement counterexamples and completion checks

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P216 safety; P217 liveness; P230 normative conformance; P260 commonality
new capability: automata-lib NFA/DFA conversion and language difference + separate deadlock/liveness checks

```
approved shared state machine + member state machine + observable-event mapping + scope binding
-> verify that each machine has a declared finite abstraction and label contract (Pydantic; NetworkX; P260)
-> map purely internal member steps to explicit epsilon transitions while preserving observable control events (automata-lib NFA construction; controlled adapter)
-> determinize and compare completed observable trace languages over the same alphabet (DFA.from_nfa; member.difference(shared); isempty)
-> recover a concrete violating trace from the difference machine when one exists (graph search; transition-to-source pointers)
-> independently check reachability, deadlock, divergence, and declared liveness requirements (pyModelChecking; NetworkX; P217)
-> issue a scoped refinement record naming the exact property and abstraction limits (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Completed-trace inclusion is one property. It is weaker than behavioral equivalence or bisimulation and can hold vacuously when no member run completes. Hiding a mandatory approval as an internal action is forbidden by the observable-event mapping. Finite abstraction needs a preservation argument before claims about unbounded data-aware processes.

theoretical positive test: An authored member adds an internal step between submit and review. After projection, its completed trace is submit-review-activate and is allowed by the shared machine. A separate completion check confirms that the terminal state is reachable.

theoretical adverse test: A member trace submit-activate produces a missing-review counterexample. A deadlocked member with no completed trace passes set inclusion vacuously but fails completion. An internal infinite loop needs divergence analysis; bounded enumeration cannot certify it away.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for checking that regional or divisional bindings retain shared lifecycle obligations.

public-data limit: The public examples are mechanical analogues. The local suite enumerates complete paths only for small authored acyclic graphs; automata-lib, weak-trace construction, and temporal model-checker integration were not run.

required implementation and evidence: Approve observable controls and abstraction, construct the automata adapters, test counterexamples and empty languages, then separately verify liveness/fairness on all relevant member states.

## P287  probabilistic workflow model -> reachability and reward properties -> conditional completion bounds

Primary family: F21.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P217 liveness; P225 simulation; P229 stress; P276 dependence bounds
new capability: stormpy probabilistic model checking + explicit stochastic model + independent linear-equation oracle

```
finite stochastic workflow + justified transition probabilities + terminal-state and time-unit definitions
-> validate probability rows, absorbing states, reward units, and model class (Pydantic; exact rational checks)
-> construct a supported PRISM or explicit model with source-bound parameters (stormpy.parse_prism_program; build_model)
-> parse the declared reachability, bounded-horizon, or reward property (stormpy.parse_properties_for_prism_program)
-> evaluate the property with recorded engine and numerical settings (stormpy.model_checking; explicit result extraction)
-> cross-check small chains using independent absorption equations and path sums (SciPy linear solve; Fraction; NetworkX SCCs)
-> compare scenario results with simulation only under the same assumptions and horizon (SimPy; P225; prov)
```

contract: DTMC steps are discrete transitions, not business days unless the model defines that relationship. A Markov assumption and stationary probabilities require justification. For an MDP, min/max results depend on the scheduler class and cannot silently become a single realized forecast. Nonabsorbing states and infinite expected times remain explicit.

theoretical positive test: In the authored chain, each step reaches ready with probability 1/2, retries with 1/4, and rejects with 1/4. Eventual ready probability is 2/3; expected time to either absorbing outcome is 4/3 steps; ready within two steps is 5/8. Independent Fraction equations and actual SciPy solves agree.

theoretical adverse test: Negative probabilities, rows that do not sum to one, mislabeled terminal states, reward units treated as days without a mapping, or a closed nonabsorbing component hidden from the output fails. A model probability must never be presented as an observed private completion rate.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for mathematically explicit completion and resilience scenarios, with empirical calibration as a separate requirement.

public-data limit: The public die model demonstrates model-checker mechanics. It says nothing about onboarding probabilities. The local suite solves a tiny authored chain; stormpy and a complete stochastic-process adapter were not executed.

required implementation and evidence: Archive the public model for tool qualification, implement the workflow abstraction and properties, justify probability estimates, test nonabsorption and scheduler extremes, and report assumptions beside every quantitative result.

## P288  independent rule annotations -> disagreement and reliability measures -> source-linked adjudication worklist

Primary family: F01.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P191 controlled records; P195 source spans; P271 explanations; P281 ambiguity
new capability: krippendorff.alpha + explicit annotation units and metrics + independent nominal-agreement oracle

```
frozen source spans + annotation guide + independent coder records + optional adjudicated reference
-> align exact source-unit identities and schema versions without treating omissions as labels (Pydantic; pandas joins)
-> preserve coder identity, independently entered actor/modality/scope values, and missing fields (source-bound annotation model)
-> compute disagreement under the declared nominal/ordinal/interval metric (krippendorff.alpha; explicit level_of_measurement)
-> compare a small nominal example with an independently specified coincidence calculation (Fraction; Counter; pytest)
-> produce disagreements and missing-coverage rows for authorized adjudication (Jinja2; P199)
-> retain the final adjudication separately from agreement statistics and original annotations (prov; P211)
```

contract: Agreement measures consistency among annotations under the selected unit and metric. They do not establish that the interpretation is correct. Coder overlap and missing annotations determine which units contribute. A degenerate zero expected-disagreement case must follow an explicit undefined-result policy. No score threshold is invented as automatic approval.

theoretical positive test: Two authored coders assign [0,0,1,1] identically, giving nominal alpha 1 under the independent reference calculation. An authored gold sequence [1,1,0,0] disagrees at every item. The worklist therefore keeps agreement and reference correctness separate.

theoretical adverse test: Filling missing values with the majority label, dropping difficult units after review, treating a sole annotation as inter-coder agreement, or converting a constant-label undefined denominator into perfect evidence fails. An incorrect source-span join invalidates the comparison.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for measuring upstream annotation consistency and organizing focused owner adjudication.

public-data limit: No actual independent annotators or approved gold interpretations were supplied. The local suite runs an authored nominal-alpha oracle, not the krippendorff package. It provides no measured NLP accuracy or policy signoff.

required implementation and evidence: Approve the annotation guide and metric, obtain genuine independent judgments, qualify missing-data behavior and library licensing, report unit coverage, and require a source-bound adjudication for semantic disagreements.

## P289  typed adapter contracts -> symbolic counterexample search -> replayable regression witnesses with explicit coverage

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P148 test linkage; P197 independent oracles; P267 mutations; P278 semantic contracts
new capability: icontract pre/postconditions + CrossHair symbolic execution + Hypothesis concrete replay

```
pure adapter function + approved preconditions/postconditions + declared data domain
-> express identity, conservation, type, null, and boundary invariants explicitly (icontract.require/ensure; snapshots where needed)
-> select actual functions with reachable contracts and record their source hashes (AST inventory; P148)
-> search for contract counterexamples under recorded per-path and per-condition budgets (crosshair check --analysis_kind=icontract --report_all)
-> replay every concrete counterexample through ordinary Python and the independent oracle (pytest; Hypothesis example database)
-> distinguish counterexample found, confirmed condition, incomplete search, unsupported operation, and tool error (typed result parser)
-> attach regression cases and trusted-code boundaries to the exact adapter version (prov; P267)
```

contract: No counterexample found within a budget is weaker than a general proof. An omitted precondition changes the property; an overly restrictive one can hide valid business inputs. Symbolic support for native libraries, I/O, side effects, and mutation must be declared. Do not permit arbitrary code side effects just to broaden analysis.

theoretical positive test: The authored cent splitter preserves nonnegative integer amounts for all 101 inputs from 0 through 100 and rejects Boolean or negative inputs. A mutant returning n//2 twice loses one cent at n=1; that concrete witness is replayable without a symbolic solver.

theoretical adverse test: Labeling an empty contract inventory successful, ignoring a symbolic timeout, dropping odd inputs from the domain, or reporting native/unsupported paths as verified fails. The returned witness must still fail when executed concretely against the identified code.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for finding adapter errors and strengthening regression evidence beyond hand-selected examples.

public-data limit: The local suite executes finite ordinary-Python cases only. CrossHair, Z3 symbolic paths, icontract decorators, and Hypothesis were not executed. The bounded arithmetic check is not a proof over all integers or all library behavior.

required implementation and evidence: Write reviewed contracts, pin compatible tooling, parse all result states, constrain effects with external isolation, and require concrete replay plus source-version linkage for each discovered defect.

## P290  verified derivation and dependency envelope -> guarded cache reuse -> fast replay without stale authority

Primary family: F32.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P17 hashes; P211 bitemporal state; P212 impact; P244 version fence; P262 canonical envelopes
new capability: DiskCache persistent bytes + content-addressed dependency keys + current authority and freshness rechecks

```
pure derivation inputs + schema/compiler/rule versions + case scope + evidence and authority versions
-> enumerate every declared dependency and effective/known-at snapshot used by the derivation (Pydantic; prov; P211)
-> construct a canonical content-addressed key including namespace and semantic profile (P262; hashlib.sha256)
-> store only validated bytes and a trusted dependency/integrity envelope (diskcache.Cache.set; explicit serialization policy)
-> retrieve through one wrapper that checks context equality, validity, integrity, and current audience permission (Cache.get; Casbin; P244)
-> recompute on miss, stale or changed dependency, failed write, or unverifiable provenance (versioned pure function; P212)
-> retain cache outcome and independently recheck authorization immediately before any side effect (Temporal activity gate; prov)
```

contract: Caching accelerates a derivation. It cannot grant authority or establish fresh evidence. TTL does not replace rule-effective dates or revocation checks. A complete key requires a reviewed dependency inventory; a hash cannot detect an omitted dependency. Untrusted pickle is excluded. Cache and FanoutCache write/transaction outcomes must not be assumed identical.

theoretical positive test: Changing any of eleven authored dependency fields changes the key, while reordering the same envelope fields does not. A valid entry is reused only before its declared validity boundary with current permission and the trusted expected payload hash.

theoretical adverse test: A changed tenant, entity, rule, evidence, compiler, or authority version misses the old key. At the exact expiration boundary the entry is stale. Permission revocation denies reuse. Changed payload bytes with an unchanged trusted hash produce corruption. A failed cache write cannot be reported as durable success.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for efficient deterministic replay that preserves evidence and authority boundaries.

public-data limit: The local suite checks a Python dictionary cache wrapper and key envelope. DiskCache, concurrent writers, process crashes, and complete dependency discovery were not executed or qualified. No speedup or private-program cache safety is claimed.

required implementation and evidence: Implement a reviewed dependency schema, use safe bytes-only serialization, test concurrent and failed writes for the selected backend, and compare cached versus uncached results across policy and permission changes.

## P291  cyclic economic interests -> exact simultaneous equations -> bounded participation and unresolved ownership blocks

Primary family: F02.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P202 relationship typing; P211 dated facts; P253 direct/component distinction and acyclic look-through; P282 exact quantities
new capability: SymPy rational matrix solving inside NetworkX strongly connected components, with independent Fraction residual checks and optional Z3 feasibility constraints

```
dated direct economic interests + explicit share-class contract + external-holder register
-> validate issuer/holder orientation, rational shares, source versions, and component identities (Pydantic; RDFLib; P253)
-> identify cyclic blocks without flattening them into repeated paths (networkx.strongly_connected_components; condensation)
-> define C[owner_company, issuer_company] and x = d + transpose(C)*x for the approved linear economic model (sympy.Matrix; Rational)
-> require a declared convergence certificate and nonsingular I-transpose(C), then solve each supported block (Matrix.det; LUsolve)
-> independently verify equations, bounds, and any claimed external-holder conservation (fractions.Fraction; optional Z3 constraints)
-> return source-linked economic participation or unresolved blocks, retaining all control and ownership declarations separately (prov; JSON)
```

contract: This extends P253 only for the stated linear economic-interest model. It never changes that proof's acyclic contract. Voting rights, effective control, beneficial-owner disclosure, treasury shares, and legal interpretation require separate approved models. Missing interests are not zero. A sufficient conservative qualification condition is a nonnegative C with every column sum below one. Blocks outside that condition require another justified certificate or remain unsupported. A numerical pseudoinverse must not manufacture a unique result for a singular block.

theoretical positive test: In the authored model, A owns half of B, B owns one quarter of A, and person P directly owns half of A. The equations give P economic participation of 4/7 in A and 2/7 in B. A separately declared external holder owns the remaining direct shares; the two computed holder vectors sum to one for each issuer.

theoretical adverse test: A closed pair with reciprocal 100-percent company holdings makes I-transpose(C) singular and stays unresolved. An unknown percentage, duplicate component, conflicting effective version, incompatible share class, or negative share blocks the affected result. An indirect summary must not be added to its components.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for exact cyclic economic calculations under an approved, complete linear model. It addresses a deliberately excluded case in P253 without treating algebra as a beneficial-owner determination.

public-data limit: The ownership graph and shares are authored. Public BODS examples establish representation mechanics only. No real circular ownership network, control conclusion, or institution-specific disclosure threshold is verified.

required implementation and evidence: Implement the pinned BODS adapter, approved economic convention, convergence gate, per-block source lineage, and independent conservation checks. Qualify incomplete and near-boundary blocks before extending the supported domain.

## P292  inherited permissions and prohibitions -> explicit conflict strategy -> policy-composition result with outstanding duties

Primary family: F05.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P191 controlled semantics; P193 scoped contradictions; P206 authorization; P260 commonality; P265 offline reference registry
new capability: Pinned ODRL vocabulary and optional OWL-RL action closure composed with RDFLib, pySHACL, and clingo policy evaluation

```
sealed policies + approved ODRL profile + action vocabulary + authenticated request context
-> load inherited policies from the sealed registry and reject missing parents or inheritance cycles in the supported profile (RDFLib; P265; NetworkX)
-> separate asserted action relationships from allowed entailed relationships (owlrl.DeductiveClosure; OWLRL_Semantics; graph difference)
-> expand compact rules and validate target, party, profile, duty, and conflict properties (pyshacl.validate; explicit ODRL adapter)
-> find scoped permission/prohibition conflicts, including encompassing actions (clingo; P193; typed action closure)
-> apply the declared profile conflict strategy and keep policy validity separate from duty fulfillment (versioned rule evaluator; P199)
-> recheck actual actor authority before recording an executable authorization (Casbin; P206; prov)
```

contract: ODRL expresses a policy chosen by an authorized owner. It cannot determine which private policy is legally controlling. Unknown profiles or unsupported actions remain unsupported. Entailed action inclusion never creates a new person's identity or delegated authority. Conflict strategy is distinct from rule applicability, policy validity, duty fulfillment, and execution permission.

theoretical positive test: A same-target permission to use and prohibition to print are conflicting under the declared print-includedIn-use relation. The public strategies yield permission precedence, prohibition precedence, or an invalid policy respectively. Permission precedence still leaves an unfulfilled duty outstanding.

theoretical adverse test: With no explicit strategy, a detected conflict invalidates the policy. Different inherited conflict values with conflicting rules cannot be resolved by picking the first record. Missing parent policies, ambiguous scope, unsupported vocabulary, or absent actor authentication cannot produce authorization.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for an interoperable, profile-bounded policy-composition layer. It makes precedence an explicit policy input instead of an engine accident.

public-data limit: The public standard does not grant private data-sharing rights. The bounded test uses RDFLib, NetworkX, and a small strategy reducer. OWL-RL, pySHACL, clingo, and Casbin composition was not executed.

required implementation and evidence: Archive the precise ontology and profile, qualify inherited/entailed rule expansion, test all conflict strategies and outstanding duties, and bind profile selection to a recorded authority decision. Compare full expansion with independent small policy tables.

## P293  version-bound record corrections -> staged JSON Patch and guarded commit -> atomic change with exact field lineage

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P126 validate-before-change pattern; P211 history; P244 stale-state fence; P245 schema migration; P262 canonical envelopes
new capability: python-json-patch and jsonpointer operations composed with Pydantic/jsonschema validation and a transactional version comparison

```
current record version + authorized ordered patch + exact schema and path policy
-> validate patch structure, operation support, source identity, and field permissions (Pydantic; jsonschema; Casbin)
-> decode JSON Pointer escapes and bind array positions to the correct pre-operation state (jsonpointer.JsonPointer; resolve_pointer)
-> apply test and mutation operations to a private copy, preserving their order (jsonpatch.JsonPatch.apply with in_place=False)
-> validate the entire candidate and compute affected requirement/source paths (jsonschema; P212; typed dependency lookup)
-> commit only if the stored version still equals the expected version, within one transaction (sqlite3 or qualified provider conditional update; P244)
-> retain the old version, patch, candidate, commit receipt, and recheck triggers (prov; hashlib; JSON)
```

contract: An operation sequence must not leak partial changes after a failing test or schema check. Array insertion shifts later positions; pointer strings must not be treated as immutable business identities. A patch cannot edit an approval or policy field without separate permission. JSON numbers and booleans have distinct comparison semantics. Provider transaction behavior requires its own qualification.

theoretical positive test: The two public examples produce the specified object member and array insertion. A permitted address correction commits once against version 1. A second version-1 writer is stale. Escaped pointer tokens identify slash and tilde characters in keys correctly.

theoretical adverse test: A successful first mutation followed by a failed version test leaves the original unchanged. A forbidden path, malformed index, missing target, unsupported operation, or candidate failing its business schema aborts the patch. Boolean true cannot satisfy a numeric test for one.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for controlled client maintenance and post-review corrections with precise invalidation. Atomic update is an integration obligation in addition to patch syntax.

public-data limit: The reference test executes a deliberately restricted add/replace/remove/test adapter with jsonpointer and SQLite. The python-json-patch package, all RFC operations, concurrent processes, and remote commits were not exercised.

required implementation and evidence: Qualify the chosen full patch engine, numeric equality, root and nested paths, array behavior, whole-record schemas, permission scope, rollback, and concurrent version fences. Unsupported local reference operations remain explicitly unsupported.

## P294  paginated source responses -> stable-snapshot and count reconciliation -> qualified evidence population

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P202 graph coverage; P210 source completeness; P211 snapshots; P242 duplicate identity; P266 API qualification
new capability: HTTPX transport-controlled pagination tests and strict page envelopes feeding a snapshot-qualified collection adapter

```
authorized collection query + page contract + source snapshot guarantee or explicit absence
-> fix tenant, filters, sort keys, page size, source version, and acquisition bounds (Pydantic; canonical query envelope)
-> retrieve each page through a bounded adapter, preserving request/response provenance (httpx.Client; response JSON; retry policy from P295)
-> validate page indices, counts, record IDs, duplicate payloads, and continuation progress (Pydantic; pandas joins; P210)
-> reconcile changing totals, missing pages, repeated cursors, and version tokens (typed collection reducer; HTTPX MockTransport tests)
-> require a qualified provider snapshot or equivalent export guarantee before asserting snapshot completeness (explicit assurance gate; P199)
-> seal accepted records together with acquisition limits and unresolved coverage (prov; hashlib; JSON)
```

contract: Matching row counts cannot establish a point-in-time population when records can change between pages. A repeated total or client-created token does not supply a provider snapshot. A short page is interpreted under the provider contract. A missing or forbidden page remains missing, rather than an empty population. Retries preserve all conflicting observations.

theoretical positive test: Under an explicitly authored provider snapshot guarantee, two pages starting at 1 and 3 contain a, b, and c, with total three and one matching snapshot token. They form a complete collection under that declared contract. Removing the guarantee yields coverage_unproven even with the same rows.

theoretical adverse test: Missing the last page gives incomplete coverage. A duplicate ID, altered total, nonadvancing continuation, or wrong page index is inconsistent. A changed snapshot token blocks complete-snapshot status. Two records can change between pages while the total stays constant; total equality alone must not pass.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for defensible acquisition and completeness accounting. The distinction directly protects ownership coverage, review populations, and conformance denominators.

public-data limit: The test executes HTTPX MockTransport and strict Pydantic page parsing with no network traffic. No live SCIM provider, full public registry, or provider snapshot guarantee is qualified.

required implementation and evidence: Specify provider continuation semantics, enforce bounded acquisition, archive every page and exact query, test mutations between pages, and obtain evidence for any claimed snapshot guarantee. Preserve coverage limits downstream.

## P295  provider rate limits and transient failures -> bounded retry schedule -> evidence acquisition without false absence

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P199 unknown gates; P218 time horizons; P241 durable timers; P242 retry reconciliation; P294 collection coverage
new capability: Tenacity retry/stop/wait policies with HTTPX response classification and explicit provider Retry-After parsing

```
approved evidence request + method/effect contract + attempt and elapsed budgets
-> classify transport failure, rate limit, transient unavailability, authentication failure, and source absence separately (HTTPX; typed status adapter)
-> parse supported server waits without shortening them and retain clock uncertainty (email.utils.parsedate_to_datetime; datetime; explicit rounding)
-> combine the server lower bound with reviewed backoff and total-budget limits (tenacity.Retrying; retry and stop policies; custom wait adapter)
-> persist a deferred request when waiting would exceed the present execution budget (Temporal timer/state; P241)
-> reconcile uncertain outcomes before retrying any side-effecting operation (P242; provider receipts)
-> emit retrieved bytes, deferred acquisition, or a terminal error with coverage status (prov; P294)
```

contract: Rate limits and failed authentication must never become evidence that a document or person does not exist. The qualified read-only profile can retry declared retrieval methods; side-effecting methods need separate idempotency and reconciliation evidence. Retry limits are enforced across restarts. Circuit breaking is an additional proposed runtime policy, not a property of a successful request.

theoretical positive test: The public value 120 means a 120-second wait. At the authored 23:57:59 UTC clock, the public HTTP-date example also yields 120. An authored 503 followed by 200 succeeds after the modeled wait, while received bytes still require content validation.

theoretical adverse test: A 120-second wait with only 100 seconds left defers the request. Authentication failure ends the local retry path. A non-idempotent POST with an uncertain outcome is not retried by the read-only adapter. Invalid waits, infinite zero-delay retries, or lost cumulative budgets cannot silently pass.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for bounded, auditable source acquisition. It improves acquisition reliability without turning availability into truth or permission.

public-data limit: The reference uses a finite response reducer and clock arithmetic. It makes no network requests or real sleeps. Tenacity, distributed budgets, durable timers, and live provider behavior were not executed.

required implementation and evidence: Pin the response taxonomy, server-wait policy, monotonic time mapping, exception classes, backoff, circuit/reset rules, and persistent budget state. Test restarts and uncertain remote outcomes in an authorized integration environment.

## P296  copied and independently attributed evidence -> origin-aware support groups -> corroboration without counting mirrors as new sources

Primary family: F31.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P17 integrity hashes; P201 linkage limits; P213 sufficient supports; P247 attribution checks; P284 reversible identity
new capability: PROV derivation/attribution records composed with typed NetworkX origin traversal and pySHACL lineage-completeness checks

```
evidence records + declared derivation links + issuer/source attestations + corroboration policy
-> distinguish document identity, byte identity, underlying observation, and publisher identity (Pydantic; hashlib; P201)
-> retain asserted derivation and attribution with versioned source references (RDFLib PROV vocabulary; prov.ProvDocument)
-> validate endpoints, provenance gaps, and the declared acyclic transformation subset (pySHACL; NetworkX)
-> trace each evidence item to known origin groups without inventing absent links (networkx.descendants; typed origin adapter)
-> evaluate explicit sufficient-support groups after collapsing documented mirrors (clingo; P213; independent set oracle)
-> return support, contradicted support, shared-origin explanations, and unresolved origin assumptions (prov; Jinja2)
```

contract: Three URLs may reproduce one observation. Different URLs, file hashes, or company names do not establish independent evidence. Known distinct origin groups also do not establish statistical independence or truth. The acceptance rule must identify what kind of independence it requires and how that property is evidenced. Ambiguous source linkage stays unresolved.

theoretical positive test: An original record and two documented mirrors produce one known origin group. A separately attested source record produces a second group while retaining all four document identities. Withdrawing the original invalidates its dependent support under the relevant source policy.

theoretical adverse test: A scraper cache cannot count as an additional corroborating source merely because it has another filename. An unknown derivation root cannot become a confirmed independent origin. A cyclic transformation graph fails this adapter's declared DAG contract. A contradictory direct record stays visible after deduplication.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for source-sensitive evidence evaluation. It complements P213, which tracks sufficient support, by preventing copied support from being mistaken for new observations.

public-data limit: The bounded test executes RDFLib and NetworkX on authored provenance. It does not authenticate publishers, establish source independence, or measure evidence reliability. The prov package, SHACL layer, and policy engine remain unexecuted.

required implementation and evidence: Define origin granularity and attestations, qualify derivation adapters, test missing/cyclic links and retractions, and require independent review of any substantive corroboration rule. Keep observation identity separate from document identity.

## P297  derived query fields and selection predicates -> typed column lineage -> complete change-impact candidates for reports and gates

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P95 schemas; P212 change impact; P285 controlled query plans; P290 dependency-key completeness
new capability: SQLGlot column-lineage analysis plus explicit predicate, join, ordering, and authorization dependencies

```
approved SQL expression + resolved schema + source-query versions + authorization context
-> parse only the admitted read-only dialect and resolve table aliases and column identities (sqlglot.parse_one; optimizer scope; P285)
-> construct output-expression dependencies (sqlglot.lineage.lineage with schema and sources)
-> separately inventory WHERE, JOIN, GROUP BY, HAVING, ORDER BY/LIMIT, and policy-filter effects (explicit AST visitors; reviewed dependency classification)
-> build value, membership, ordering, schema, and authority edges (NetworkX; RDFLib; Pydantic)
-> identify affected outputs and cache entries for each changed input field (networkx.descendants; P212; P290)
-> compare incremental invalidation with full query recomputation on bounded mutations (DuckDB or SQLite; pytest; prov)
```

contract: A field can affect whether a row exists without appearing in its output expression. Value-only lineage is insufficient for report completeness or cached authorization. SELECT star requires the exact schema version. Dynamic SQL, opaque functions, unresolved identifiers, and unsupported dialect features block a complete-lineage claim. Conservative extra dependencies are recorded separately from proven necessary dependencies.

theoretical positive test: A query computes gross minus credit as net for region A. The authored row initially returns net 80. Changing only region to B removes the row while its arithmetic value stays 80. Region must therefore invalidate the reported result even though it is absent from the arithmetic expression.

theoretical adverse test: A projection-only map misses that change. An untracked join filter, renamed column, changed authorization predicate, or changed ordering before LIMIT must prevent reuse of a claimed complete dependency key. Parsing an unresolved query cannot certify lineage.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for finer-grained impact analysis and guarded recomputation. It extends case-level provenance to data-transformation semantics.

public-data limit: The local reference executes an authored dependency graph and actual SQLite queries. SQLGlot extraction, arbitrary SQL, real warehouse schemas, and optimizer equivalence were not tested.

required implementation and evidence: Qualify the supported SQL subset, pair column lineage with predicate visitors, include schema and access-policy versions, and compare dependency-triggered reruns with complete recomputation across controlled mutations.

## P298  operational aggregates and protected-unit limits -> reviewed privacy mechanism and budget reservation -> controlled statistical release

Primary family: F06.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P231 interval measures; P240 contributor conservation; P242 idempotent ledger; P264 nonleaking views; P279 declared loss tradeoffs
new capability: OpenDP measurement/composition objects with explicit contribution bounding and a transactional privacy-budget ledger

```
approved statistical question + protected-unit definition + contribution limits + release policy
-> separate persons/cases from events and bind the adjacency relation and cohort (Pydantic; exact contributor identities; P231)
-> apply a reviewed contribution-bounding transformation with a known stability contract (OpenDP transformation API; independent bounded-neighbor tests)
-> construct a supported measurement and inspect its privacy map against the chosen input distance (OpenDP measurement API; Measurement.map/check)
-> compose compatible measurements under the approved accounting model (opendp.combinators.make_basic_composition or separately qualified adaptive composition)
-> reserve the full privacy cost atomically before any new release can escape (sqlite3 transaction; immutable release IDs; P242)
-> publish only approved outputs and record measurement, ledger, and validity evidence without exposing raw protected data (prov; output allowlist; P264)
```

contract: Event-level limits do not automatically establish person-level privacy. Protected-unit linkage, contribution bounds, adjacency, domains, metrics, randomness, epsilon, and delta must be reviewed together. Basic sequential accounting sums compatible costs. Parallel composition requires demonstrated disjoint protected units. Repeated fresh noise draws are new releases. A private seed or raw count must not leak through test logs or metadata. A budget ledger alone proves no privacy guarantee.

theoretical positive test: Under an authored person-level model, events 7, 8, and 9 for one person are aggregated and clipped to ten; another person contributes four. Removing the first person's contribution changes the bounded sum by ten. Two approved pure-DP costs of 2/5 and 3/5 exhaust a budget of one.

theoretical adverse test: Clipping each event separately still allows the first person to contribute 24. A further release costing 1/1000 is rejected after the two reservations. A changed immutable reservation, negative budget, changed cohort, incompatible metric, or unaccounted fresh draw cannot pass.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for an optional statistical-release layer after full privacy and utility qualification. This can support program measurement while keeping case-level execution evidence under separate access controls.

public-data limit: Only exact contribution and budget accounting runs locally. OpenDP mechanisms, secure randomness, empirical utility, adaptive composition, and privacy guarantees were not executed or established. This proof makes no legal anonymization determination.

required implementation and evidence: Build and independently review the complete measurement pipeline, protected-unit mapping, ledger transaction, crash/retry behavior, output channel, and randomness handling. Validate utility separately from privacy. Preserve failed reservations and consumed budgets honestly.

## P299  locale-tagged numeric evidence -> exact parsing and semantic typing -> source-preserving international input

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P191 supported syntax; P195 source-offset preservation; P282 units and meaning; P288 annotation disagreement
new capability: Babel locale/numbering-system parsing composed with Decimal, Lark field grammars, Pydantic types, and explicit strictness policy

```
source span + explicit locale/numbering system + field type + approved parsing profile
-> retain original characters and normalization offsets without guessing locale from a person's identity (P195; Pydantic)
-> restrict the admitted lexical form and field semantics (Lark; explicit amount/count/percentage grammar)
-> parse using the declared locale, numbering system, and strictness (babel.numbers.parse_decimal)
-> reject nonfinite values and enforce currency/unit/range/precision independently (decimal.Decimal; Pydantic; P282)
-> format only for display with an explicit quantization policy, then verify the intended roundtrip (Babel format_decimal; Decimal comparison)
-> return exact value, source span, parser profile, and unresolved-locale or unsupported-form state (prov; JSON)
```

contract: Locale parsing establishes a numeric interpretation under a chosen convention. It does not validate a translation, issuer, currency, field meaning, or document authenticity. The same characters can have different values under different locales. The machine's default locale is never a silent input. Lenient and strict parsing are separate supported modes; changing one is a profile change.

theoretical positive test: The documented strings 1,099.98, 1.099,98, 12 345,123, and the documented Arabic-separator example yield their stated Decimal values under the published default parsing mode. The authored value 1,234 means 1234 with en_US and 1.234 with de_DE. Without a locale, the adapter returns unresolved.

theoretical adverse test: A German-formatted number under strict en_US parsing is rejected. NaN cannot become a valid amount. In the tested Babel environment, the published Russian example with an ordinary space is accepted leniently and rejected strictly; a separately labeled nonbreaking-space variant passes strict parsing. Rounding a high-precision source for display cannot overwrite the exact stored value.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for deterministic international form and evidence ingestion. The strict/lenient distinction is an observed boundary that must be part of the adapter contract.

public-data limit: Babel numeric functions actually run on the cited examples. General multilingual document extraction, translation fidelity, date interpretation, and private form coverage remain untested. The example projection is not a complete locale conformance suite.

required implementation and evidence: Pin Babel and its locale data; version the admitted formats and strictness; qualify missing locale, alternative spaces, numbering systems, nonfinite inputs, excess precision, and exact/display roundtrips. Keep unsupported natural-language amounts outside the accepted grammar.

## P300  interrupted source downloads -> version-bound byte-range reconciliation -> complete evidence bytes or explicit incomplete acquisition

Primary family: F03.

**theoretical requirement; full chain not executed**

reuses: P17 integrity; P210 coverage conservation; P247 trusted attribution; P265 sealed registry; P269 checkpoint restore; P294 acquisition coverage
new capability: fsspec storage adapters with HTTPX raw response handling, explicit Content-Range validation, and whole-object integrity gates

```
permitted source object + expected version/length/hash contract + retained partial chunks
-> bind all requests to one selected representation and byte-coordinate convention (HTTPX headers; explicit content-encoding policy)
-> validate 206 status, Content-Range, payload length, total length, and matching strong version validators (typed range parser; Pydantic)
-> reconcile chunk coverage, duplicates, and conflicting overlap without concatenating by arrival order (interval accounting; hashlib; P210)
-> stage only validated bytes through the selected storage backend (fsspec filesystem/cat_file/pipe_file; backend-specific adapter)
-> require complete byte coverage and an independently trusted whole-object digest before final publication (hashlib.sha256; explicit integrity gate)
-> publish atomically only on a backend whose commit behavior is qualified, retaining source and acquisition evidence (local os.replace or qualified remote commit; prov)
```

contract: Public byte-range endpoints are inclusive; internal half-open intervals need an explicit conversion. Encoded and decoded byte offsets must not be mixed. A 200 response to a range request requires a separately validated full-representation path. Unknown total length, inconsistent versions, or conflicting bytes block this profile's completion claim. An ETag is a representation validator, not a cryptographic identity assertion. A hash is trusted only through an independent expected-value chain.

theoretical positive test: The public known-length header maps to internal interval [42,1234), length 1192, total 1234. Two authored chunks [0,4) and [4,10) reconstruct exactly 0123456789 in either arrival order. fsspec's memory backend reads back the same ten bytes after verification.

theoretical adverse test: A missing chunk, changed ETag, invalid range, wrong length, conflicting overlap, or wrong full-object hash prevents completion. The public unknown-total header remains unresolved for complete coverage. A full 200 response must not be appended to an existing partial file. A weak version validator is outside this selected profile.

assessment: CONDITIONAL_FIT for resumable evidence acquisition and archive recovery. This proves a separate byte-integrity boundary before semantic parsing or evidence use.

public-data limit: The bounded test executes header parsing, HTTPX response objects, hashes, and fsspec memory storage with no network traffic. Live server behavior, HTTP backend range reads, large files, disk crashes, durable atomic publication, and source authenticity remain unqualified.

required implementation and evidence: Define raw versus decoded byte handling, backend commit guarantees, maximum object/chunk sizes, empty-object behavior, resume manifests, trusted digest acquisition, and source permission. Test complete/missing/mixed-version ranges and forced interruption before promotion.

### Bounded continuation: JSON-e affine configurator and product

This is a later stateless SCENARIO profile. It does not discharge the original browser/UI profile above. Its source schema, factory and constructor are the actual accepted template objects below. source is a varying validated model; answers selects gain and offset; runtime_input supplies x. Output equals gain * x + offset within the declared integer bounds.

Recorded pins: Python 3.12.14; json-e 4.8.4; jsonschema 4.23.0; fastjsonschema 2.21.2; toolz 1.1.0; numpy 2.3.5; hypothesis 6.131.9; hypothesis-jsonschema 0.23.1; pytest 8.3.5.

```text
source_schema + source + factory + constructor
-> round-trip source (json.dumps with allow_nan=False; json.loads)
-> validate source (jsonschema.Draft7Validator(source_schema).validate)
-> generate configurator (jsone.render(factory, {source, constructor_asset: constructor}))
-> serialize/reload configurator (json.dumps; json.loads)
-> select its configuration_schema, runtime_schema and constructor (operator.itemgetter)
-> validate serialized/reloaded answers against its generated configuration_schema (jsonschema.Draft7Validator.validate)
-> generate product using its constructor and {source, answers, runtime_schema} (jsone.render)
-> serialize/reload product (json.dumps; json.loads)
-> validate serialized/reloaded runtime_input against product.runtime_schema (jsonschema.Draft7Validator.validate)
-> execute product.program with runtime_input (jsone.render)
-> compare with separate exact arithmetic (decimal.Decimal; operator.mul; operator.add; numpy.testing.assert_string_equal after str conversion)
```

These braces describe argument bindings. They are not a new callable or an executable host. Use existing composition/data-selection primitives. Source and answer correspondence use canonical JSON comparisons; independent configuration validation uses fastjsonschema.compile with use_default=False and use_formats=False. Generated invalid answers must be rejected by both validators. The actual template assets are:

```json
{"source_schema":{"$schema":"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#","title":"Finite affine-family source model","type":"object","additionalProperties":false,"required":["basis","source_id","revision","gains","offset_limit","input_limit"],"properties":{"basis":{"const":"SCENARIO"},"source_id":{"type":"string","minLength":1,"maxLength":12},"revision":{"type":"integer","minimum":0,"maximum":100},"gains":{"type":"array","minItems":1,"maxItems":5,"uniqueItems":true,"items":{"type":"integer","minimum":-5,"maximum":5}},"offset_limit":{"type":"integer","minimum":0,"maximum":100},"input_limit":{"type":"integer","minimum":1,"maximum":1000}}},"factory":{"artifact_type":"AffineConfigurator","artifact_version":1,"source":{"$eval":"source"},"configuration_schema":{"$$schema":"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#","title":"Choose the affine product parameters","type":"object","additionalProperties":false,"required":["gain","offset"],"properties":{"gain":{"type":"integer","enum":{"$eval":"source.gains"}},"offset":{"type":"integer","minimum":{"$eval":"-(source.offset_limit)"},"maximum":{"$eval":"source.offset_limit"}}}},"runtime_schema":{"$$schema":"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#","title":"Input to the configured affine product","type":"object","additionalProperties":false,"required":["x"],"properties":{"x":{"type":"integer","minimum":{"$eval":"-(source.input_limit)"},"maximum":{"$eval":"source.input_limit"}}}},"constructor":{"$eval":"constructor_asset"}},"constructor":{"artifact_type":"ExecutableAffineProduct","artifact_version":1,"source":{"$eval":"source"},"answers":{"$eval":"answers"},"runtime_schema":{"$eval":"runtime_schema"},"program":{"$eval":"{'$eval': '(' + str(answers.gain) + ') * x + (' + str(answers.offset) + ')'}"}}}
```

The Python-produced bounded program also has a recorded execution through the v0.3.1 Go JSON-e CLI in their common subset. General cross-language equivalence, automatic template discovery, interfaces, stateful execution and external effects remain outside this profile.

    Stage contract: Use admitted Hypothesis strategies; hypothesis-jsonschema only for its supported dialect intersection.

    Stage contract: Ghostwriter style="unittest" emits published TestCase.assertEqual comparisons for compatible call signatures; generated TODO/st.nothing blocks admission.
  P344.S3: GeneratedGuardModule -> StandardRunnerEvidence
    Functions: L50.run pytest.main
    Stage contract: Run generated modules under pytest; retain collected counts, failures, skips, health checks, timeouts and reproducible minimized counterexamples.
Independent oracle/invariant: Independent library results or mathematically justified output invariants supply expectations. Ghostwriter fuzz/no-crash and self-roundtrip do not establish arbitrary semantic equivalence.

Remaining work:
  - Generic cross-language/stateful/HG04 test assembly from contract schemas still needs a qualified public generator/binding.
  - No hand-authored test body, expected fixture, comparison helper or bespoke runner closes that gap.

### Bounded continuation: native assertion binding

The attempted Ghostwriter JSON roundtrip emitted st.nothing() and could not generate an input. A later proposed alternative binds Hypothesis directly to published NumPy assertions. It preserves the original Ghostwriter-specific S2 as a separate open profile.

```text
accepted Draft 7 schema
-> generated values (hypothesis_jsonschema.from_schema)
-> correlated source/answer/input values (hypothesis.strategies.shared)
-> actual library compositions (hypothesis.strategies.builds; toolz.compose; toolz.do; operator.itemgetter; functools.partial)
-> schema-dependent generated choices (SearchStrategy.flatmap with from_schema)
-> independent expected values or justified invariants
-> public assertion binding (hypothesis.given(actual=..., desired=...) (numpy.testing.assert_string_equal))
-> standard pytest collection and execution
```

Strategies and data wiring in this bounded implementation were authored. The bindings contain no authored function/class/lambda/assert bodies. Generic automatic generation of the wiring, stateful histories and arbitrary whole-chain oracles remain open. Use the P305 continuation’s recorded pins for this profile.

    Functions: L07.check z3.Solver.check; L08.check cvc5.Solver.checkSat

Remaining work:
  - The authors' Similix5.0 research documents self-application and compiler-generator construction. End of framework specification. All 349 primary proof definitions are present.
